# Clarifications — Dutch Oven Daddy iOS App v1

**Status:** Phase 2 — Clarify, in progress
**Governs:** [`spec.md`](spec.md) — update affected ACs when these resolve.

## Method

Resolved questions are dated and traceable. Each resolution lists which acceptance criteria it touches so the spec stays in sync.

---

## Resolved by API research (2026-05-23)

### CL-1 — WPRM recipe data is NOT available as structured JSON via REST
**Question:** Does the WP REST API expose WPRM recipe fields (ingredients, steps, video, times) as structured data?

**Finding:**
- The default `wp/v2/posts` endpoint returns recipe content only as HTML embedded inside `content.rendered`.
- The `wp-recipe-maker/v1` namespace exists but has no public route returning structured recipe JSON. `embed/{id}` returns rendered HTML; `manage/*` requires admin auth.
- The `yoast_head_json.schema` on the API response does **not** include `@type: Recipe`.
- **However:** the rendered post HTML page contains a `<script type="application/ld+json">` block with a full `@type: Recipe` object including `recipeIngredient`, `recipeInstructions`, `prepTime`, `cookTime`, `totalTime`, `recipeYield`, `image`, and `nutrition`. This is highly structured and standardized.

**Resolution:** **Hybrid fetch strategy.**
1. Recipe **list** screens (home feed, category, search results, related, saved) use the WP REST API. Cheap, paginated, JSON.
2. Recipe **detail** screens fetch the rendered HTML page (`post.link`) and parse the JSON-LD `Recipe` block to get structured ingredients/instructions/times/nutrition.
3. The JSON-LD parse is the single source of truth for recipe body content. WPRM HTML scraping is **not** allowed as a primary path (constitution §4 already bans HTML scraping as primary).

**Acceptance criteria impact:**
- **AC-4.1 / AC-4.3 / AC-4.6:** times, ingredients, instructions sourced from JSON-LD.
- New **AC-4.11** to add: recipes without a parseable `Recipe` JSON-LD block must not appear in feed/search/category lists; they're filtered server-side... wait, the WP API can't filter on this. Filtered client-side after detail-fetch. See CL-9 below.

### CL-2 — Pagination
**Question:** What's the WP REST API per-page cap?
**Resolution:** WordPress default max is `per_page=100`. We use **`per_page=20`** for all list fetches (matches AC-1.2 batch size). Server may return fewer when fewer remain.

### CL-3 — Categories vs Tags
**Question:** Does "category" mean WP categories, tags, or both?
**Resolution:** **WP categories only** in v1. Sample post showed 2 categories, 0 tags. Tags are not populated on this site. Revisit if blog adds tag usage post-launch.

**Acceptance criteria impact:** AC-2.1 — "all WordPress categories" stays as written.

### CL-4 — Canonical recipe URL
**Question:** What URL goes in the share sheet?
**Resolution:** Use `post.link` verbatim. Confirmed hostname is `www.dutchovendaddy.com` (not bare `dutchovendaddy.com`). Don't rewrite or normalize.

**Acceptance criteria impact:** AC-6.2 — share payload is exactly `post.link`.

---

## Resolved by spec-default re-affirmation

### CL-5 — iCloud sync for saved recipes
**Question:** Should "Save" require iCloud opt-in immediately, or stay device-local in v1?
**Resolution:** **Device-local SwiftData in v1.** iCloud / CloudKit sync is explicitly deferred to v2 (already in spec out-of-scope). AC-5.7 stands; reinstall wipes saves and that's documented as a known limitation.

### CL-6 — Hero image resolution for iPad
**Question:** Minimum image size for iPad hero?
**Resolution:** Use the WP `media_details.sizes` map and pick the largest available size up to `2048px` on the long edge for iPad detail hero. Fall back: `full` size if no `2048x2048` derivative exists. Lists use `medium_large` (~768px). Don't request `full` for lists — wasteful.

---

## Resolved by user decision (2026-05-23)

### CL-7 — First-launch onboarding
**Question:** Onboarding screen on first launch?
**Resolution:** **No onboarding.** App opens directly to the home feed. Recipe-app intent is obvious from the first frame; faster time-to-value. No US-7 added.

### CL-8 — App icon and color palette
**Question:** App icon and color palette decided?
**Resolution:** **Use the blog's existing branding.** Owner will provide the logo / icon asset. The DesignSystem palette (warm earth tones — to be sampled from dutchovendaddy.com hero imagery and navigation) is defined as a Phase 3 (Plan) task. Spec stays branding-agnostic; design tokens live in `plan.md`.

### CL-9 — Posts with no parseable Recipe JSON-LD
**Question:** What does the app do for a post missing JSON-LD `Recipe`?
**Resolution:** ~~**Hide the post entirely.** Filter at the list layer. A user must never reach a detail screen that can't render. Implementation: when a post's detail fetch + JSON-LD parse fails for the *first* view, the post is added to a per-install blocklist and removed from subsequent list renders. We *attempt* parse lazily (not eagerly for every list item — would tank performance) and treat the first-failure as ground truth until the next pull-to-refresh.~~ *Amended by [CL-63](#cl-63-us-37--t-640-2026-05-27) (US-37 / T-640, 2026-05-27): JSON-LD-less posts are now classified as articles and rendered via `ArticleDetailView` (HTML body sanitized via `HTMLSanitizer`), not hidden from lists. The blocklist plumbing (`CachedRecipe.jsonLDFailedAt`) stays as the kind discriminator — a non-nil value means "is article," not "is hidden." See CL-63 for full lineage.*

**Acceptance criteria impact:** new **AC-1.7** and new **AC-4.11** in `spec.md` (both amended by CL-63).

### CL-10 — Non-recipe posts (roundups, articles)
**Question:** Include non-recipe posts as articles, or filter out?
**Resolution:** ~~**Punt to Phase 3.** Plan step will sample ~50 recent posts from the live blog, count how many lack Recipe JSON-LD, and decide based on real data. If <5% are non-recipe, CL-9's hide-on-missing logic absorbs them. If higher, we revisit and may add an Article view. Spec marks this as a **TBD: post-mix audit** in `plan.md`.~~ *Closed by [CL-63](#cl-63-us-37--t-640-2026-05-27) (US-37 / T-640, 2026-05-27): adopted the "we revisit and may add an Article view" branch without re-running the Phase-3 audit — Spencer's round-7 user report of a specific roundup post by name ("Best Dutch Oven Recipes (30+ Tried and Tested Favorites)") is the post-mix audit data, and the chosen path is the Article view CL-10's "if higher" branch pre-authorized. See CL-63 for full lineage.*

## Phase 6 amendments (post-launch)

Bugs surfaced after v1.0 was feature-complete. Each is locked by a regression test (see `spec.md` "Test pyramid" section) so it can't silently regress.

- **CL-11 (2026-05-23)** — TelemetryDeck SDK fatal-errors when `signal()` runs before `initialize()`. Fix: transport guards its own `configured` flag. Locked by REG-1. Commit `74231c9`.
- **CL-12 (2026-05-23)** — WP REST `_embed=wp:featuredmedia` is silently dropped when `_fields` is also requested. Fix: omit `_fields` when using `_embed`. Locked by REG-2. Commit `74231c9`.
- **CL-13 (2026-05-23)** — `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` dropped `canonicalURL` on insert, so recipe detail navigation auto-dismissed (AC-4.11) on JSON-LD parse failure. Locked by REG-DOD-NAV-1. Commit `14e4cf9`.
- **CL-14 (2026-05-23)** — `Button` + `LazyVGrid` + `ScrollView` swallows pan gestures on iOS 26, making the feed un-scrollable. Locked by REG-DOD-LIST-SCROLL. Commit `130aa18`.
- **CL-15 (2026-05-23)** — `xcodegen generate` clobbers hand-edited `App/Info.plist` keys (launch screen, orientations) that aren't mirrored in `project.yml`. Locked by REG-INFO-PLIST-CLOBBER. Commit `0c98f6e`.
- **CL-16 (2026-05-23)** — **Cook Mode is in scope for v1.0.** Was an implicit non-feature before (constitution §2 had "Cooking mode (screen-awake, in-app timers)" in spec out-of-scope; this amendment promotes the screen-awake half — no in-app timers yet). Spec amendment via new **US-7** with seven ACs covering the Cook Now button, full-screen takeover, `isIdleTimerDisabled` toggle, swipe/tap step navigation, shared ingredient-check state, exit affordances, and `cookModeStarted` telemetry. Constitution §2 amended to list Cook Mode as in-scope; §9 amended to document the idle-timer toggle as a UIKit device-state change (not new data collection) and to add `cookModeStarted` to the analytics allowlist. Authorized by consultant-pass approval.
- **CL-17 (2026-05-23)** — **Onboarding sheet is in scope for v1.0.** Reverses **CL-7** ("No onboarding"). The consultant-pass argument was that a single-screen, single-button welcome sheet costs ~30 minutes of build time and meaningfully helps first-time users find the heart-save and search affordances. Spec amendment via new **US-8** with three ACs covering the one-time sheet, `dod.onboardingCompletedV1` UserDefaults flag, "Get cooking" dismiss button, and the iPad-same-as-iPhone behavior. CL-7 is now superseded; subsequent readers should treat US-8 as the controlling decision.
- **CL-18 (2026-05-23)** — **Compact layout default is 2-column grid (was 1-column).** Plan §0 / Cluster G previously specified a one-column compact layout in `T-150`; consultant pass argues a denser grid better matches the "browse for inspiration" primary job. Spec amendment via new cross-cutting **CC-9** covering Feed, Categories, Search, and Saved at 2-column compact / 3-column regular, with the RecipeCard hero height tuned to keep ≥3 rows above the fold on iPhone 13 baseline. Implementation tracked in plan.md Phase 6 cluster, task T-300.
- **CL-19 (2026-05-23)** — **Local ingredient index sourced from JSON-LD parses, not from a WP API change.** Recipe Hunters frequently want to search by ingredient (the AC-3.2 wording even calls it out as a known v1 gap). CL-1 already established that the WP REST API does not expose a recipe-body / ingredient field; extending it server-side is out of scope. Resolution: maintain a **local-only** `CachedIngredient` SwiftData table populated as a side effect of `RecipeStore.mergeDetail(_:)`. Each detail open writes its parsed ingredients into the index; subsequent searches run a REST title/excerpt pass and a local ingredient pass and merge with explicit ranking (REST title > REST excerpt > local-only). SwiftData bumped V1 → V2 (lightweight, additive — no field rename or removal) — see `Packages/DODPersistence/MIGRATION.md`. New spec story **US-12** captures the full requirement; new **REG-12** locks the merger + index + filter logic with unit tests. (Note: the original task brief proposed naming this CL-16; renumbered to CL-19 because CL-16 already documents Cook Mode.)

## Phase 7 amendments (comments + ratings, 2026-05-24)

- **CL-21** — Promote "Comments and ratings" out of the v1 deferred list. Constitution §1 changes from "read-only" to "mostly read-only with two write surfaces." Constitution §9 adds guest identity (name + email in Keychain, sent only to dutchovendaddy.com), updates the App Privacy questionnaire to include Contact Info + User Content. Adds AnalyticsEvent cases `recipeRated` and `recipeCommentSubmitted` to the allowlist.
- **CL-22** — Editing/clearing the guest identity from inside the app is deferred to v1.1. v1.0 requires uninstalling to reset. Tracked here; no AC.
- **CL-23** — Rating + comment moderation: the live blog's WP Discussion settings are configured to "comment author must fill out name and email" + held-for-approval. App surfaces both states explicitly (AC-14.4). Owner accepted this UX in the dispatch question; no further amendment needed.

## Phase 8 amendments (post-Phase-6 polish, 2026-05-24)

- **CL-24 (US-16)** — Saved tab icon variant. Decision: use SF Symbol `bookmark` (outline) when the tab is unselected and `bookmark.fill` when selected. **Why:** matches iOS system convention (compare Mail's "Flagged" tab and Safari's bookmarks); avoids a custom asset; selection-aware variant is what the rest of `AppTab.systemImage` does implicitly via SwiftUI's tab styling. Considered: a custom multi-color bookmark + a single non-filling outline. The system-symbol fill behavior wins because it stays correct across iOS versions and respects user appearance settings without us touching color tokens.
- **CL-25 (US-16)** — Tab order. Decision: **Recipes → Categories → Saved → Search** (current is Recipes → Categories → Search → Saved). **Why:** Saved is the "return user" surface and deserves a thumb-zone slot; Search is the lowest-traffic surface per existing telemetry. Considered: leaving Saved at position 4 and only changing the icon. Rejected because the original ask is to "swap" the two — half-doing it would surface a second decision later. Changing `AppTab.allCases` order is the single source of truth and is what the implementing PR touches.
- **CL-26 (US-17)** — Saved-recipes widget sizes. Decision: small (1 saved recipe) + medium (3 saved recipes). **Why:** matches the existing today's-featured widget surface area; `.systemLarge` adds layout cost for a usage pattern (browsing many saves on the home screen) that doesn't yet have evidence. Considered: add `.systemLarge` showing 6 saves. Deferred to v1.1; revisit only if user testing surfaces demand.
- **CL-27 (US-17)** — Saved-recipes widget empty state. Decision: dedicated placeholder ("Save a recipe to see it here") that tap-targets to the Saved tab via a new `dod://saved` deep link. **Why:** failing silently with a blank card violates "no surprise empty states" (the same principle as AC-1.5, AC-3.4, AC-5.8). Considered: hide the widget entirely when empty. Rejected because iOS widgets don't have a hide-self affordance — the user would see a blank card; explicit copy is friendlier.
- **CL-28 (US-17)** — Saved-recipes widget refresh trigger. Decision: host app forces `WidgetCenter.shared.reloadTimelines(ofKind: "SavedRecipesWidget")` whenever `SavedStore` writes or removes a saved recipe; in addition, the widget's timeline provider returns entries with a 15-minute refresh cap as a safety net for crashes or background-write paths. **Why:** the existing featured widget already uses the snapshot-then-reload pattern (see `WidgetSnapshotStore.write` + the call site in `DODApp.swift`); reusing it keeps the contract uniform across both widgets. Considered: background fetch in the widget extension. Rejected per AC-17.6 and NFR-3 (no background network in v1).
- **CL-29 (US-17)** — Saved-recipes widget tap target. Decision: tap on a recipe row deep-links to that recipe's detail (`dod://recipe/<id>`, existing US-9 parser); tap on the widget chrome (placeholder state, or whitespace around recipe rows) deep-links to the Saved tab (`dod://saved`, new parser case). **Why:** matches user mental model — tapping a card means "open this thing"; tapping background means "go to where this thing lives." Considered: route every tap to the Saved tab regardless. Rejected because it wastes the one-tap-from-cook ergonomic that the user story explicitly wants.
- **CL-30 (US-18)** — Dark-mode audit framing. Decision: structure US-18 as **audit + targeted fixes**, not as a feature with deliverables guaranteed in advance. The audit doc (`appearance-audit.md`) is the deliverable; any code change is incidental. **Why:** constitution §7 already mandates WCAG AA in both modes — US-18 enforces what's already required, surface by surface, with snapshot tests filling the gaps before any visual change so the diff is provable. Considered: opening this as a list of "known dark-mode bugs to fix." Rejected because there's no current bug list — the work is to find out, not to patch a known set. AC-18.6 explicitly authorizes a clean audit to close the story.
- **CL-31 (US-19, 2026-05-24)** — Categories modernization scope: **layout-pass-only, not a DesignSystem token change.** The backlog entry for "Categories tab — modernize visual language" explicitly flagged the choice: token-level change in `DODDesignSystem` (which would re-snapshot every component across Feed, Search, Saved, recipe detail, comments, widget, etc.) vs. a Categories-only layout pass. Decision: layout-pass only. **Why:** the staleness here is rooted in `CategoryListView`'s specific layout choices — `.plain` `List`, hand-rolled chevron `HStack`, no `.searchable` — not in the design tokens. The tokens (`DODColor.label`, `DODColor.labelSecondary`, `DODType.body`, `DODType.caption`, `DODSpacing.*`) already match what the modernized Feed / Search / Saved surfaces use. Changing tokens would force re-recording every existing L4 baseline in the repo (DesignSystem, Feed, Categories, Search, Saved, RecipeDetail, comments, ratings, widget, onboarding) for zero functional gain on the surfaces that already look right. Considered: bumping `DODSpacing` (denser grid) or adding a new "row padding" token. Rejected — no other surface complained about its spacing; over-fitting one decision to one surface is exactly the failure mode this CL guards against. Implementing PR: T-340 / [#22](https://github.com/adamsned/dod-ios-app/pull/22); the PR diff is bounded to `Packages/DODFeatureCategories/**` plus test snapshots.
- **CL-32 (US-19, 2026-05-24)** — List style: `.insetGrouped` over `.plain` or `.grouped`. **Why:** `.insetGrouped` is the iOS-stock pattern for a sectioned-but-only-one-section list (Settings, Mail account list, Shortcuts gallery picker) — rounded card with system separators, inset from screen edges, system grouped background. `.plain` is what the current implementation uses and is what reads as stale (full-bleed rows with hairline separators is a 2018 idiom on a 2026 device). `.grouped` (the older style) renders unrounded inset cards and looks weirdly old-school in iOS 17+. Considered: keeping `.plain` and only swapping the chevron treatment. Rejected — the inset card is half of what makes the modern surface feel modern; swapping just the chevron leaves the flat-list smell. Considered: rolling our own `ScrollView` + `LazyVStack` of custom cells matching the Feed grid card style. Rejected — Categories is a list, not a gallery; over-designing it would diverge from iOS Settings conventions and make every other category-app-list in iOS look out of place by comparison. The right move is to lean into the system style, not to invent.
- **CL-33 (US-19, 2026-05-24)** — Add `.searchable` filter inline with the modernization, not as a separate follow-up. The backlog entry doesn't mention search explicitly, but the live blog has 35+ categories per the production data sample taken 2026-05-24, and the existing list is unfilterable except by manual scroll. Adding `.searchable` is a one-line modifier and is what every iOS-stock long-list surface ships with (Settings, Shortcuts, Music browse). **Why land it in the same PR:** it's the same code path (the categories array), the same testing surface (snapshot baselines that already need re-recording for the layout change), and the same "this surface feels stale" complaint the backlog entry is reacting to. Splitting it into a follow-up T-341 would re-record the baselines twice for no review-quality gain. Considered: defer search to a separate task. Rejected — it's bundled here because the marginal cost is near-zero and the marginal value is high. If user testing later wants a more elaborate search UX (search by category description, recently-tapped categories, etc.), that's a fresh US in the backlog; this CL covers only the bare client-side name filter.

## Phase 10 amendments (2026-05-24)

- **CL-34 (US-20, 2026-05-24)** — Search filter chip iconography: `tag.fill` over `tag`, `number`, `crop.rotate`, or keeping `folder`. **Why `tag.fill`:** WordPress categories are taxonomy terms (CL-3 — the blog's "Categories vs Tags" decision treats both as filterable taxonomies; the app surfaces only categories in v1 but the semantic is "filter by taxonomy"). `tag.fill` is the iOS-stock glyph for that semantic — Reminders uses it on its tag filter pills, Files uses it in the tag column header, and Photos uses it on the people-and-pets tag chips. The `.fill` variant matches the chip's selected-state convention: the chip already toggles a filled cast-iron-brown capsule when a category is picked, so a filled glyph reads as "this chip is a filter primitive" even in its idle unselected state. Considered: outline `tag` — rejected because the surrounding chip glyphs (`clock`, `clock.arrow.circlepath`) are visually weighted as solid line drawings; a thin outline `tag` would look anemic next to them at the 13-pt caption rendering size. Considered: `number` — rejected because `number` reads as "ordinal / count," not as "filter by name." Considered: `crop.rotate` (the WP admin tag editor uses something like it) — rejected as obscure; nobody outside the WP admin recognizes that glyph. Considered: keeping `folder` and only changing the empty-state glyph — rejected because the empty state's `questionmark.folder` is correctly read as "thing-not-found in a search context" (the `?` carries the meaning, not the folder); the live bug is on the chip, not on the empty state. Implementing PR: T-350 / [#23](https://github.com/adamsned/dod-ios-app/pull/23).

- **CL-35 (US-21, 2026-05-24)** — **Widget image bridge: file-export to App Group container.** The featured widget (US-9) and the saved-recipes widget (US-17 / T-322) both currently render a placeholder glyph in place of the recipe hero image. The root cause T-322's agent flagged: *"The existing `CachedImage` SwiftData store keeps bytes in SwiftData, not filenames in the App Group container. The widget extension renders a placeholder for nil filenames per AC-17.5."* The widget extension cannot reach the host app's SwiftData store (separate processes, separate sandboxes — App Group sharing covers UserDefaults + files but not SwiftData containers without a custom container URL), and constitution §9 implicit + AC-17.6 forbid widget-side network fetches. Three options considered:

  1. **File-export bridge (chosen).** When the host caches image bytes via `RecipeStore.cacheImage(url:bytes:...)`, also write the bytes to a file inside the shared App Group container under a deterministic filename derived from the URL (SHA256 + extension preserved). The snapshot wire format carries the filename; the widget reads the file via `FileManager.default.containerURL(forSecurityApplicationGroupIdentifier:)`. Eviction stays write-through — `evictImagesIfNeeded()` deletes both the SwiftData row and the file. **Cost:** ~2× storage for images that get cached (200 MB SwiftData cap + same bytes on disk under the App Group). Widget code stays simple — read a file, hand the URL to `AsyncImage`, done. **Why this wins:** the saved-recipes widget already half-implemented this pattern in T-321/T-322 — `SavedRecipesWidgetSnapshot.Entry.heroImageFilename` already exists, and the widget already calls `containerURL(forSecurityApplicationGroupIdentifier:)` + appends the filename. The host side is the missing half. Bias-toward-Option-A from the task prompt; investigation confirmed the saved widget was already on this track.
  2. **Shared SwiftData container.** Move the `CachedImage` SwiftData store into the App Group container so the widget extension can `open` it directly. Single source of truth; no 2× storage. **Why rejected:** SwiftData container opens are heavy (10–50ms even for a small store) and the widget timeline provider is called frequently. The widget process would need to migrate the schema on first open if the host hasn't bumped it yet (Schema V3 today). Concurrent writes from host + opportunistic reads from widget would also need write-protection — SwiftData's `ModelContainer` is not designed for cross-process readers and "careful concurrency" handwaving here is risky for a widget surface where a crash means a blank tile on the user's home screen. Option A trades ~200 MB of disk (worst case, never hit in practice — see CL-35.2 below) for a much simpler concurrency story.
  3. **URLSession in the widget.** Widget extension fetches the image directly from the WP CDN. **Why rejected:** AC-17.6 wording ("No network calls from the widget extension itself") and constitution §9 implicit (no background network if avoidable) both forbid this. The featured widget has the same shape so the same constraint applies — there's no "this widget can but that widget can't" carve-out worth introducing.

  **CL-35.2 — Storage worst-case:** the bridge writes a file for *every* image bytes cached, not just those needed by the widget. For the featured widget today that means the top-5 feed-entry hero images (CL-9 says max 5 entries in `WidgetSnapshot`). For the saved widget that means up to 3 saved-recipe hero images (CL-26 says max 3). Generally only ~8 files exist concurrently. The 200 MB image cache cap (NFR-2) already bounds the SwiftData store; mirroring it as files yields ~200 MB more on disk in the absolute worst case, far below any iPhone storage warning threshold. No mitigation needed in v1; revisit if usage data later shows a problem.

  **CL-35.3 — Eviction ordering:** the bridge deletes the file inside the same `evictImagesIfNeeded()` SwiftData transaction that deletes the row. If the file delete fails (file already gone, container vanished, etc.) the SwiftData delete still succeeds — the file delete is best-effort. Both directions ("row exists but file gone" and "file exists but row gone") are gracefully handled by the readers: the widget renders the gradient placeholder when the file is missing, and the host's `image(url:)` accessor only consults SwiftData so a stale file is invisible.

  **Implementing PR:** T-360 / `feat/T-360-latest-recipe-widget`. PR diff is bounded to `Packages/DODPersistence/Sources/DODPersistence/RecipeStore.swift` (write/evict hooks), a new `Packages/DODSupport/Sources/DODSupport/WidgetImageBridge.swift` (filename derivation + container URL resolver — shared by host + widget), `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/FeedDependencies.swift` (snapshot now carries `heroImageFilename`), `Widget/FeaturedRecipeWidgetEntryView.swift` (resolve filename to `file://`), plus widget snapshot wire-format additive bump and tests in `Packages/DODSupport/Tests/` + `Packages/DODPersistence/Tests/`.

- **CL-36 (US-21, 2026-05-24)** — Display-name change from "Today's Recipe" to "Latest Recipe." **Why:** the widget already reflects whatever sits at the top of the WP feed — i.e. the newest post, not a daily-curated pick. The "Today's" name implied a cadence the widget never honored (the feed updates whenever the blog publishes; "today" might be days-old if no new post has landed). "Latest" matches what the widget actually shows. **Why land it as a CL even though it's a single string:** it's a user-facing surface (visible in the iOS widget gallery), and any marketing screenshots, App Store screenshots, or TestFlight release notes pinning the old name will need a parallel update. Recording the decision here lets a future reader see why the name in screenshots and in source no longer match. Considered: "Featured Recipe" (matches the variable name `FeaturedRecipeWidget`). Rejected because "featured" suggests editorial selection, which the widget doesn't do — it just shows the newest post. "Latest" is the most honest single word. Considered: leave the name and add a CL noting the mismatch. Rejected because the user explicitly requested the rename and the cost is one string. Description copy stays unchanged ("See the latest Dutch Oven Daddy recipe right on your home screen.") — that wording was already accurate. The widget kind identifier (`"com.dutchovendaddy.DODApp.Widget.FeaturedRecipe"`) and the internal type name (`FeaturedRecipeWidget`) stay unchanged so the existing `WidgetCenter.shared.reloadAllTimelines()` plumbing and the marker code in PR #19 (`widgetOpened(kind: "featured", recipeID:)`) keep working without a parallel rename — only the display name shown to users moves.

- **CL-37 (US-22, 2026-05-24)** — Lock-screen widget family scope: **`.accessoryRectangular` only.** Apple's `WidgetFamily` exposes three lock-screen-eligible families on iOS 16+: `.accessoryRectangular` (multi-line, ~170×72pt tall enough for a title + one body line), `.accessoryCircular` (round, very small — built for single glyphs / step counters / battery-style readouts), and `.accessoryInline` (a single line of text rendered alongside the clock/date glyphs at the top of the Lock Screen). The backlog entry asked specifically for a "rectangular-family lock-screen widget"; this CL formalizes that and explains why the other two are not in scope. **Why rectangular:** the latest-recipe surface needs to show *both* a title and a short description, and the user must be able to identify a recipe at a glance — the title alone in `.accessoryInline` doesn't carry enough context ("Bourbon Berry Cake" is fine; "Recipe With Three Things" is meaningless), and a `.accessoryCircular` surface forces a glyph-only treatment that has no good answer for "what recipe?" because recipes don't have brand glyphs. **Why not `.accessoryCircular`:** a useful circular surface needs a 1–2 character payload or a single icon. The featured-recipe widget data (title + excerpt) doesn't fit either. We'd ship a knife-and-fork glyph that means nothing — which is exactly the placeholder anti-pattern we already removed from the home-screen widget. **Why not `.accessoryInline`:** the inline family is shared between every inline widget the user has installed (weather, battery, calendar event, etc.) — it cycles through them and is rate-limited by the system. The recipe title would surface so rarely it would feel broken. **Why same snapshot as the home-screen widget:** lock-screen widgets are text-info-only by design (no image rendering, monochrome rendering pipeline) — the *only* fields the rectangular widget needs from `WidgetSnapshot.Entry` are `title` and `excerpt`, both of which the existing US-9 snapshot already carries for the home-screen widget. Adding a separate `LockScreenWidgetSnapshot` would write the same bytes to a second App Group key for zero gain. Reusing `WidgetSnapshot` also means there's no new host-side observer or write-site to maintain — every existing call to `WidgetSnapshotStore.write(...)` (via `FeedViewModel`'s post-load hook) already updates the lock-screen widget the same way it updates the home-screen one. Considered: ship `.accessoryRectangular` + `.accessoryCircular` together as a "lock-screen family." Rejected per the circular rationale above. Considered: defer the lock-screen widget entirely and revisit in v1.1. Rejected — the home-screen widget infrastructure (App Group, snapshot store, deep-link parser) is already in place and the marginal cost of the rectangular surface is small (one new `WidgetConfiguration` + one new entry view, ~70 lines of code per the SavedRecipesWidget template). Considered: add `.widgetAccentable` modifiers to selectively tint parts of the rendered text. Deferred — text-only, default monochrome rendering is the most legible default; if user testing shows the recipe title is hard to spot against busy Lock Screen wallpapers, that's a fresh US in the backlog. Implementing PR: T-370 (this branch).

- **CL-38 (US-16, 2026-05-24)** — **In-recipe Save affordance flips from heart to bookmark.** Explicitly amends `AC-16.3` and extends `CL-24`. **Lineage:** CL-24 (2026-05-24) chose `bookmark` / `bookmark.fill` for the Saved tab icon over the original heart, but AC-16.3 deliberately carved the in-recipe Save heart out of the swap — the rationale at the time was to "keep this change reversible and surface a single decision at a time." After the T-310 tab-icon change shipped (PR #10), user feedback was that the in-recipe heart now reads as inconsistent next to the bookmark tab — the affordance for the same action ("save this recipe for offline") shouldn't change glyph depending on where you're looking at it. CL-38 reverses the AC-16.3 carve-out and extends CL-24's bookmark decision into every saved-recipes surface in the app: the in-recipe navigation-bar Save button (`AC-4.7`), the floating-action Save button (T-302, sticky variant of the same button), the Saved tab empty-state icon + copy (`AC-5.8`), the Save snackbar wording (`AC-5.1`), the onboarding bullet copy (`AC-8.1`), the `OpenSavedRecipesIntent` Siri shortcut glyph (`AC-10.4`), and the EmptyState / OnboardingSheet doc-preview heart references in DesignSystem. **What CL-38 does NOT touch:** the wire-format analytics string `kind: .saved` in `widgetOpened` (T-323 surface — internal string, not user-facing copy); the Live Activity Cook Mode glyphs (US-11 territory, unrelated to saving); the other tab icons (Feed `house`, Categories `square.grid.2x2`, Search `magnifyingglass`). Considered: leaving AC-16.3 as-is and treating the in-recipe heart as legacy / brand. Rejected — the original CL-24 + AC-16.3 split was a deliberately reversible decision, and the reversal trigger ("if it feels inconsistent in user testing") explicitly fired. Considered: a custom multi-color bookmark glyph distinct from the tab icon's. Rejected for the same reason CL-24 picked the stock SF Symbol — the system bookmark already follows fill conventions across iOS versions; a custom asset adds maintenance for zero functional gain. Implementation tracked as T-380 in `tasks.md` Phase 10 cluster.

- **CL-39 (US-23, 2026-05-24)** — **Widget appearance audit framing: audit + targeted fixes, not pre-listed bugs.** Mirrors CL-30 for US-18 / dark-mode polish. The backlog entry "Widget readability under 'Clear' and 'Tinted' home-screen appearances" doesn't list specific widget surfaces that fail under iOS 18+ Tinted / Vibrant rendering — it asks the work to **find out**. The audit document (`specs/dod-ios-app/widget-appearance-audit.md`) is the deliverable; any code change is incidental. **Why this framing:** constitution §7 already mandates WCAG AA contrast across appearance modes; US-23 enforces that mandate surface-by-surface for the iOS 18+ widget appearances that landed after constitution §7 was last amended. The audit's job is to inventory which widget surfaces (`FeaturedRecipeWidget` small / medium / placeholder, `SavedRecipesWidget` small / medium / empty) render legibly under each rendering-mode environment value (`.fullColor`, `.accented`, `.vibrant`) the WidgetKit pipeline can hand the widget extension, and to either fix the failures in-place (bounded `WidgetCard*.swift` changes) or to file T-391+ follow-up tasks for fixes that don't fit the PR scope. **What CL-39 explicitly authorizes:** a clean audit closes the story (AC-23.6). If the existing brand asset-catalog colors already produce legible monochromatic treatments under the system's automatic desaturation pass, and the few hardcoded `Color.black` / `Color.white` literals live only on overlay layers the system also tints correctly, then the audit doc + the new Tinted-mode snapshot baselines (which exist purely as a regression net) ship as the complete deliverable, with no `WidgetCard*.swift` source changes. Considered: scope the story as "ship `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.fullColor)` on every hero image and call it done." Rejected — the audit might surface that the hero image actually looks better when the system tints it (the monochromatic treatment is what users opted into when they picked a tint), and pre-deciding the answer skips the surface-by-surface verification step constitution §7 demands. Considered: defer the audit to a v1.1 post-launch task. Rejected — the iOS 18 appearances shipped to users on day one and home-screen customization is one of the surface-level moments a new user picks the app up from a screenshot; if the widget looks broken in their tinted screenshot, the install rate suffers. The cost of this audit is bounded (one document + a handful of additive snapshot tests); the cost of skipping it is a customer-visible breakage iOS 18 surfaces on a stock setup. **Lock-screen widget scoping:** US-22 / T-370 (the lock-screen rectangular widget) ships on a separate branch (`feat/T-370-lock-screen-widget`, PR #26) that has not merged to `main` at the time US-23 starts. The audit is scoped to home-screen widgets only — lock-screen accessory widgets render in a different system pipeline (`AccessoryWidgetGroup` family, system-monochromatic, not the home-screen Tinted/Clear pipeline this story audits) and would be a parallel audit if a lock-screen surface ever needs its own verification. Document the scoping in `widget-appearance-audit.md` explicitly. If T-370 happens to merge before US-23's PR rebases, the auditor may opt to extend the matrix to the lock-screen surface at review time, otherwise it stays a documented gap with the framing above. Implementing PR: T-390.

- **CL-40 (US-25, 2026-05-25)** — **Saved Recipes widget gallery description copy: "Quick access to your saved recipes."** Amends `AC-17.1`. **Lineage:** the original description shipped with T-321 / US-17 — "Your saved recipes, one tap from a cook." — tried to lean into a cooking-tactile pun ("one tap from *a cook*"), but parses ambiguously in the iOS widget gallery: "a cook" reads first as a noun ("a person who cooks") rather than the intended verb sense ("a cooking session"), and the construction is unusual enough that a user picking the widget from the gallery pauses to parse it instead of immediately knowing what the widget does. iOS-stock widget-gallery descriptions are short, direct, action-noun phrases (Reminders: "Quickly see your upcoming reminders"; Photos: "View highlights from your photo library"; Notes: "Quickly see your notes"). This CL rewrites the string to match that convention. Same pattern CL-36 established for the "Today's Recipe" → "Latest Recipe" rename — user-facing widget-gallery copy gets a CL even when the diff is a single string. **Chosen string:** "Quick access to your saved recipes." Mirrors the iOS-stock "Quickly see your X" / "Quick access to X" idiom (Reminders, Notes, Stocks), reads in one beat, and is honest about what the widget actually does (the widget renders the most-recently-saved recipes with deep-link tap targets — i.e. quick access to them). **Alternatives considered:** (1) **"Your saved recipes at a glance."** Considered. Rejected — "at a glance" is closer to the iOS-stock "Glances" Watch-app terminology and reads as describing a passive read-only surface; the saved widget is interactive (tap → recipe detail), so "at a glance" undersells the affordance. (2) **"Tap to open any recipe you've saved."** Considered. Rejected — verbose and instructs the user how to use the widget instead of telling them what the widget is. Apple's stock widget descriptions describe; they don't instruct. (3) **"Your bookmarked recipes, right on the home screen."** Considered. Rejected — the comma + "right on the home screen" clause is redundant context (the widget gallery is the home-screen widget picker by definition; the user already knows they're picking a home-screen widget), and "bookmarked" is correct vocabulary post-CL-38 but adds a syllable that "saved" doesn't. (4) **Keep the original.** Rejected — the user explicitly flagged it as awkward; the cost of the rewrite is one string + this CL, and the gain is one less ambiguous moment in the widget gallery. **Why land it as a CL even though it's a single string:** it's a user-facing surface (visible in the iOS widget gallery), and any marketing screenshots, App Store screenshots, or TestFlight release notes pinning the old description will need a parallel update. Recording the decision here lets a future reader see why the description in screenshots and in source no longer match. **What CL-40 does NOT touch:** the widget kind identifier (`SavedRecipesWidget`), the display name (`"Saved Recipes"`), the supported families (`.systemSmall` + `.systemMedium`), the `containerBackground` treatment, the snapshot wire format, the deep-link grammar (`dod://recipe/<id>`, `dod://saved`), the timeline cadence, the analytics event (`widgetOpened(kind: "saved", ...)`), or the L4 widget-face baselines (`SavedWidgetSnapshotTests`) — the description string lives in the iOS widget gallery UI, not on the rendered widget face itself. Implementing PR: T-400.

## Phase 10 amendments (round 2, 2026-05-25)

- **CL-41 (US-26, 2026-05-25)** — **`CommentComposer` action-row order: visually swap to Submit-leading, Cancel-trailing per the user's literal request.** The composer is rendered inline inside `RecipeDetailRatingsSection.commentComposerSection` (a `VStack`) on the recipe detail screen, not inside an iOS `confirmationDialog` / `alert` / `Form` sheet — so the platform-default action ordering for *those* containers doesn't apply automatically. **The state before this CL:** `CommentComposer.actions` rendered `HStack { Cancel; Spacer(); Submit }` — Cancel at leading, Submit at trailing, with a flexible gap between them. That ordering matches the iPhone-stock convention Apple's Human Interface Guidelines (Foundations → Layout → Action buttons; HIG section "Buttons → Sheets, action sheets, and alerts") documents for sheet-style button rows: *"In a sheet, put the action that completes the task in the upper-trailing corner or, in a form-style sheet's button row, at the trailing edge of the row; place the cancel action at the leading edge."* Mail's compose sheet, Reminders' new-list sheet, and Calendar's new-event sheet all follow that pattern. **What the backlog asked for:** the round-4 entry literally said "**Swap** 'Submit' and 'Cancel' button positions in the rating-submit composer." The most direct read is "put Submit where Cancel is and Cancel where Submit is" — i.e. swap their visual positions. The backlog goes on to note "Worth a CL on the iOS convention here — typically primary actions live at the trailing/right edge with cancel at leading/left on iPhone, but `confirmationDialog` and `alert` defaults differ. Capture the chosen pattern + cite Apple HIG when this graduates." That sentence explicitly invites a CL where the chosen pattern *may* depart from the iPhone-stock HIG default — i.e. the user is on notice that the swap may go against HIG and is asking us to make a deliberate call. **Final decision:** honor the literal swap — render `HStack { Submit; Spacer(); Cancel }` so Submit sits at the leading edge and Cancel sits at the trailing edge of the row. This is a deliberate departure from the iPhone-stock pattern; the trade-off is that it matches the user's stated visual preference (Submit on the left of the row, Cancel on the right) at the cost of being inconsistent with iOS Mail / Reminders / Calendar compose sheets. The HIG paragraph cited above is the canonical authority, but it doesn't *forbid* the swapped order — it describes the typical pattern. For an inline form-section button row inside a `VStack` on a scrolling detail screen (not a system-presented sheet), the deviation reads as a brand choice rather than a usability error. The cancel role (`role: .cancel`) is preserved on the trailing button so VoiceOver and the system back-gesture still treat it as the cancel affordance — the swap is purely visual. **Alternatives considered:** (1) **Keep the existing HIG-compliant ordering and only tighten the gap** (remove `Spacer()`, both buttons at leading). Rejected — the user's word was "swap," not "tighten"; the swap interpretation is more honest to the literal ask. (2) **Render the buttons as a `ToolbarItem` pair** (leading Cancel, trailing Submit) and drop the inline `HStack`. Rejected — the composer is not wrapped in a `NavigationStack` at its usage site (`RecipeDetailRatingsSection.commentComposerSection` is a plain `VStack`), so toolbar items would not render; introducing a `NavigationStack` purely for two buttons would be over-engineering. (3) **Match `confirmationDialog`'s implicit ordering** (Cancel last, primary above in a sheet-style stack). Rejected — `confirmationDialog` is a SwiftUI presentation modifier, not an inline `VStack` composer; the composer is a sheet-style form, not a system action sheet, and the HIG paragraph for inline form action rows takes precedence for the *layout* — only the *order* deviates per the user's request. **What this means for VoiceOver / accessibility:** the cancel-role button still announces as "Cancel" with the cancel trait regardless of visual position, and the submit button still announces as "Submit" / "Submitting…" with the standard button trait. The swap doesn't change the focus order in the underlying `HStack` (SwiftUI's accessibility tree mirrors the source order, which is now Submit-then-Cancel). VoiceOver users land on Submit first and Cancel second — opposite the visual-position-aware reader, but consistent within itself (the source order matches what's drawn). **What this CL does NOT touch:** the `.borderedProminent` Submit treatment + accent tint, the `.bordered` Cancel treatment, the `role: .cancel` semantic on the Cancel button, the `isSubmitting` disabled state on both buttons, the `canSubmit` gate guarding Submit, the "Submitting…" label swap when in-flight, the button accessibility labels, the `onSubmit` / `onCancel` callback signatures, the outer `.padding(DODSpacing.lg)` on the composer, or the `Spacer(minLength: 0)` above the actions row inside the composer body. The diff is bounded to the order of the two `Button { }` blocks inside `actions`. Implementing PR: T-410.

- **CL-42 (US-26, 2026-05-25)** — **Sticky `RecipeDetailFloatingActions` Save + Share row is removed; amends T-302's "Phase 6 recipe-detail polish" decision.** T-302 (Phase 6 consultant pass, 2026-05-23) shipped `RecipeDetailFloatingActions.swift` as a bottom-trailing overlay anchored to `RecipeDetailView.body` via `.overlay(alignment: .bottomTrailing)`. The justification at the time was "polish — keep the primary save + share actions reachable when the user has scrolled past the nav bar." On-device feedback after T-380 (CL-38, bookmark glyph everywhere) landed: the nav-bar Save + Share (AC-4.7 + AC-4.8) are sufficient on their own; the sticky bottom row duplicates the same two actions and reads as visual clutter on top of the (scrollable) ingredients / instructions / related / comments content underneath it. **Why removal, not relocation:** the nav bar already has a stable, expected home for the two actions. Apple's HIG (Foundations → Toolbars → Navigation bars) frames primary nav-bar actions as the canonical destination for screen-level commands. Maintaining two copies of the same affordance, where one is permanently visible and the other is "also there at the bottom for convenience," forces the user to learn that the two are wired to the same view-model state — a cognitive load that earns nothing because the nav bar is always within thumb reach on iPhone via the natural one-handed grip that keeps the top of the screen accessible (the iPhone 16 Pro physical button layout + iOS's haptic scroll-to-top tap keep this true). Considered: keep the floating actions on iPad (where the nav bar is further from the thumb) and remove on iPhone. Rejected — the spec doesn't yet support per-size-class conditional UI for a save affordance, and CC-8 already mandates an adaptive iPad layout via `NavigationSplitView` (which puts the toolbar inside the detail pane, still within reach). Considered: replace the floating Save + Share with a single floating "Cook Now" CTA when Cook Mode is available. Rejected — `CookNowCTA` already renders inline inside the recipe detail's scroll content (above the ingredients section), and adding a duplicate floating copy would re-introduce the exact problem this CL solves. **What's removed:** `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/RecipeDetailFloatingActions.swift` is deleted. `RecipeDetailView.body`'s `.overlay(alignment: .bottomTrailing) { floatingActionsOverlay }` modifier is removed. The `floatingActionsOverlay` private `@ViewBuilder` property on `RecipeDetailView` is removed. **What's preserved:** the nav-bar Save (bookmark glyph per T-380) + Share buttons stay exactly as they are. The save haptic via `.sensoryFeedback(.success, trigger: viewModel.isSaved)` on the body stays — it's wired to the view-model state, not to the removed view. `viewModel.toggleSaved()` and `viewModel.didShare()` are untouched. **What this means for snapshots:** any L4 baseline that includes the sticky bottom-trailing button stack needs re-recording, but a grep for `RecipeDetailView` snapshot tests turns up zero direct screen-level baselines in the repo (the existing `RecipeDetailRatingsViewSnapshotTests` only snapshots `RecipeDetailRatingsSection`, not the parent `RecipeDetailView`). So the snapshot churn from CL-42 is limited to anything that renders `RecipeDetailFloatingActions` directly (no callers other than the deleted overlay) — i.e. zero. Implementing PR: T-410.

- **CL-43 (T-420, 2026-05-25)** — **Why a live newest-post L2 test in addition to the existing REG-2 fixture-based one.** REG-2 (constitution §6 AC-T4, locked by `DODIntegrationTestsTests.LiveAPITests.postsReturnNonNilHeroImage`) already asserts `WPRestClient.posts()` returns posts with non-nil `heroImage` URLs against the live API — that test catches the original `_embed` / `_fields` interaction bug it was written for and is the right shape for that failure mode. REG-16 / T-420 adds a *second* L2 test that targets a different failure surface: contract drift on **newly-published posts** that the existing fixture-tier proves nothing about. Two distinct reasons to land it alongside REG-2 rather than collapsing the two:
  1. **Newest-post specificity.** REG-2's assertion is "at least half of the posts in page 1 have hero images" — a sane shape sanity check, but it lets a hero-image regression on the most-recently-published post hide behind older posts that still happen to round-trip. Many WP REST drift modes are publish-time: a plugin update changes how a brand-new post's `_embed.wp:featuredmedia` array is serialized, or a server-side cache only invalidates for new posts. REG-16 asserts on `per_page=1` (the newest post specifically) plus on the same newest post turning up in the local store after the production-equivalent feed-load path runs. The newest post is the surface that matters for US-1's AC-1.1 ("home feed shows the 20 most recent published recipes") — if a regression hides the newest post, the entire app's first-glance value disappears, and REG-2's "half pass" gate doesn't fire.
  2. **Early-warning surface area.** The nightly job (per AC-T3) is the project's contract-drift watchdog. Each additional live assertion is a cheap-to-add early-warning probe: the cost is one HTTP call inside the same job that already makes a handful, and the value is catching shape regressions before a user files an App Store review. Considered: extend REG-2 in place to also assert "first item has hero image" rather than adding a new test method. Rejected — that conflates two distinct failure modes (fixture round-trip vs. live newest-post pickup) in one named test, which makes the nightly failure report less actionable when one of them fires. The two-named-tests shape mirrors the same pattern `LiveAPITests.commentsEndpointReturnsRealData` + `LiveAPITests.ratingsEndpointReturnsRealAverage` already establish for REG-13 / REG-14. Considered: route the assertion through a full `FeedViewModel.refresh()` instantiation (real `RecipeStore`, real `LiveFeedDependencies`, real `NetworkMonitor`). Rejected for v1 — the SwiftData `ModelContainer` setup needs a Main-actor host and adds significant scaffolding for a test whose only purpose is to assert the network-tier shape; the equivalent `WPRestClient.posts()` call (which the production `LiveFeedDependencies.fetchPosts(page:)` wraps verbatim) plus a direct `RecipeStore.cache(listItems:)` + `RecipeStore.listItems(forIDs:)` round-trip exercises the same code path the production refresh runs and keeps the test inside the existing `DODIntegrationTests` package surface (which depends on `DODNetworking` + `DODDomain` only — no SwiftData container plumbing). The test still proves the assertion the backlog asked for ("the newest post is present in the local store after refresh") because `RecipeStore.cache(listItems:)` is the same call the production `FeedViewModel.loadInitial()` makes via `LiveFeedDependencies.cache(listItems:)`. **Live-blog-empty fallback:** if the blog returns zero posts for `per_page=1` (extraordinarily unlikely but possible during a maintenance window or a fresh-install dev environment hitting a staging URL), the test fails with a clear `try #require(...)` message rather than crashing — same shape the existing `LiveAPITests.postsReturnNonNilHeroImage` uses for the equivalent guard. Implementing PR: T-420.

- **CL-44 (US-24, 2026-05-25)** — **Categories tab brown: surface-level tinting over per-cell tinting.** The backlog entry for "Categories tab brown" (round-4 round, post-Phase-10) explicitly flagged the choice: paint the entire scroll surface brown via `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden) + .background(DODColor.castIronBrown)` on the `List`, or paint each row cell brown via `.listRowBackground(DODColor.castIronBrown)`. Both are one-line modifier additions; their visual implications are very different. **Decision: surface-level.** **Why:** the user's framing — "the categories list cells AND the `.searchable` field background in the Categories tab should pick up the same brown used by the recipe cards" — explicitly names two surfaces that the cell-level approach cannot reach. The `.searchable` field in the `.navigationBarDrawer` placement is rendered by SwiftUI in the navigation-bar drawer area, OUTSIDE the `List`'s row-background layer; `.listRowBackground` modifies row interiors and has zero effect on the search field's container area. Painting just the rows brown would leave the search field's surround as the system grouped background (cream in light, near-black in dark), producing a two-tone "rows are brown / search is cream" treatment the user did not ask for. The surface-level pattern paints the area around the inset-grouped row cards AND the area behind the `.searchable` field in one move — both surfaces match the brand brown, the user gets the literal interpretation of their request. **Why not paint the cells themselves brown:** because the cells house the row content (category name `DODColor.label`, recipe count `DODColor.labelSecondary`, chevron `Color.tertiary` system-derived). All three of those text colors are tuned for legibility against the system cell background — white in light, dark earth in dark. Repainting the cell to a fixed-brown (`castIronBrown` does not vary by appearance — `#3D2B1F` in both light and dark per the colorset definition) would put dark-on-dark text in light mode AND dark-on-dark text in dark mode, blowing the constitution §7 WCAG AA mandate from both ends. The surface-level pattern leaves cells system-default so the established row-text contrast carries forward unchanged. **`.searchable` legibility under brown:** the field's chrome is a system-tinted glass material; iOS layers it above the scroll surface and raises its material weight to maintain contrast against whatever's behind it. Empirically (verified via the re-recorded snapshot baselines in this PR) the field stays readable against the dark brown in both light and dark mode. If a future iOS release changes this rendering and the field becomes hard to read, a constitution §7 follow-up T-431 entry is the right framing — custom search-field styling in this PR would invent a SwiftUI affordance with no system-default fallback path. **Considered:** apply a brand-tinted variant of `castIronBrown` (lighter in light mode, true brown in dark) so the surface contrast against the cell fill stays balanced. Rejected — the brand brown IS the brand brown; adjusting it by appearance would mean the Categories surface uses a *different* brown from every other place `castIronBrown` already appears (recipe-card time chip at `Components/RecipeCard.swift:104`, offline banner at `Components/OfflineBanner.swift:34`, snackbar at `Components/Snackbar.swift:48`, search filter chip at `SearchView.swift:187`). The brand cohesion the user asked for is best served by reusing the exact existing token. **Considered:** ship cell-level brown anyway and accept the two-tone surround. Rejected — the user's framing explicitly mentions the search field, so the cell-level pattern fails the literal request. **Considered:** introduce a new mid-warmth `DODColor.surfaceBrown` token tuned per-appearance to act as a Categories-specific surface. Rejected for the same reason CL-31 (US-19 / T-340) rejected new tokens for the modernization pass — the staleness here is layout/surface-color, not the token palette; adding a token to solve a one-surface problem over-fits the design system to a single screen and forces every existing baseline that uses the old token to be re-recorded for zero gain elsewhere. Implementation tracked as T-430 in `tasks.md` Phase 10 cluster.

- **CL-47 (T-396, 2026-05-25)** — **Lock-screen widget readability audit: parallel pipeline, parallel framing.** Mirrors [CL-39](#cl-39) (T-390 home-screen widget appearance audit) but scoped to the lock-screen accessory rendering pipeline. T-370 / [#26](https://github.com/adamsned/dod-ios-app/pull/26) shipped `LatestRecipeLockScreenWidget` with `WidgetCard.LockScreenRectangular` / `LockScreenEmpty` variants (US-22, AC-22.1..AC-22.5). T-390's audit explicitly scoped lock-screen widgets OUT — "lock-screen accessory widgets render in a different system pipeline... and would be a parallel audit if a lock-screen surface ever needs its own verification" (CL-39, last paragraph). **T-396 is that parallel audit.** **Why a separate CL and not an extension of CL-39:** the lock-screen pipeline has its own failure modes that the home-screen Tinted/Clear/Vibrant matrix doesn't surface — (a) the system applies a wallpaper-aware monochrome tint at present-time, not at render-time, so `widgetRenderingMode` environment-injection snapshots prove nothing about lock-screen legibility; (b) the user's Lock Screen wallpaper is any arbitrary photo or system background, so contrast must be evaluated against three reference wallpapers (dark, light, photo) rather than against the four iOS 18 home-screen appearance values; (c) the readable surface area is ~172×76pt rather than the home-screen widget family sizes, so truncation/wrap rules need separate verification; (d) `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(_:)` (T-390's primary lever) is a home-screen-pipeline modifier and has no effect on accessory family widgets — the lock-screen equivalent opt-in is `.widgetAccentable()` and it semantically opts elements *into* the system tint pass rather than *out* of it, which is the inverse of T-390's `.fullColor` opt-out pattern. **What the audit covers:** the five readability dimensions the lock-screen pipeline can break (text truncation behavior under a worst-case 80-character title, body text legibility through the vibrancy pass, empty-state copy legibility, layout hugging in the system-allotted ~172×76pt frame, and any hardcoded `.white` / `.black` / hex colors that would render unpredictably through the system tint). **What CL-47 explicitly authorizes:** a clean audit (no source touches) is an acceptable outcome — mirrors CL-39's "audit may surface zero fixes" framing. The lock-screen widget code shipped on T-370 was designed to the readability constraints from the start (text-only, no glyphs, semantic typography via `.font(.system(.headline, ...))` / `.font(.system(.caption, ...))`, no hardcoded colors, `.lineLimit(2)` on the title with default `.tail` truncation, `.lineLimit(1)` on the excerpt, `.widgetAccentable()` on the entry view) so a clean audit is the expected outcome, not a surprise. **What the audit IS:** a written verification that the existing implementation meets the readability bar against three reference wallpapers (dark, light, photo), plus the targeted-fix authority CL-39 grants — if a surface fails, the fix replaces hardcoded colors with semantic ones (`.primary`, `.secondary`), adds `.widgetAccentable(true)` on key elements that should pick up the system tint, tightens line limits + truncation modes, or adds a contrast scrim. **What the audit is NOT:** a re-architecture of the lock-screen widget, a change to the `WidgetSnapshot` wire format, a change to the `widgetURL` grammar, or a change to the `LatestRecipeLockScreenTimelineProvider` cadence — those are US-22 contract surfaces and out of scope. **Manual verification matters more here than for T-390:** the lock-screen pipeline's wallpaper-aware tinting cannot be snapshot-tested the way the home-screen `widgetRenderingMode` matrix can — the system applies the tint at present-time against the user's actual wallpaper, not at render-time against an injected environment value. So the audit's primary evidence is manual: install the widget on a sim, cycle through 3 reference wallpapers (dark / light / photo), confirm text stays legible in all three. The L4 snapshot baselines (`LockScreenWidgetSnapshotTests` populated / populated-long-title / empty) lock the *layout*; the manual verification locks the *contrast*. **Spec/tasks trace:** amends `AC-22.X` with an explicit contrast-pinning AC if the audit surfaces a need; tracked as T-396 in `tasks.md` Phase 10 cluster alongside T-390 / T-394. **Coordination note:** T-394 and T-395 are parallel widget-surface audits running on different branches at the same time; CL slot collisions on `clarifications.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free slot.

- **CL-45 (REG-T-360, 2026-05-25)** — **Latest Recipe widget hero image: feed-side prefetch is required for the image bridge to populate files.** T-360 / [#24](https://github.com/adamsned/dod-ios-app/pull/24) built `WidgetImageBridge` per CL-35 Option A and wired `RecipeStore.cacheImage(url:bytes:)` to mirror cached bytes into the shared App Group container at a deterministic filename. The featured widget extension reads files by that filename and renders the gradient placeholder when the file is absent. **Root cause:** `cacheImage(...)` is only called from `LiveSavedDependencies.preDownloadImages(forRecipeID:urls:)` (AC-5.2's saved-recipe pre-download). The feed-load path (`FeedViewModel.loadInitial` → `LiveFeedDependencies.publishWidgetSnapshot(items:)`) populates the widget snapshot's `heroImageFilename` strings via `WidgetImageBridge.filename(for:)` but never asks `cacheImage(...)` to write the bytes — the filenames point at files that have never existed. Result: the widget always falls back to the placeholder for newly-installed users who haven't saved any recipes yet. **Fix:** `LiveFeedDependencies.publishWidgetSnapshot(items:)` kicks off a non-blocking `Task { ... }` after the snapshot writes, iterating the same trimmed `WidgetSnapshotConfig.maxEntries` slice and calling `ImageLoader.data(for: url)` + `store.cacheImage(url:bytes:)` for each entry's `heroImage`. The cache call routes through the same path `preDownloadImages` already uses, so the bridge fires for free and the next 15-min timeline refresh (CL-28) picks up the freshly-written files. The prefetch is fire-and-forget — feed-load latency is unaffected, and per-URL failures are logged inside `ImageLoader` / `cacheImage` and swallowed so the snapshot still writes even when one image fails. **Why feed-side prefetch over widget-side or extension-side fetch:** the widget extension is forbidden from network calls (AC-17.6 + constitution §9 implicit), so the bytes must arrive via the host. The host already has `ImageLoader` and `cacheImage` plumbed; the missing piece was the call site, not new infrastructure. **Why fire-and-forget (not awaited in `publishWidgetSnapshot`):** the snapshot write itself is on the feed-load path's critical timing, and the widget's gradient placeholder is the correct fallback for the brief window between the first feed load and the first timeline-refresh-after-prefetch. Blocking the snapshot on N image downloads would slow every feed refresh for a benefit that materializes one timeline tick later anyway. **Diagnostics:** added `DODLog.persistence.debug(...)` inside `cacheImage(...)` and `WidgetImageBridge.writeImage(bytes:for:)` so future regressions of this exact shape (filename in snapshot but no file on disk) surface in `Console.app` instead of requiring a backlog round-trip. Implementing PR: T-362.

- **CL-48 (US-23 / T-394, 2026-05-25)** — **Featured / Latest Recipe widget contrast scrim: replace the existing three-stop bottom-band gradient with a full-height two-stop scrim (`.clear` → `.black.opacity(0.55)`) inside `WidgetCard.Small`'s `ZStack`.** Root cause: `WidgetCard.Small` renders its recipe title via `.foregroundStyle(.white)` (`WidgetCard.swift:81`) inside a `ZStack` over the hero image. The existing bottom-anchored 110pt gradient (three stops, capped at 0.45/0.75 opacity at the bottom band) provides enough contrast in `.fullColor` (Standard / Dark) rendering, but under iOS 18+ `widgetRenderingMode == .accented` (Tinted + Clear) the `containerBackground` is stripped, `.white` collapses into the wallpaper-tinted default group, and the bottom-band gradient flattens to roughly the same wallpaper-tinted plane — the title becomes invisible on light wallpapers (REG-T-390 user report: "white text on a white background is not acceptable"). Fix: replace the three-stop bottom-anchored gradient with a full-height two-stop `LinearGradient(colors: [.black.opacity(0.0), .black.opacity(0.55)], startPoint: .top, endPoint: .bottom)` rendered as the middle layer of the `ZStack` (behind the title `VStack`, in front of the `Hero`). The two-stop full-height shape gives a consistent dark scaffold that scales with the entire card frame (158pt rather than the existing 110pt bottom band), and the `.black.opacity(0.55)` literal at the bottom is the calibrated WCAG-AA-equivalent contrast scaffold CL-39 / AC-23.7 mandates — preserves enough scrim density in `.accented` mode for the wallpaper-tinted `.white` text to read against, without darkening the top of the card so much that `.fullColor` mode loses the hero photo's color identity. **What this CL does NOT do:** does not add `.widgetAccentable(true)` on the title (AC-23.7's part-(b) requirement is intentionally deferred — the scrim alone is the minimal-diff fix the user-reported regression demands; if a follow-up audit determines the wallpaper-tinted title needs an explicit accent-group opt-in too, that's a clean T-39X follow-up under AC-23.7's mandate), does not change `.foregroundStyle(.white)` (keeps the brand title color), does not touch `WidgetCard.Medium` (HStack layout — text renders on `surfaceElevated`, not over the hero, no text-over-image surface to fix), does not touch `WidgetCard.Placeholder` (text on `surfaceElevated`, same situational shape T-394 explicitly carved out), does not touch `WidgetCard.Hero` (T-390's `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.fullColor)` opt-out stays), does not touch `WidgetCard+Saved.swift` (T-395 cleared the saved variants by-construction per CL-46), does not change the `WidgetSnapshot` wire format, does not change the `widgetURL` grammar, does not change the analytics events. **What changes:** one `LinearGradient` block inside `WidgetCard.Small.body` is replaced — the diff is bounded to ~10 lines. The 12 `WidgetCardTintedAppearanceSnapshotTests` baselines that T-390 added are re-recorded as part of the PR (the bottom band's shape changes for every populated Small surface in `.accented` + `.vibrant` modes; the surrounding Saved and placeholder baselines pass byte-identical because the source change is bounded to `WidgetCard.Small`). The Standard light + Standard dark baselines (`SnapshotTests.test_widgetCard_*`, `SnapshotTests+AppearanceAudit.test_widgetCard_*`) are byte-identical — the full-height scrim renders with the same final pixel at the title's anchor in Standard mode because the `.black.opacity(0.55)` at the bottom matches the previous `.black.opacity(0.75)` middle-stop area within sub-pixel JPEG-noise tolerance; if any of the existing Standard baselines drift more than the snapshot harness's 0.0001 perceptual tolerance, they're re-recorded too and the diff is noted in the PR body. **Considered:** keep the existing three-stop bottom-anchored gradient and *also* add the full-height two-stop scrim on top of it (defense-in-depth). Rejected — the existing three-stop gradient's purpose was the same contrast scaffold the new scrim provides; stacking two gradients with overlapping intent invites future-engineer confusion ("why are there two scrims?") and adds a `.black` color literal that the `.accented` pipeline has to flatten. The minimal-diff principle the prompt mandates is one scrim, calibrated correctly. **Considered:** keep `.foregroundStyle(.white)` AND add `.widgetAccentable(true)` per AC-23.7's two-part requirement (b), so the title splits into the accent group while the scrim stays in the default group, giving the system two color planes to work with (tint-color title over dark scrim). Rejected as out-of-scope for the minimal-diff fix — the `.widgetAccentable` opt-in is the AC-23.7-compliant version of this fix and is the right call for a v2 polish pass, but the immediate user-reported regression is "invisible text on light wallpaper" and the scrim alone solves the visibility floor by giving the `.white` title a dark band to read against regardless of which color group the system flattens it into. The accent-group opt-in is the right follow-up if user testing surfaces the wallpaper-tinted title is still hard to read against extreme-bright wallpapers (e.g. pure white) — that's a clean T-39X entry under AC-23.7's mandate. **Considered:** convert `.foregroundStyle(.white)` to `.foregroundStyle(.primary)` so the system handles the foreground color natively in `.accented` (would render in the user's tint color rather than wallpaper-tinted white). Rejected — `.primary` in widget contexts maps to the system label color, which in `.fullColor` (Standard) mode would render as the system label-on-image (black or near-black depending on the wallpaper appearance), losing the brand "white title over recipe photo" visual identity the design intentionally pinned. The scrim approach preserves the white-title identity in `.fullColor` (the dominant rendering mode for most users) and provides a contrast floor in `.accented`. **Spec/trace:** CL-39 (US-23 audit framing); AC-23.7 (Featured-widget pin, two-part requirement, this CL addresses part-(a) only and explicitly defers part-(b) to a follow-up); CL-46 (T-395 saved-widget sibling-audit verdict, clean); REG-T-390 (the user-reported regression that surfaced the bug). Implementing PR: T-394 (this branch, `fix/T-394-widget-text-contrast-v2` — sibling to `fix/T-394-widget-text-contrast` which is the parallel-agent v1; merge-time collision on the CL-48 slot is the documented resolution path).

- **CL-51 (US-30 / T-520, 2026-05-26)** — **App-wide background + foreground color overhaul: token mapping + WCAG AA verification.** Backlog round-6 item 9 specified an app-wide background + foreground re-tint by literal hex values: dark mode background `#42210B`, dark mode foreground (cards / surfaces / chips) `#281F19`, light mode background `#F9F6EF`, light mode foreground (cards / surfaces / chips) `#FFFFFF`, with text colors explicitly unchanged. The existing `DODColor` palette in `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Colors.swift` has no token literally named "background" or "foreground", so this CL captures the mapping decision the agent must make. **Decision: `Surface` → background, `SurfaceElevated` → foreground.** **Why:** the `Colors.swift` doc-comments already pin these semantic roles. `Surface` is documented as "Default screen background" (line 11 — the screen-wide backdrop, what `RootView` / feed / search / saved / categories surfaces all paint behind their content) and `SurfaceElevated` is documented as "Card / sheet surface above ``surface``" (line 13 — the recipe card, snackbar, sheet, modal, and chip surface that sits visually above the screen background). The user's "background" / "foreground" intent maps cleanly: screen-wide backdrop = `Surface`; cards / surfaces / chips above it = `SurfaceElevated`. **Why NOT touch `Cream` even though its name suggests a background role:** in this codebase `Cream` is the foreground TEXT color used on dark brand surfaces — `RecipeCard` time chip text (line 100, `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.cream)` on a `CastIronBrown` chip), `Snackbar` text (line 35), `OfflineBanner` text (line 30), `CookNowCTA` button label (line 26), `WidgetCard` rendered title (line 274), `GuestIdentitySheet` (line 123), `OnboardingSheet` (line 106), `CookModeView` (line 150), `InstructionStep` (line 18), `ModerationBadge.posted/.failed` foreground (line 57), and `SearchView`'s active filter chip label (line 183, `isOn ? DODColor.cream : DODColor.label`). Re-tinting `Cream` toward the new background palette would invert every one of those text-on-brand-dark surfaces and break legibility everywhere `CastIronBrown` / `BurntOrange` / `WarmGold` show through. `CreamSubtle` is unused in production source (only referenced by `ColorsTests`), so leaving it alone is a no-op. **Why NOT introduce a new `DODColor.background` token:** the existing `surface` already plays that role per its doc-comment and per its actual call-site usage (every screen's `.background(DODColor.surface)` modifier is the screen-wide backdrop). Adding a `background` synonym alongside `surface` would force every call site that today reads `surface` to be triaged ("is this a backdrop or a card?") for no semantic gain, would require re-recording every snapshot baseline that uses either token (which is the same baseline churn as just re-tinting the existing tokens), and would risk a future contributor reaching for the wrong one. The cleanest path is to re-tint the two existing tokens to the requested hex values in-place. **Mapping summary:** `Surface.colorset` → light `#F9F6EF`, dark `#42210B`; `SurfaceElevated.colorset` → light `#FFFFFF` (was already `#FFFFFF` — byte-identical pre/post for light-mode Any-Appearance), dark `#281F19` (was `#2A1F18` — a 2-bit-per-channel shift toward the user's exact hex). **Tokens explicitly NOT touched per AC-30.3:** `Label.colorset` (`#2C2C2C` light / `#E6DECF` dark — primary body text), `LabelSecondary.colorset` (`#6B6B6B` light / `#A8A39A` dark — supporting text), `Charcoal.colorset`, `Accent.colorset`, `CastIronBrown.colorset`, `BurntOrange.colorset`, `WarmGold.colorset`, `DarkEarth.colorset`, `Cream.colorset`, `CreamSubtle.colorset` — all byte-identical pre- and post-T-520. The `Colors.swift` enum itself is unchanged (no token additions, no renames, no `bundleColor` call-site edits). **WCAG AA contrast verification against unchanged text tokens** (per AC-30.4): contrast ratios computed with the WCAG 2.1 relative-luminance formula (sRGB linearization → weighted sum → `(L1+0.05)/(L2+0.05)`):
  - **Dark mode, Label (`#E6DECF`) on the new Surface dark (`#42210B`):** lum(Label dark) ≈ 0.7358, lum(`#42210B`) ≈ 0.02251, ratio ≈ **10.84:1** (well above the 4.5:1 AA body-text floor; comfortably above the 7:1 AAA floor).
  - **Dark mode, Label (`#E6DECF`) on the new SurfaceElevated dark (`#281F19`):** lum(`#281F19`) ≈ 0.01471, ratio ≈ **12.14:1** (≥ AA + AAA).
  - **Dark mode, LabelSecondary (`#A8A39A`) on Surface dark (`#42210B`):** lum(LabelSecondary dark) ≈ 0.3683, ratio ≈ **5.77:1** (above the 4.5:1 AA body-text floor; just below the 7:1 AAA floor — but the body-text AA floor is what the constitution §7 / `accessibility-audit.md` mandate and that's well met).
  - **Dark mode, LabelSecondary (`#A8A39A`) on SurfaceElevated dark (`#281F19`):** ratio ≈ **6.46:1** (above AA).
  - **Light mode, Label (`#2C2C2C`) on Surface light (`#F9F6EF`):** lum(Label light) ≈ 0.02351, lum(`#F9F6EF`) ≈ 0.9176, ratio ≈ **12.79:1** (≥ AA + AAA).
  - **Light mode, Label (`#2C2C2C`) on SurfaceElevated light (`#FFFFFF`):** ratio ≈ **13.5:1** (≥ AA + AAA — pure white background gives the maximum possible contrast for this label).
  - **Light mode, LabelSecondary (`#6B6B6B`) on Surface light (`#F9F6EF`):** lum(LabelSecondary light) ≈ 0.1542, ratio ≈ **4.74:1** (above AA — same comfortable margin the pre-T-520 `#FAF6EE` background gave).
  - **Light mode, LabelSecondary (`#6B6B6B`) on SurfaceElevated light (`#FFFFFF`):** ratio ≈ **5.74:1** (above AA).
  
  All four directions pass WCAG AA against unchanged text tokens; no deviation from the user-specified hex values is needed. `accessibility-audit.md` is updated in the same spec commit to reflect the new Surface / SurfaceElevated hex values in the contrast table (the pre-T-520 row "Label on Surface | #2C2C2C on #FAF6EE | #E6DECF on #1B140E" is split into the Surface + SurfaceElevated pair with the new hex values). **What CL-51 explicitly authorizes:** asset-catalog `Contents.json` edits to `Surface.colorset` and `SurfaceElevated.colorset` only; re-recording every L4 snapshot baseline whose rendered surface includes either token; the `accessibility-audit.md` contrast table update. **What CL-51 explicitly does NOT authorize:** any `Colors.swift` source edit, any `DODColor` call-site rewrite, any token rename / addition / removal, any touch of `Label.colorset` / `LabelSecondary.colorset` / `Charcoal.colorset` / `Accent.colorset` / `CastIronBrown.colorset` / `BurntOrange.colorset` / `WarmGold.colorset` / `DarkEarth.colorset` / `Cream.colorset` / `CreamSubtle.colorset`, any snapshot test source edit (only `__Snapshots__/**/*.png` re-records). **Re-recorded baseline scope:** every `XCTest` snapshot that renders a view containing `DODColor.surface` or `DODColor.surfaceElevated` is re-recorded — that's the bulk of `DODDesignSystemTests/__Snapshots__/**`, the feature snapshot tests under `DODFeatureCategoriesTests`, `DODFeatureRecipeDetailTests`, `DODFeatureSearchTests`, and the widget tests under `WidgetCardTintedAppearanceSnapshotTests` + `SavedWidgetSnapshotTests` + the standard widget surfaces. **Out of scope for re-record:** the lock-screen widget baselines (`LockScreenWidgetSnapshotTests/test_lockScreenWidget_rectangular_*`) — the lock-screen pipeline paints with `Color.clear` as `containerBackground` and defers to the system's `AccessoryWidgetBackground` (per T-396 / CL-47), so the re-tinted Surface / SurfaceElevated tokens are not rendered on those surfaces. Same for any icon-only or text-only baseline that does not paint a surface token. **Considered:** keep both `Cream` AND `Surface` as "background-like" tokens and re-tint both in parallel. Rejected — `Cream` is a foreground text color in this codebase, not a background; the audit of `grep -rn 'DODColor.cream\b'` shows 13 production call sites and every single one is `.foregroundStyle(DODColor.cream)` (text on a brand-colored backdrop), never `.background(DODColor.cream)`. Re-tinting `Cream` would invert every one of those overlays. **Considered:** introduce a slightly-lighter dark-mode foreground (e.g. `#332620` instead of the user-specified `#281F19`) for contrast headroom on the LabelSecondary token. Rejected — the verification above shows `#281F19` already clears AA with a 6.46:1 ratio (well above the 4.5:1 floor); deviating from the user's exact hex without contrast pressure would be a needless paraphrase of a precise design ask. **Considered:** also re-tint `CreamSubtle` since its name suggests it pairs with `Surface`.
