Rejected — `CreamSubtle` has zero production call sites (only `ColorsTests.swift` enumerates it for the bundle-loading smoke test), so re-tinting it is a no-op visible only in the test enumeration; touching it would be churn for nothing. **Implementing PR:** T-520. **Coordination note:** T-500 (Search polish) and T-510 (new-recipe fix) run on parallel branches and may also re-record some Search-tab snapshot baselines. The color overhaul has wider scope, so when the PRs collide on `__Snapshots__/**/*.png` paths, T-520's color-tinted versions win because they're the source of truth for the new surface tokens.

- **CL-52 (US-31 / T-440, 2026-05-26)** — **Recipe scaling: fraction-rendering precision (eighth-cup canonical set, 1/16 snap tolerance), warning threshold rationale (>12 servings on a 5-quart home dutch oven), and the `FractionRenderer`-in-`DODSupport` decision (two consumers: recipe detail view + Cook Mode drawer).** Round-3 backlog "Recipe scaling" entry (dad's idea, sized S) calls for tapping a "Serves N" stepper and watching ingredient quantities re-render with cook-friendly fractions. This CL captures three decisions the implementer must lock before the source change lands.

  **Decision 1: Canonical fraction set = `{1/8, 1/4, 1/3, 1/2, 2/3, 3/4, 7/8}` + whole numbers.** **Why these seven and not others:** these are the cook-grade fractions every measuring spoon + measuring cup set in a US kitchen actually carries. A 1/8 tsp + 1/4 tsp + 1/2 tsp + 1 tsp set covers spoon-level dosing; a 1/4 cup + 1/3 cup + 1/2 cup + 1 cup set covers cup-level. The 2/3 cup and 3/4 cup are the only "between" cup measures that show up on standard sets (e.g. Pyrex, OXO, KitchenAid). 7/8 cup is a stretch — most sets don't carry it — but it's included because the snap distance from 3/4 cup (0.75) to a full cup (1.0) is large enough (0.25) that a scaled-up 7/8 result wants a closer rendering than "0.88 cup". 5/8 and 3/8 are NOT in the set because they would crowd 1/2 and 2/3, which are the dominant "middle" measures — keeping fewer canonical fractions means more values snap to common spoon/cup measures rather than the kitchen-junk-drawer ones. **Why integers as a separate render branch:** "2 cups" vs "2 0/8 cups" (or worse, "2.0 cups") — the integer-only render is the cook's expected default. The `renderQuantity(_:)` function checks `whole + remainder` separately and never emits an "X 0/Y" string.
  
  **Decision 2: Snap tolerance = 1/16 (= 0.0625).** **Why 1/16:** that's the half-distance between successive 1/8 increments. A remainder within 1/16 of a canonical value snaps to it; anything outside falls into the next-nearest canonical band. The math: `0` ↔ `1/8` mid = 0.0625, `1/8` ↔ `1/4` mid = 0.1875, `1/4` ↔ `1/3` mid = 0.2917, `1/3` ↔ `1/2` mid = 0.4167, `1/2` ↔ `2/3` mid = 0.5833, `2/3` ↔ `3/4` mid = 0.7083, `3/4` ↔ `7/8` mid = 0.8125, `7/8` ↔ `1` mid = 0.9375. Within those bands, every value snaps to the closest canonical. The mid-distance bands are uneven (1/3 sits closer to 1/4 than to 1/2) — which is the whole point: a `0.34` remainder snaps to `1/3` rather than `1/2` because 1/3 is its physical neighbor on the measuring-spoon side. **Why not 1/32:** would force more values to fall into the decimal-fallback branch, which renders as e.g. `"0.13"` — readable, but not what a cook expects. 1/16 is the largest tolerance that doesn't merge two adjacent canonical fractions into one band. **Why not adaptive per-fraction tolerance:** the canonical set is dense enough that a flat 1/16 tolerance keeps the mid-distance math symmetric; adaptive tolerance would invite "this should round to X but I expected Y" arguments that the spec can't pre-empt.
  
  **Decision 3: `FractionRenderer` lives in `Packages/DODSupport`, not in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail`.** **Why:** Cook Mode's ingredients drawer (`CookModeView.ingredientsDrawer`, declared in `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/CookModeView.swift`) renders the same ingredient rows the detail screen does. If the user scales to 8 servings on the detail screen and then taps "Cook Now," the drawer must show the same scaled quantities — otherwise the user gets two different ingredient lists for the same cook. The renderer needs to be importable from both surfaces. Putting it in `DODSupport` (the existing home for `StepTimerParser`, `HTMLSanitizer`, `WidgetImageBridge` — pure utility code) means both view layers consume one canonical fraction table. The L1 test suite for the renderer lives in `Packages/DODSupport/Tests/DODSupportTests/FractionRendererTests.swift` per the constitution §6 L1 mandate ("every view model, networking type, and domain transform" gets a unit test home). **Why not embed the renderer in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail`:** would force `CookModeView` to import it via the same module (it does), but it would also force any future consumer (a hypothetical share-sheet export, a future widget surface that wants to render scaled quantities, a future Spotlight indexer) to take a dependency on the feature package — which violates the constitutional dependency direction (features depend on support, never the reverse).
  
  **Decision 4: Warning threshold = 12 servings on a 5-quart home dutch oven.** **Why 12 and not 8 or 16:** five-quart is the most common home-cook size on Lodge's and Le Creuset's catalogs (the brand's "Dutch Oven Daddy" identity already orbits Lodge cast iron at this size). A typical stew, chili, or braise renders at roughly 1.5 cups per serving = 0.4 quarts per serving. A 5-quart pot at 80% fill (the practical max — anything more risks overflow during simmer) = 4 quarts of usable volume = ~10 servings. 12 is the just-past-the-comfortable-cap number that warrants a "you should consider two batches" caption. The threshold is a soft signal — the math at 13 servings still works, it's just that the cook should know they're scaling past what their pot can physically hold. **Why a caption and not a confirmation alert:** the user knows their kitchen; the app shouldn't gate. A non-blocking caption ("Most home dutch ovens cap out around 12 servings. Consider doubling the recipe in two batches instead.") with an `exclamationmark.triangle.fill` glyph in `DODColor.burntOrange` conveys the constraint without disabling the stepper. **Threshold encapsulation:** the constant `FractionRenderer.dutchOvenServingWarningThreshold = 12` and the helper `FractionRenderer.shouldShowDutchOvenWarning(forServings:)` live in `DODSupport` so the view-model gate and the view's render condition use the same number — future tuning lives in one place. **Why not threshold by recipe category:** the spec doesn't carry pot-size metadata per recipe; a chili and a stew both nominally cook in a 5-quart, but the densities differ. The 12-serving threshold is a generic warning that triggers for *every* recipe — false-positive-friendly is the correct bias because the caption is informational, not blocking.
  
  **Decision 5: Source `Recipe` is never mutated; scaling is pure presentation.** **Why:** the source recipe is the contract with the JSON-LD parse (per AC-4.11). Mutating `Recipe.servings` or `RecipeIngredient.text` would taint the cache (`RecipeStore.cache(detail:)`), the widget snapshot (the saved-widget publisher reads `Recipe.title` and could read `servings` if a future widget surface wanted it), and the Cook Mode drawer (which takes a `Recipe` directly, not a scaled view-model projection). The view layer multiplies at render time inside `RecipeDetailView.ingredientsSection`'s `ForEach` body via `FractionRenderer.scale(ingredient.text, by: viewModel.servingsScaleFactor)` and passes the result as `IngredientCheckRow.displayText`; `ingredient.text` (the source string) is left untouched, and `IngredientCheckRow.accessibilityLabel` reads from `displayText` so VoiceOver announces the scaled quantity. **Why not store the scale on `Recipe` as a transient field:** the `@Model` `CachedRecipe` SwiftData type would need a new column, and the in-memory `Recipe` value type would need a property that's nil for every code path except the recipe-detail render — adds shape complexity for what's already cleanly solved by a view-model `userServings` integer and a derived `servingsScaleFactor` Double. **Why not persist `userServings` across app launches:** out of scope for v1. The backlog entry doesn't ask for it ("pure presentation logic; no schema change since the source recipe is untouched"). If a v1.x user-test session surfaces a "I scale every batch to 8" pattern, that's a clean follow-up that adds a `lastScaledServings` `UserDefaults` key keyed by recipe id — but the v1 path stays scale-resets-on-screen-exit.
  
  **Decision 6: Stepper range = 1...24.** **Why upper bound 24:** the warning threshold is 12. The stepper needs to reach past it so the warning gets exercised (you can't see the caption if you can't get to 13). Doubling the warning threshold (24) gives the user enough headroom to scale a 4-serving recipe up to 6× (24 / 4) — which is the upper end of "I'm hosting a small dinner party" usage. Higher than 24 is the "doing a catering job" tier that doesn't fit the single-stepper UI anyway (the cook is doing more spreadsheet math than recipe-app math). **Why lower bound 1:** a single serving is the floor. Zero servings is meaningless (and would force every quantity to zero, an unproductive output); negative servings are obviously invalid. Both ends are clamped at the view-model layer via `setUserServings(_:)` so any programmatic set is correct by construction. **Why a stepper and not a slider:** integer values, the user needs to land on exact counts (you don't host a party for "between 6 and 7 people"), and the stepper's `−` / `+` controls map directly to the "I want one more / one fewer" intent. A slider would invite imprecision and require a label readout that's effectively the same as the stepper's current value display anyway.
  
  **Decision 7: Ingredient parser walks the head of `RecipeIngredient.text`; no structured amount/unit decomposition.** **Why:** the JSON-LD `recipeIngredient` payload from WPRM is a single string per ingredient with quantity + unit + name interleaved (`"720 g bread flour"`, `"1 1/2 pounds cod fillets (cut into 2-3 inch uniform chunks)"`, `"Salt and freshly ground black pepper (to taste)"`, `"melted butter (for dipping)"`). There is no structured `amount` + `unit` + `name` decomposition to lean on — that's a recipe-data-source contract decision baked into CL-1 (JSON-LD parse from the rendered post page, not a structured WP REST endpoint). The renderer walks the head of the string looking for, in precedence order: a mixed number (`"1 1/2"` or `"2 ½"`), a decimal (`"1.5"`), a fraction (Unicode `"½"` or ASCII `"1/2"`), or a plain integer (`"4"`). The first match wins; everything from the first non-quantity character to the end is appended verbatim to the rendered quantity (with a single normalizing space between). Lines that don't begin with a digit or a Unicode vulgar fraction return verbatim — the renderer never invents a quantity, so `"Salt and freshly ground black pepper (to taste)"` stays correct at any scale. **Why not parse units too (e.g. "cup" / "tablespoon"):** unit normalization is a separate problem (gram-to-cup conversion, oz-to-tbsp conversion, plural vs singular agreement) that the spec doesn't tackle. The renderer rewrites the quantity; the unit string travels untouched. **Why not handle "and" glue ("1 cup and 2 tablespoons"):** the leading-quantity-only parse is sufficient for the common case — multi-quantity ingredients are rare in WPRM-published recipes (the canonical pattern is one quantity per line). If user testing surfaces a pattern of multi-quantity lines that scale wrong, that's a clean follow-up — but the v1 spec doesn't carry it.
  
  **What CL-52 ADDS to the L1 test suite:** `FractionRendererTests` with 26 named test cases in `Packages/DODSupport/Tests/DODSupportTests/FractionRendererTests.swift` covering the four backlog-quote load-bearing examples (`½ × 1.5 → ¾`, `1 × 2 → 2`, `2 ½ × 2 → 5`, `1 ⅓ × 3 → 4`), the decimal-source snap (`1.5 × 2 → 3`), the warning threshold boundary (≤12 doesn't trigger, ≥13 does), the quantity-less-line pass-through, the factor-of-1 short-circuit, the integer/fraction/decimal/mixed parse-precedence cases, and the `renderQuantity(_:)` table directly. **What CL-52 ADDS to the L4 baselines:** `RecipeServingsScalerSnapshotTests` with 6 baselines (3 scale points × 2 modes: default = 4 servings, scaled-up = 8 servings, warning-threshold = 16 servings; each in light + dark per the constitution §6 L4 mandate). **What CL-52 does NOT touch:** no `RecipeIngredient` schema change, no `Recipe.servings` schema change, no `RecipeStore` change, no `WidgetSnapshot` change, no new analytics event, no new deep-link case, no `DODColor` token addition. **Considered:** scale per ingredient (per-row `Stepper`) instead of per recipe (top-of-screen `Stepper`). Rejected — every ingredient in a recipe scales by the same factor by definition; per-ingredient steppers would invite a non-monotonic "salt should be doubled but flour shouldn't" failure mode that the cook has to manage manually. The single top-of-screen stepper is the correct abstraction. **Considered:** persist `userServings` per recipe in `UserDefaults` so the scale carries across screen visits. Rejected for v1 — the backlog entry explicitly says "pure presentation logic; no schema change." A v1.x follow-up can add a `lastScaledServings` key keyed by `recipeID` if user testing shows a habitual-scale pattern. **Considered:** snap to 1/4-cup precision only (`{1/4, 1/2, 3/4}` + whole) instead of eighth-cup. Rejected — the 1/3 + 2/3 cup measures are common enough on standard cup sets that omitting them would produce "snap up to 3/4 or down to 1/2" decisions that don't match what the cook actually owns. The eighth-cup set is the lowest-common-denominator US-kitchen vocabulary. **Considered:** localize the warning copy + threshold for non-US users (different metric pot sizes, different serving-density conventions). Rejected for v1 — the audience is US users on a US-recipe blog (dutchovendaddy.com is US-only in v1 per the existing site setup). Internationalization is a v2 conversation. **Considered:** render the warning as a modal alert instead of an inline caption. Rejected — the stepper is a quick-tap surface; gating it behind a sheet would break the "tap to scale" loop the user expects. The caption is non-blocking and self-dismissing once the user steps back below 13. **Implementing PR:** T-440 (this branch, `feat/T-440-recipe-scaling`). **Coordination note:** T-530 (Any-time filter composition) runs on a parallel branch and touches Search code (`SearchResultMerger` / `WPRestClient` query construction); the two PRs touch completely independent code paths (Search vs Recipe Detail) and there are no expected source-file collisions. Spec-file conflicts at merge time (both branches add a new CL entry and a new US entry) are normal and resolved by whoever lands second renumbering — the prompt for this task fixed CL-52 / US-31 / T-440 for this branch's spec commit.

- **CL-49 (US-29 / T-500, 2026-05-26)** — **Search-tab polish bundle: five small fixes in one PR, including the AC-20.3 reversal (no-results glyph swap), the "tag search no-results" root cause (it isn't a tag-search feature; the misread came from the "Try" pill double-setting a filter), the "Recent" Clear-All affordance, the "All categories" menu-row removal, and the "Try" pill action change.** Bundles five round-6 backlog items into a single PR because each touches `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchView.swift` (or its `RecentSearches` helper) and the snapshot baseline churn is shared. Splitting would re-record the same `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` artifacts up to five times and force the user into N parallel review queues for what reads as one coherent Search-tab pass.

  **CL-49.1 — "Tag search returns 'No recipes match'": the root cause is not a missing tag-search feature.** The round-6 backlog entry reported "When the user searches via a tag chip, the result is a 'no recipes match' empty state instead of actual filtered results. The tag-search path is broken." Investigation surfaced a different shape than the report described:
  - **There is no tag-search feature in the code.** A repo-wide `rg -n 'tag' Packages/DODFeatureSearch/` returns five hits: two `tag.fill` SF Symbol references in `SearchView.swift` (one on `FilterChipRow.categoryChip`, one on `IdleSuggestionsView`'s "Try" pill — both for the *category* taxonomy, not a tag taxonomy), one in `SearchFilters.swift`'s doc-comment (referring to "category-tagged items" in the merger contract), and two in `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests.swift`'s doc comments about the `tag.fill` glyph T-350 introduced. None of these are a tag-search feature. The WP REST data source surfaces only *categories* in v1 per CL-3 ("WP categories only in v1. Sample post showed 2 categories, 0 tags. Tags are not populated on this site. Revisit if blog adds tag usage post-launch.") — the codebase contains zero references to a `tag` taxonomy outside of the `tag.fill` SF Symbol used for category chips.
  - **What the user actually tapped was a "Try" category pill.** The pre-T-500 `IdleSuggestionsView.onCategoryTap(...)` action handler executed both `viewModel.filters.categoryID = category.id` AND `viewModel.query = category.name`. The pill *visually* looks like a tag chip (it renders the `tag.fill` glyph per T-350 / AC-20.2), so a user reasonably reads it as "tap to search by this tag." But the action under the hood was different.
  - **Why the user saw "no recipes match":** the category-filter chain (`SearchFilters.apply(to:categoryIDsByRecipe:totalSecondsByRecipe:recentlyViewedIDs:)`) requires `categoryIDsByRecipe[item.id]?.contains(categoryID) == true` per `SearchFilters.swift:55-58`. The `categoryIDsByRecipe` map is hydrated *after* the REST search completes via `dependencies.categoryIDs(forRecipeIDs: allIDs)` in `SearchViewModel.performSearch()`. For freshly-fetched REST results whose recipe IDs are not yet in the **local recipe-detail cache**, `categoryIDsByRecipe` returns an empty/nil entry for that ID, and `nil?.contains(...)` evaluates `false` — so the filter drops every REST result whose detail page hasn't been opened before. On a fresh install (or a returning user whose cache has been evicted), this produces the smoking gun: 0 results, "No recipes match '\(viewModel.query)' — Try a different word or clear a filter." The user reads this as "tag search is broken." The actual failure is a filter-vs-cache race that only manifests because the pill double-set a filter the user never asked for.
  - **The fix is AC-29.1's action-handler change.** The "Try" pill stops setting `filters.categoryID`. Tapping "Beef" now sets the search text to "Beef" and runs a normal REST text query, the same as if the user had typed the word. The category filter chip remains at "All categories" and the result set is the unfiltered REST response. The category-vs-cache race is bypassed entirely. **There is no tag feature to remove** — `tag.fill` is just an SF Symbol, used here for category chips, and the round-6 entry's wording ("the tag-search path") was the user's reasonable misread of a chip that *looks* like a tag chip but acts like a category filter setter.
  - **Considered: remove the "Try" pills entirely.** Rejected — the pills are useful as idle-state discovery (US-12 / AC-12.4 still wants them) and the "Try" framing reads naturally when the action is "populate the search field." Removing them would lose the affordance the user explicitly likes; changing the action keeps the affordance and fixes the bug. **Considered: keep the filter behavior but pre-hydrate `categoryIDsByRecipe` for the active query's REST results before applying filters.** Rejected — this is the right architecture for *user-driven* filter chip toggles after results land (where the user explicitly opts into a filter knowing the result set is the current REST page), but for the "Try" pill the user's mental model is "search for this word" not "filter by this taxonomy." Changing the action matches the mental model; changing the architecture to support a filter the user didn't ask for is over-engineering. **Considered: pre-cache every WP category's recipe IDs at app launch so the filter pass has the data on a cold cache.** Rejected — that adds a network round-trip on launch for a feature that's better solved by changing the click action.

  **CL-49.2 — `RecentSearchesView` "Clear All" button: trailing edge of the section header.** AC-29.2 adds a "Clear All" button at the trailing edge of the "Recent" section header in `IdleSuggestionsView`. The button calls `viewModel.clearRecentSearches()`, a new public method on `SearchViewModel` that wraps `RecentSearches.clear()` (the existing method per `RecentSearches.swift:53-55`) and resets `recentSearches` to an empty array so the view's `@Observable` redraw fires. **Why a new `SearchViewModel` method rather than calling `RecentSearches.clear()` directly from the view:** the view-model owns the `recentSearches` published state and is the only path that updates it; routing through the VM keeps the @Observable refresh contract uniform with every other recents-related mutation (the existing `performSearch` → `recents.record(trimmed)` → `recentSearches = recents.recent()` path follows the same shape). Having the view reach into `recents` directly would split the source-of-truth contract across two writers. **Why a button next to the section header rather than a swipe-to-delete on each pill:** swipe-to-delete is a UITableView idiom that doesn't map cleanly to a `FlowLayout` of capsule pills, and the user asked specifically for "Clear All next to Recent" — i.e. the bulk action, not per-row deletion. **Accessibility label:** `"Clear all recent searches"` per the iOS-stock pattern for destructive trailing-edge buttons (Mail's "Clear" in the Junk folder header, Photos' "Clear All" in the Recently Deleted album header). **Considered: confirmation dialog before wiping.** Rejected — recents are device-local UX-quality state (per `RecentSearches.swift`'s "what is essentially a UX-quality cache that survives reinstall-loss with no impact" doc comment); the user re-populates them by searching again, so an alert tax is over-engineering for the recovery cost. **Considered: only show "Clear All" when there are 3+ recents.** Rejected — when there's only one, the user might still want it gone, and the button rendering cost is negligible; the conditional was the kind of cleverness that ends up surprising users ("why did the button disappear?"). The button renders whenever the Recent section is rendered (i.e. whenever recents is non-empty), per AC-29.2.

  **CL-49.3 — `questionmark.folder` → `questionmark.circle` for the `.noResults` empty state: explicit AC-20.3 reversal.** T-350 / AC-20.3 (2026-05-24) explicitly carved out the `questionmark.folder` empty-state glyph from the `folder` → `tag.fill` swap, on the rationale that "the `?` carries the meaning, not the folder." The round-6 user feedback contradicts that rationale: users in practice read `questionmark.folder` as "this is in some folder I haven't found" rather than as "this isn't anywhere." `questionmark.circle` reads more cleanly as "not found, period" — it's the glyph Settings' search, Mail's search, and Notes' search all use for "no matches." The swap is one SF Symbol string literal on `SearchView.swift` line ~74. **Why reverse the carve-out rather than leave it for a future polish round:** the bundle PR already touches this exact view (the four other items in CL-49); the glyph swap is one-line, the cost is zero, and the user explicitly flagged it. **Considered: `magnifyingglass` over `questionmark.circle`.** Rejected — `magnifyingglass` is the glyph for the *idle* "type to search" state (already used at `SearchView.swift:32` in the search field's leading icon and at the IdleSuggestionsView fallback empty state at `SearchView.swift:215`), and reusing it for the no-results state would conflate two semantically different states ("waiting for input" vs "got input, found nothing"). **Considered: `magnifyingglass.circle.fill` (the iOS-stock "spotlight no-results" glyph in some Apple apps).** Rejected — the `.circle.fill` variant reads as a button affordance (Mail uses it on its compose button); pairing it with the empty-state title would be a glyph mixed-signal. `questionmark.circle` (the outlined variant) is the closest iOS-stock match for "this query returned nothing." **Considered: keep `questionmark.folder` because AC-20.3 explicitly carved it out.** Rejected per the round-6 user feedback above; AC-20.3's carve-out was a reasonable call at the time (it bounded T-350's diff) but the assumption it made about user reading turned out to be wrong, and the cost of reversing is one-line.

  **CL-49.4 — "All categories" menu-row removal: the chip's label-via-`selectedCategoryName` is preserved.** AC-29.4 removes the `Button("All categories") { filters.categoryID = nil }` first row inside `FilterChipRow.categoryChip`'s `Menu` content. The user clears the category filter by either (a) tapping the currently-selected category in the menu to deselect it, or (b) navigating to the Categories tab via the bottom-nav (which is the actual "browse all categories" affordance — having "All categories" as a menu item *inside* Search was a redundant jump that didn't earn its space). **Why not remove the entire category chip:** the chip is the primary US-12 filter affordance and still has the right behavior once the user has applied a category — re-tapping the same category clears it. The menu just lost its first-row redundancy. **Why preserve the chip's `selectedCategoryName` "All categories" text on the chip itself:** the chip's label is self-describing; when no category is picked the chip should read "All categories" (the default state) so the user knows what it does. That label is the chip's *display* — it's not a tappable menu row. The deleted first row was the *menu's* "All categories" — a separate render path inside `Menu { ... }`'s content that pre-T-500 listed "All categories" before iterating the category list. **Considered: remove the entire category chip from the Search tab.** Rejected — that's a US-12 / AC-12.2 contract change (the filter chip row is a documented surface) and the user only flagged the redundant menu row, not the chip itself. The category chip stays; only the redundant menu row inside it is deleted. **Considered: replace the "All categories" first menu row with a "Browse all in Categories tab" button that deep-links to the Categories tab.** Rejected — that introduces a new affordance the user didn't ask for, and the bottom-nav already covers it. The "no-op" outcome (re-tap to deselect, or use the bottom-nav for browsing) is the right minimum.

  **CL-49.5 — "Try" suggestions populate the search text: same root cause as CL-49.1, same fix.** AC-29.1's action change ("Try" pill → set search text, not filter) is both the fix for item 5 of the round-6 bundle AND the fix for item 1 (the "tag search no-results" bug per CL-49.1). They're the same call site (`IdleSuggestionsView`'s `onCategoryTap` action handler) and the same code change. The two backlog items reading as separate is a UX outcome — the user's mental model of "Try should fill the search bar" is item 5; the user's mental model of "tag search shouldn't return no-results" is item 1. Both resolve to a single one-line action-handler change in `SearchView.swift`'s `IdleSuggestionsView` call site (the `case .idle:` branch's `onCategoryTap:` closure). **Why the search runs after setting the text:** the existing `SearchViewModel.query` `didSet` calls `scheduleSearch()` (per `SearchViewModel.swift:24-26`), which kicks off the same 300ms debounce path typing uses. Setting `viewModel.query = category.name` triggers the search just like typing would. **Why use the existing `query` setter rather than calling a new `setSearchText(_:)` method:** the setter already does the right thing; adding a new public method would be over-engineering for the same outcome. The AC-29.1 wording allows either path — `setSearchText` or direct setter — because the existing `selectRecent(_:)` method on the view-model already follows this pattern (sets `self.query = query` directly per `SearchViewModel.swift:88`); using the same pattern for `onCategoryTap` keeps the call-site shape uniform.

  **What CL-49 does NOT touch:** the `SearchResultMerger` ranking logic (no change to title/excerpt/local-tier ordering or dedupe rules), the `SearchFilters.apply(...)` filter chain (the chain is still correct for user-driven chip toggles; only the entry point that called it without a hydrated cache is changed), the `SearchViewModel.scheduleSearch()` debounce (still 300ms per AC-3.1), the `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` REST+local merge path (the bug was in the caller, not the function), the `RecentSearches.maxEntries` cap (still 10), the `RecentSearches.storageKey` version suffix (still `dod.recentSearchesV1`), the telemetry contract (still `StringHasher.sha256Hex(trimmed)` per AC-3.6), the `WPRestClient.search(query:)` REST request shape, the `dependencies.searchIngredients(matching:)` local-index call, the `FilterChipRow`'s cook-time chip, the `recentlyViewedOnly` toggle chip, the offline-state copy / glyph (`wifi.slash` + "Search needs internet" stays), the idle-state fallback (when both recents and topCategories are empty, the "Find a recipe" + `magnifyingglass` empty state still renders), the search-field's leading `magnifyingglass` icon, the search-field's trailing clear button (`xmark.circle.fill` `viewModel.clear()`), or the Categories tab's `.searchable` filter (different surface, US-19 territory). **Implementing PR:** T-500 (this branch, `feat/T-500-search-tab-polish`). Parallel with T-510 (new-recipe surfacing, different code path — `LiveFeedDependencies` / `RecipeStore` / WP REST) and T-520 (color overhaul — `DODColor` token re-record). T-520 will re-record many snapshots; if T-520 touches the same `SearchView` snapshot files this PR touches, T-500's changes win because T-500 is the semantic owner of `SearchView.swift` for this round (T-520's diff is token-level, not surface-level). T-510's diff is feed/networking-only and doesn't intersect.

- **CL-46 (US-23 / T-395, 2026-05-25)** — **Saved Recipes widget contrast audit: clean — no `WidgetCard+Saved.swift` fix needed, same readability mandate extended to saved surfaces.** Sibling audit to the parallel-branch [T-394](tasks.md) fix on the Featured / Latest Recipe widget (`fix/T-394-widget-text-contrast`). T-394's CL-46 (same number — branches collide deliberately at merge time per the parallel-audit plan) captures the root cause of T-390's `widget-appearance-audit.md` Findings 2+3 miss on `WidgetCard.Small`'s `.foregroundStyle(.white)` recipe title over the hero gradient (same-group flattening under `widgetRenderingMode == .accented` + `containerBackground` stripping); T-395 runs the **same audit** against the saved-recipes surfaces declared in `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Components/WidgetCard+Saved.swift` (`WidgetCard.SavedSmall`, `WidgetCard.SavedMedium`, `WidgetCard.SavedEmpty`, `WidgetCard.SavedListRow`) plus their entry-view shell at `Widget/SavedRecipesWidgetEntryView.swift` and the widget configuration at `Widget/SavedRecipesWidget.swift`. **Audit verdict: clean — no source fix.** **Why clean:** the saved variants render text **NEXT TO** the `Hero` primitive, not OVER it. `SavedSmall.body` is a `VStack` (eyebrow → spacer → `Hero` thumbnail → title text); `SavedListRow.body` is an `HStack` (36pt-square `Hero` thumbnail on the left → title text on the right); `SavedMedium.body` is a `VStack` of `SavedListRow`s under the "Saved" eyebrow. There is no `ZStack` overlay placing text on top of an image anywhere in the file. The text in every saved variant sits on the `DODColor.surfaceElevated` brand background applied via `.background(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` (and the same token also applied at the widget chrome level via `.containerBackground(for: .widget) { DODColor.surfaceElevated }` in `SavedRecipesWidget.body`). Under `.accented` rendering the `containerBackground` is stripped and the asset-catalog brand colors collapse to the default group, but every text+background pairing in the saved variants stays in the *same* default group together — there is no foreground-vs-scrim contrast collapse like T-394's hero-title case where one element was supposed to be tinted differently from the other. **Hero image `.fullColor` opt-out inheritance:** the saved variants reuse the same `WidgetCard.Hero` primitive from `WidgetCard.swift` that T-390 already annotated with `widgetAccentedRenderingMode(.fullColor)` on the loaded image in the `WidgetCard.Hero.loadedImage(_:)` private helper. The opt-out fires transitively for `SavedSmall`'s 56pt-tall thumbnail and `SavedListRow`'s 36pt-square thumbnail — no per-variant change needed. The design intentionally lets the *empty/loading* fallback gradient tint with the system (per the audit doc's documented behavior at `widget-appearance-audit.md` Finding 4) so empty rows blend with the user's wallpaper rather than fighting it. **`SavedEmpty` placeholder visibility:** the empty-state copy ("Saved Recipes" headline + "Save a recipe to see it here." body) renders in `DODColor.label` / `DODColor.labelSecondary` on a `DODColor.surfaceElevated` background with a `DODColor.burntOrange` `bookmark.fill` SF Symbol above. All four asset-catalog tokens flatten into the default group together under `.accented` — no two-group contrast collapse, but also no text-over-image surface. The placeholder is the same situational shape as `WidgetCard.Placeholder` in the featured widget, which T-394 also explicitly excluded from the source fix (text on `surfaceElevated`, not text on a wallpaper-tinted plane). **Saved-at timestamp chip:** N/A — the saved variants do not render any time/timestamp chip (`TimeChip` is only used by the featured `Small` / `Medium` per `WidgetCard.swift`). The `SavedRow` data struct only carries `title` + `heroImageURL` (no timestamp), so there is no chip surface to audit. **What CL-46 (T-395 sibling) adds to AC-23.7:** the readability mandate T-394 pins for "text over image" extends to the saved surfaces by reference — any future saved-recipe widget variant that introduces a text-over-image composition (e.g. a hypothetical large-family saved-widget hero with overlaid recipe title) inherits AC-23.7's two-part requirement (explicit dark scrim band + `.widgetAccentable(true)` opt-in on the foreground). The current `WidgetCard+Saved.swift` does not have any such composition, so the mandate is enforced by-construction. **What CL-46 (T-395 sibling) does NOT touch:** any `WidgetCard+Saved.swift` source (verified clean), any `Widget/SavedRecipes*.swift` source (the entry view uses semantic accessibility labels and shares the same `surfaceElevated` background already on the design-system layer), the saved widget snapshot baselines (`SavedWidgetSnapshotTests`'s 12 Standard light + dark PNGs and the 6 Tinted/Vibrant PNGs under `WidgetCardTintedAppearanceSnapshotTests/test_savedWidget_*` are byte-identical because no source change), the snapshot wire format (`SavedRecipesWidgetSnapshot.Entry`), the deep-link grammar (`dod://recipe/<id>?source=saved`, `dod://saved`), the analytics event (`widgetOpened(kind: "saved", ...)`), or US-17's existing acceptance criteria (AC-17.1..AC-17.9 are pinned). **Considered:** add `.widgetAccentable(true)` to the `"Saved"` eyebrow text + the `SavedEmpty` `bookmark.fill` glyph as a *defensive* opt-in mirroring the lock-screen widget's bundle-level `.widgetAccentable()` in `LatestRecipeLockScreenWidgetEntryView`. Rejected — the lock-screen widget runs in `widgetRenderingMode == .vibrant` where the modifier semantically maps to "tint this leaf" because the entire surface is one monochrome material; on the home screen under `.accented` the modifier creates a *separation* between accent group and default group (the whole point of T-394's fix). Adding it to the saved-widget eyebrow + empty-state glyph would split them into the accent group while the surrounding title text stays in the default group, producing a *new* contrast disparity where none exists today. The saved variants' "everything flattens together" behavior under `.accented` is actually the correct outcome here. **Considered:** ship the spec-only T-395 entry but skip the entire fix attempt and frame it as "T-394 covers everything, no T-395 needed." Rejected — the audit framing (CL-39's "audit + targeted fixes" path, AC-23.6's clean-audit closure) explicitly requires per-surface verification; capturing the verdict in a CL-46 entry (and graduating any future regression report against the saved widget to a follow-up T-39X under the same audit-trail pattern) is the constitution §6 audit-trail behavior the saved surface deserves. **Considered:** wait for T-394's PR to merge and amend CL-46 in place rather than authoring a sibling entry on a parallel branch. Rejected — the two audits are independent surfaces (different files, different rendering compositions) and pushing them through the same PR would conflate "fix the smoking gun" with "verify the sibling is clean." Parallel branches keep each audit's scope crisp; merge-time collision on the CL-46 number is the documented resolution (whoever lands second renumbers or amends the existing entry, mirroring the same pattern T-362 / T-410 used when running parallel to other Phase 10 work). Implementing PR: T-395 (this branch, `fix/T-395-saved-widget-tint-contrast`).

- **CL-53 (US-12 / T-530, 2026-05-26)** — **"Any time" filter doesn't appear to compose with the category filter: root cause is that REST search results carry their WP `categories: [Int]` taxonomy on the wire but the network → domain → cache pipeline drops them, so `RecipeStore.categoryIDs(forRecipeIDs:)` returns `[recipeID: []]` for every fresh REST hit and `SearchFilters.apply(...)` silently excludes the entire result set.** Root cause traced by the round-6 backlog item 6: WP REST `/wp/v2/posts?search=...` returns each `Post` with a `categories` field (already verified — `WPDTO.Post` decodes it into `let categories: [Int]?` per `WPDTOs.swift:20`, and `WPRestClientPostsTests.decodesAndMapsToRecipeListItem`'s fixture pins `"categories": [1590, 334]` on the wire). But `WPDTO.Post.toRecipeListItem(heroImage:)` at `WPDTOs.swift:235` does NOT propagate `categories` into the `RecipeListItem` — the `categoryIDs` slice is dropped at the network → domain boundary. Downstream, `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` calls `dependencies.cache(listItems: restResults)`, which routes into `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` (lines 22-55 of `RecipeStore.swift`) — and that method never touches `existing.categoryIDs` / inserts a row with a non-empty `categoryIDs` array. So the only path that populates `CachedRecipe.categoryIDs` is `RecipeStore.mergeDetail(_:)` (line 92), which runs on recipe-detail open as a side effect of the JSON-LD parse. For freshly-fetched REST search hits whose detail pages have NOT yet been opened, the recipe row exists in the cache (from `cache(listItem:)`'s insert) but `categoryIDs` is an empty array. `RecipeStore.categoryIDs(forRecipeIDs:)` builds its return dictionary as `Dictionary(uniqueKeysWithValues: rows.map { ($0.id, $0.categoryIDs) })` — so the map contains `[recipeID: []]` for the row, NOT a missing entry. `SearchFilters.apply(...)` line 56 checks `guard categoryIDsByRecipe[item.id]?.contains(categoryID) == true else { return false }` — and `[]?.contains(10)` is `false`, so picking the category chip drops every fresh REST hit. The user then layers the Any-time chip on top expecting a narrower set; the AND with the cook-time predicate yielded zero results because the category-filtered set was already empty. From the user's seat, this reads as "the Any-time filter doesn't appear to be composing correctly with the category filter" — but the actual failure surface is the category filter dropping the fresh REST results before the cook-time filter ever runs. **Why CL-49.1 didn't already fix this:** CL-49.1 (T-500) addressed the *idle-state "Try" pill* path — that path was setting `filters.categoryID` as a side effect of tapping a category-suggestion pill, and the user's mental model was "type this in," not "filter by this." T-500 routed that pill into the query field and left the category-filter chain alone. CL-49.1 explicitly recognized that "this is the right architecture for *user-driven* filter chip toggles after results land (where the user explicitly opts into a filter knowing the result set is the current REST page)." That's the surface this CL fixes: when the user explicitly taps the category chip (or the Any-time chip on top), the filter chain must be able to match against fresh REST results, which means the WP `categories` taxonomy must round-trip into the cache via the same `cache(listItem:)` path REST results take. **Fix (REG-17):** add an optional `categoryIDs: [Int]?` field to `DODDomain.RecipeListItem` (Optional so existing Codable payloads decode cleanly — same backward-compat pattern `canonicalURL` followed per `RecipeListItem.swift:16-18` doc comment), populate it from `WPDTO.Post.categories` inside `toRecipeListItem(heroImage:)`, and update `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` to write the value through to `CachedRecipe.categoryIDs` on both the update and the insert paths. The cook-time filter still requires a JSON-LD `totalTime` parse to populate `CachedRecipe.totalSeconds` — that's documented MISS behavior per `RecipeStore+IngredientIndex.swift:84-87` and stays unchanged. Together, the category filter now matches against every fresh REST hit (using the wire data already in flight), and the cook-time filter composes AND-wise against that filtered set — picking "Beef and Red Meat Recipes" + "≤1 hour" returns the recipes that (a) are in the Beef category per the REST taxonomy AND (b) have a known total-time ≤60min per the local detail cache. The MISS-on-unknown-totalSeconds semantic is preserved (a recipe the user has never opened doesn't surface under a cook-time filter even if it's in the right category — same documented behavior the existing `SearchFiltersTests.categoryFilterTreatsUnknownAsMiss` test pins). **What `cache(listItem:)` does on conflict between an existing cached `categoryIDs` and the REST-supplied one:** the REST payload is authoritative if non-empty (WP's category taxonomy is the source of truth for which categories a post belongs to), but a nil value from the wire must NOT clobber an existing populated array (mirrors the existing `canonicalURL` policy at lines 32-34: "Only overwrite when a non-empty value is available so we don't clobber a good existing value with an empty one"). Implementation: `if let categoryIDs = listItem.categoryIDs, !categoryIDs.isEmpty { existing.categoryIDs = categoryIDs }` on the update branch, and a similar guard inlined into the insert branch's `CachedRecipe(...)` initializer call (the initializer's default `categoryIDs: [Int] = []` per `CachedRecipe.swift` means we can pass through the unwrapped value or fall back to the default). **Regression test (REG-17):** new `SearchResultMergerTests.composeCategoryAndCookTimeFiltersForFreshRESTResults` (the merger lives in `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Tests/DODFeatureSearchTests/SearchResultMergerTests.swift` per the prompt's "wherever the merger lives" framing — composition is exercised at the post-merge `SearchFilters.apply(...)` boundary the merger feeds into, so the merger test file is the natural home for the contract). The test sets up two recipes both in category 10 (one with `totalSeconds: 25 * 60`, one with `totalSeconds: 90 * 60`), runs the merger, applies the filters `{categoryID: 10, cookTime: .under60}`, and asserts only the under-60 recipe survives. A second assertion swaps `cookTime: .under15` and asserts neither survives (both >15min). The contract is the AND composition pinned to AC-12.3 for fresh REST results. The store-side cache round-trip is covered by a new `RecipeStoreCategoryIDsRoundTripFromListItem` test in `Packages/DODPersistence/Tests/DODPersistenceTests/SearchIndexTests.swift`'s `SearchFilterInputsTests` suite — caches a `RecipeListItem` with `categoryIDs: [10, 20]`, asserts `categoryIDs(forRecipeIDs: [id])[id] == [10, 20]` without ever calling `mergeDetail`. A third assertion in `WPRestClientPostsTests.decodesAndMapsToRecipeListItem` extends the existing fixture-based round-trip to check `item.categoryIDs == [1590, 334]`, locking the network → domain mapping. **Considered:** pre-hydrate `categoryIDsByRecipe` via a special "fetch the WP categories taxonomy for these IDs" REST round-trip the moment the user toggles the category chip. Rejected — that's a network round-trip the WP categories taxonomy doesn't directly support per-post (you'd have to fetch each recipe's detail or query categories by recipe-ID, neither of which is a free op), and CL-49.1 already documented this as "considered + rejected: pre-cache every WP category's recipe IDs at app launch — adds a network round-trip on launch for a feature that's better solved by changing the click action." The cleaner fix is to use the categories that **are already on the wire** in every REST search hit — they're sitting unused in `WPDTO.Post.categories` today. **Considered:** add the category data to the cache via a new public method on `RecipeStore` (`func injectCategoryIDs(_ map: [Int: [Int]]) async throws`) and call it from `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` after the REST + cache pass, threading the category data from `WPDTO.Post` through a side channel. Rejected — pushes the responsibility up to the view-model layer and forces every call site that wants the same fix (Feed, Categories, Saved, Saved widget snapshot publisher) to either repeat the injection call or accept inconsistent behavior. The HTTP-DTO seam where `WPDTO.Post.toRecipeListItem(heroImage:)` already maps wire → domain is the lowest seam that fixes this for every consumer; adding categories to `RecipeListItem` makes the fix automatic for `LiveFeedDependencies.fetchPosts(page:)` and `WPRestClient.posts(categoryID:)` too (Feed + Categories tabs now also benefit from cached category-IDs, which is correct-by-construction behavior — those surfaces just don't expose a category filter chip today so the benefit is latent). **Considered:** make `RecipeListItem.categoryIDs` non-optional `[Int]` with default `[]`, since the WP REST payload always returns the field (even if empty). Rejected — `RecipeListItem` is `Codable` and persisted via `WidgetSnapshotStore` and friends; older payloads (pre-CL-53) decode through synthesized `init(from:)`, and a non-optional field with no default at the call site would fail to decode any pre-CL-53 widget snapshot or any cached `RecipeListItem` JSON. Optional with default `nil` is the safe path the existing `canonicalURL`, `heroImage`, `totalTimeDisplay` fields all use for the same reason. **Considered:** also persist `categoryIDs` into `WidgetSnapshot` / `WidgetSnapshotStore` so the widget surfaces can filter too. Rejected — out of scope; this is a Search-tab filter fix, and the widget surfaces don't have a category filter today. The widget snapshot wire format stays byte-identical (the optional field on `RecipeListItem` doesn't auto-propagate into `WidgetSnapshot.Entry` because `WidgetSnapshot` uses a separate `Entry` struct, not `RecipeListItem` directly — verified at `Packages/DODSupport/Sources/DODSupport/WidgetSnapshot.swift:38`). **Considered:** change `SearchFilters.apply(...)`'s missing-vs-empty handling so that an empty `categoryIDs` array on a cached row falls through to "unknown → admit" rather than "miss → exclude." Rejected — that inverts the documented behavior `categoryFilterTreatsUnknownAsMiss` pins, and silently admitting recipes the system has no taxonomy data for would also break the explicit `categoryIDsByRecipe[item.id]?.contains(categoryID)` semantics for legitimate empty arrays (a recipe that genuinely belongs to zero categories should not match a category filter). The correct fix is to populate the data, not to weaken the filter. **What CL-53 does NOT touch:** `SearchFilters.apply(...)` (the chain stays AND-wise — fixing the data input, not the filter logic), `SearchResultMerger.merge(...)` (ranking + dedupe stays correct — locked by REG-12), `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` flow (still REST + local + merge + filter; the change is invisible at this layer because the cache pass it already calls now propagates one more field), the 300ms debounce (AC-3.1 still locks it), the telemetry hash contract (AC-3.6 still locks the SHA-256 hash, not the raw query), `RecipeStore.mergeDetail(_:)` (still the JSON-LD-driven update path for the *full* recipe detail — categories now arrive earlier via the REST search cache, but the detail merge still overwrites with the parsed value, which is identical in practice because WP serves the same taxonomy for both endpoints), the `CachedRecipe` SwiftData schema (the existing `var categoryIDs: [Int] = []` field stays — no migration, no schema bump, additive value-only write path), the `searchIngredients(matching:)` local index (US-12 / AC-12.1 contract unchanged), the cook-time bucket semantics (`CookTimeBucket.contains(totalSeconds:)` unchanged), the "Recently viewed" chip (independent filter slice), the offline graceful-degradation behavior (`offlineWithLocalIngredientHitsStillShowsResults` still passes — categories don't gate the local ingredient pass), the existing snapshot baselines (`SearchViewSnapshotTests`, `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` — no view change, no chip layout change). **Spec/trace:** US-12 (AC-12.2 filter chips, AC-12.3 filters compose without network — REG-17 is the AC-12.3 lock for *fresh REST results*); REG-12 (US-12 ingredient + filter chain — REG-17 is the composition-specific extension); CL-49.1 (the prior accepted trade-off this CL supersedes for the user-driven chip toggle path); AC-T4 (regression test in same PR as the fix). Implementing PR: T-530. **Coordination note:** T-440 (Recipe Scaling) is running on a parallel branch in this same round; T-440's diff is recipe-detail-only (`Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/**` + possibly `DODDomain.Recipe` for the scaling factor field) so source-side collision is zero. Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` / `backlog.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL slot (T-530 reserved CL-53; T-440 reserves CL-52 or the next free).

- **CL-50 (US-1 / T-510, 2026-05-26)** — **New-recipe surfacing: WP REST responses are served from `URLCache.shared` (and Cloudflare's CDN) without a `Cache-Control` directive, so `WPRestClient.posts()` can return a stale page-1 batch that omits a just-published recipe.** Root cause traced by the round-6 backlog "New-recipe surfacing" entry: T-420's REG-16 nightly L2 test (`LiveAPITests.newestPostIsReachableViaFeedRefresh`) passes in isolation — one fresh HTTP call inside a clean test process returns the newest post id and the round-trip through `WPRestClient.posts()` → `RecipeStore.cache(listItems:)` → `RecipeStore.listItems(forIDs:)` surfaces it. But in production the user keeps the app foregrounded across multiple refreshes, and `WPRestClient.get(...)` builds its `URLRequest` with the default `cachePolicy = .useProtocolCachePolicy` and no explicit `Cache-Control` request header. Two consequences stack: (a) **iOS URLCache.shared.** `URLSession.shared` consults its global cache before hitting the network; the WP REST response carries `Last-Modified` but **no `Cache-Control` or `Expires`**, so URLSession applies HTTP heuristic freshness — typically `0.1 * (now - Last-Modified)` — and returns the cached payload within that window. With a CDN-cached origin response showing `Age: 225` and `Last-Modified` 5 minutes old, the heuristic window is ~30 seconds during which pull-to-refresh returns the same 20 IDs the previous refresh just saw. (b) **Cloudflare CDN.** `cf-cache-status: HIT` confirms `dutchovendaddy.com`'s WP REST endpoint sits behind Cloudflare's CDN with a multi-minute edge TTL (the `x-wp-cf-super-cache-disabled-reason: API Request` header confirms WP's own super-cache is bypassed, but Cloudflare's edge cache is independent and active). Even when iOS URLCache misses, the CDN can serve a stale edge copy that predates a just-published recipe — the user pulls to refresh, the request leaves the device, the CDN returns the same pre-publish JSON, and the newest post stays invisible until Cloudflare's edge TTL expires. **Why T-420's L2 test missed this:** the test runs in a fresh `swift test` process where `URLCache.shared` is empty, makes one fresh call, and the response naturally bypasses both caches because there's no prior entry to serve. The CDN side gets touched by exactly one request, so any edge-cache staleness is statistically unlikely on a single run. The bug only surfaces under the *production* call pattern: user-driven refreshes within seconds-to-minutes of each other against a Cloudflare-cached endpoint. **Fix (REG-18):** set two markers on the `URLRequest` `WPRestClient.get(path:queryItems:)` constructs before handing it to `httpClient.data(for:)`: `request.cachePolicy = .reloadIgnoringLocalCacheData` (forces URLSession to skip URLCache.shared and hit the network) AND `request.setValue("no-cache", forHTTPHeaderField: "Cache-Control")` (asks Cloudflare to revalidate with origin per RFC 7234 §5.2.1.4). Both lines are required: cachePolicy alone bypasses iOS URLCache but lets the CDN serve a stale edge copy; `Cache-Control: no-cache` alone tells the CDN to revalidate but leaves URLCache.shared serving stale entries on the device side. The two-line fix forces both layers to revalidate, so pull-to-refresh actually picks up newly-published recipes. The change lives in `WPRestClient.get(path:queryItems:)` (the shared helper that backs `posts()`, `categories()`, `search()`, `comments()`, `ratings()` — every WP REST call this app makes), so all WP REST surfaces benefit identically. The WP REST payloads are small JSON (<50KB) and the app makes maybe a dozen REST calls per session, so the bandwidth/latency cost of bypassing both caches is negligible compared to the user-visible cost of the feed missing a freshly-published recipe. **Why bypass cache on every call rather than only on pull-to-refresh:** the alternative would be to expose a `forceFresh: Bool` parameter on `WPRestClient.posts()` and have `FeedViewModel.refresh()` set it. Rejected — that pushes the cache-control decision up to the call site and invites the same bug shape in any *future* call site that forgets to set it (e.g., a categories-refresh flow, a search-results refresh, a pull-to-refresh on the Saved tab). The constitution §6 "test pyramid" mandate to lock regressions at the lowest seam they can be locked at applies: bypass at the HTTP client level is the lowest seam that makes every call site correct by construction. **Why not also opt-in to `URLRequest.cachePolicy = .reloadIgnoringLocalAndRemoteCacheData`:** that policy adds `Pragma: no-cache` on top of `Cache-Control: no-cache`. Cloudflare honors both; `Pragma` is the HTTP/1.0 equivalent and adds no signal Cloudflare doesn't already get from `Cache-Control`. Sticking with `.reloadIgnoringLocalCacheData` + the explicit `Cache-Control` header keeps the request shape readable in `Charles` / `mitmproxy` traces (one header, one cache directive) without changing observable behavior. **Why not just add a cache-busting query parameter (e.g., `_t=<unix-ms>`):** that would force a CDN miss too (verified — `curl '...?_t=12345'` returns `cf-cache-status: MISS` and a fresh response from origin), but it pollutes Cloudflare's cache with a unique key per request and defeats the CDN's value for everyone hitting the same origin. The `Cache-Control: no-cache` header tells the CDN to revalidate this specific request with origin while still using the same cache key — keeps the CDN useful for non-app HTTP clients that don't opt out. **Why not `cachePolicy = .reloadRevalidatingCacheData`:** that policy tells URLSession to revalidate the cached entry with the server via `If-Modified-Since` rather than refetching unconditionally. The WP REST response carries `Last-Modified` but the server's revalidation response (`304 Not Modified`) would still come from Cloudflare's edge cache, not origin — same staleness problem. `.reloadIgnoringLocalCacheData` plus the request header forces a network round-trip that Cloudflare specifically routes to origin. **What CL-50 does NOT touch:** `WPRestClient`'s decoder shape (no change to JSON decoding), the `WPDTO` family (no change to wire-format parsing), `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` / `RecipeStore.listItems(forIDs:)` (the store-side path was already correct — the L2 test proved it on every nightly run since T-420 merged), `FeedViewModel.loadInitial(forceReplace:)` / `FeedViewModel.refresh()` (the view-model code was already correct), the blocklist clearing path (`AC-1.7` semantics unchanged), the offline-cache hydration path (`hydratedFromCache()` still returns nil per T-091/T-101 punt), or the `LiveFeedDependencies` widget-snapshot publish hook (CL-45's T-362 prefetch is unaffected — image bytes still arrive via `ImageLoader` + `RecipeStore.cacheImage(url:bytes:)`, and those `ImageLoader` calls hit `dod.shared`'s CDN image cache where edge caching is *desirable* and stays in place). **What CL-50 ADDS to AC-T4:** REG-18 — a regression test that fails on `origin/main` (URLCache.shared serves a planted stale response) and passes after the fix (URLCache is bypassed, fresh network data returned). The test lives in `Packages/DODIntegrationTests/Tests/DODIntegrationTestsTests/LiveAPITests.swift` alongside REG-2 and REG-16, gated by the same `DOD_RUN_LIVE_TESTS=1` env var so PR CI skips it and the nightly job picks it up. **Manual verification:** install the app on iPhone 17 simulator, capture the first 5 feed entries by id from the app, run `curl 'https://www.dutchovendaddy.com/wp-json/wp/v2/posts?per_page=5' | jq '.[].id'` against the same blog, confirm the two lists match. The PR body records the comparison output. **Considered:** make the cache-bypass opt-in via a `freshOnly: Bool` parameter that `FeedViewModel.refresh()` passes through. Rejected for the bypass-on-every-call reasoning above — pushing the decision up to the call site invites the bug to come back in any future call site that forgets it. **Considered:** keep the default `URLRequest` cache policy and instead invalidate `URLCache.shared` for the posts URL inside `FeedViewModel.refresh()` (`URLCache.shared.removeCachedResponse(for: request)`). Rejected — this doesn't address Cloudflare's edge cache (the second layer), and threading the right `URLRequest` from `FeedViewModel` down through `LiveFeedDependencies` and into `WPRestClient.get(...)` would invert the abstraction (the view-model would need to construct or peek at the request URL the client builds). The HTTP-client-level fix is cleaner. **Considered:** add a `Cache-Control: max-age=0` request header instead of `no-cache`. Rejected — `max-age=0` and `no-cache` have very similar semantics, but `no-cache` is the more direct expression of intent ("the request explicitly does not trust any cached response") and is what Cloudflare's docs use as the canonical bypass directive. **Considered:** also set the `URLRequest.networkServiceType` to `.responsiveData` to lower latency on the bypass-cache round-trip. Rejected — that flag changes QoS scheduling, not cache behavior, and would conflate two concerns (caching vs. latency) in one change. **Considered:** disable `URLCache.shared` globally at app launch (`URLCache.shared = URLCache(memoryCapacity: 0, diskCapacity: 0)`). Rejected — that would also disable URLCache for `ImageLoader`'s hero-image fetches and for any future side-call that wants HTTP caching (e.g., a categories fetch where staleness for ~5 minutes is fine). The per-request opt-out keeps the global cache available for surfaces that should use it. **Spec/trace:** US-1 (AC-1.4 pull-to-refresh shows newly published recipes); AC-T4 (regression test in the same PR as the fix); REG-18 (the new regression bullet under the Test pyramid that names the failure mode and the test that locks it). Implementing PR: T-510.

- **CL-56 (US-32 / T-550, 2026-05-26)** — **Settings page v1 ships as a skeleton inside `DODFeatureFeed`, not a new `DODFeatureSettings` package; sub-feature implementations defer to T-551 / T-552.** Three skeleton-first decisions for the Settings entry the round-4 backlog graduated: (1) **Package placement.** `SettingsView` + `SettingsViewModel` land in `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/` rather than a new `DODFeatureSettings` Swift package. Rationale: the entry-point gear icon lives in `FeedView.swift` (Recipes tab's nav bar), the Settings surface is one small screen with three rows, and adding a new Swift package would require touching `project.yml` (new package entry + scheme + dependency rewrite on the app target), the workspace, and the build matrix — none of which buys anything for a single view. Bundling into `DODFeatureFeed` keeps the diff bounded to one feature module and lets a future Settings expansion extract the package cleanly when the surface grows (e.g., when T-551 adds the metric-unit conversion path and T-552 adds the WP REST About Me fetch). The pattern mirrors how `RecipeDetailFloatingActions` initially lived inside `DODFeatureRecipeDetail` rather than its own package, then stayed there because the floating-actions surface never grew large enough to justify extraction. (2) **Scope split.** The user's primary ask is the gear-icon entry point. The three rows are bundled into v1 because the visual completeness of the Settings surface depends on all three landing together — a Settings screen with only a gear icon and an empty list looks broken. But the *implementation depth* behind each row is split: the metric-units Toggle ships as UI + UserDefaults round-trip only (T-551 will plumb the flag into the ingredient-rendering path); the About Me NavigationLink pushes a placeholder destination ("Coming soon — fetched from /about-me/") and T-552 will swap the placeholder for the WP REST fetch + cache + offline-fallback flow (the existing CL-1 hybrid strategy is the obvious template). The version footer ships inline because it's a four-line `Bundle.main.infoDictionary` read with no follow-up depth. (3) **List style.** `SettingsView` uses `.listStyle(.insetGrouped)` and `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden) + .background(DODColor.surface)` to match the Categories tab per CL-54 / T-560. The user's round-4 framing was explicit ("Layout styled to match the Categories tab"), and the post-T-520 `Surface` token now carries the brand palette on every screen, so matching Categories means matching the rest of the app by-construction. **What CL-56 does NOT touch:** any existing user-story acceptance criteria (US-1..US-31 are pinned), any existing analytics event, the deep-link grammar, the snapshot wire format, `DODColor` tokens, any other Feature package, the project.yml file (no new package = no project.yml churn = no XcodeGen regenerate trap REG-INFO-PLIST-CLOBBER warned us about). **Considered:** create a new `DODFeatureSettings` package mirroring the Feature-per-tab pattern (DODFeatureFeed, DODFeatureCategories, DODFeatureSearch, DODFeatureSaved). Rejected for the v1-skeleton scope reasons above — and because Settings is not a top-level tab (it's a push from the Recipes tab), so it doesn't actually fit the Feature-per-tab pattern even structurally. A future extraction (e.g., when Settings becomes its own top-level tab, or when it grows past ~5 screens) is the natural trigger. **Considered:** ship only the gear icon + an empty SettingsView placeholder in v1, then graduate the three rows as separate tasks. Rejected — the user's framing was an integrated Settings page with the three rows, and shipping only the entry point would land a visibly-empty screen with no obvious next user action. **Considered:** ship the metric-units toggle wired to actual ingredient conversion in v1. Rejected — that path needs a `FractionRenderer.metricEquivalent(...)` extension (currently the renderer only scales — see CL-52), the right call sites in `RecipeDetailView.ingredientsSection` and `CookModeView.ingredientsDrawer`, an `@Environment(\.useMetricUnits)` value or equivalent dependency-injection plumbing, plus the L1 + L4 test additions. That's a separate ~3-hour task (T-551) and bundling it into T-550 would conflate "ship the entry point" with "ship the conversion logic." **Considered:** ship the About Me WP REST fetch in v1 using the existing `WPRestClient` + `RecipeStore`-style hybrid pattern from CL-1. Rejected — needs a new `WPRestClient.pages(slug:)` method, a `CachedAboutMe` schema entry on the persistence layer (or a UserDefaults-cached `Data` blob if we want to avoid a schema bump), the offline-fallback empty-state copy, and the HTML-stripping logic to render the WP page content as readable Swift text. That's also a separate ~3-hour task (T-552). The skeleton placeholder ("Coming soon — fetched from /about-me/") makes the deferral explicit to the user. **Spec/trace:** US-32 (new, this CL); T-550 (this PR, ships the skeleton); T-551 / T-552 (follow-up tasks for the metric-unit conversion and the About Me fetch, both filed under Phase 10 in `tasks.md`). **Coordination note:** T-550 runs on a parallel branch to T-560 (Categories brown revert) and T-570 (dark foreground refinement). Source-side collisions are zero — T-560 touches `Packages/DODFeatureCategories/**`, T-570 touches `Packages/DODDesignSystem/**` asset catalogs, T-550 touches `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/**`. Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL slot (T-560 reserved CL-54; T-550 takes CL-56; T-570 will take CL-55 or CL-57 depending on landing order).

- **CL-54 (US-24 / T-560, 2026-05-26)** — **Amends CL-44: revert the Categories tab brown-surface override to `DODColor.surface`, matching the post-T-520 contract every other top-level tab already uses.** Supersedes the T-430 / CL-44 decision to paint the Categories list scroll surface with `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden) + .background(DODColor.castIronBrown)`. **Decision: swap the brown token for `DODColor.surface` (the same one-line `.background(DODColor.surface)` Feed (`FeedView.swift:26`), Saved (`SavedView.swift:18`), and Search (`SearchView.swift:25`) already apply).** **Why:** CL-44's original framing was that the Categories tab was "stranded as the only top-level tab whose background does not carry the brand palette" — the user wanted the Categories surface to feel like the rest of the app rather than the system-cream/near-black grouped fill. T-430 solved that by painting the surface `castIronBrown` because at the time `DODColor.surface` was the iOS-stock grouped background (light `#FAF6EE`, dark `#1B140E`), which had no brand presence. **T-520 (US-30 / CL-51) then re-tinted `Surface` to the user-specified hex values `#F9F6EF` (light) and `#42210B` (dark) — the warm cream / brown-tinted palette the user actually wanted on every screen.** With that overhaul on `main`, every tab's surface now carries the brand palette by-construction: Feed renders on `Surface`, Saved renders on `Surface`, Search renders on `Surface`, Categories *would* match too if its `castIronBrown` override were swapped for `DODColor.surface`. The current override produces the opposite of what CL-44 was after: Categories is the only tab painted a flat `#3D2B1F` (the appearance-invariant `castIronBrown`), while every other tab uses the new appearance-aware `#F9F6EF` light / `#42210B` dark `Surface` token. The visual cohesion CL-44 wanted is now best served by adopting `surface` here too — the post-T-520 `Surface` token IS the brand surface, and the Categories tab should match. **What changes in source:** swap the token in `CategoryListView.swift`'s `loadedList` (`Packages/DODFeatureCategories/Sources/DODFeatureCategories/CategoryListView.swift`) from `.background(DODColor.castIronBrown)` to `.background(DODColor.surface)`. The `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden)` modifier stays — `.insetGrouped`'s default fill is `UIColor.systemGroupedBackground` (an iOS-system color, NOT one resolved through the asset catalog's `Surface` colorset), so suppressing it via `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden)` is still required to make the asset-catalog `surface` token actually paint the surround. **Why not paint with `DODColor.surfaceElevated`:** the `.insetGrouped` list style already paints the row cells with `UIColor.secondarySystemGroupedBackground` (the system default for raised cells inside a grouped list), so the cells already render an appearance-appropriate near-white/near-black card fill that contrasts the `surface` token correctly. Re-painting cells with `.listRowBackground(DODColor.surfaceElevated)` would also work but introduces a second explicit token reference where the system default already gives us the two-tier `surface` (around the cards) + system-cell (in the cards) treatment the user is asking for. The smaller diff is "swap the surface token only; let cells inherit the system default" — matches the Feed / Saved / Search pattern exactly (those tabs also paint `surface` on the screen-level container and let SwiftUI-managed cards/lists handle elevated surfaces internally). **Why not paint with `DODColor.surfaceElevated`:** the `.insetGrouped` list style already paints the row *cells* with `UIColor.secondarySystemGroupedBackground`, which the asset catalog resolves through `SurfaceElevated` (`#FFFFFF` light / `#281F19` dark post-T-520). The system pipeline already gives us the two-tier `surface` (around the cards) + `surfaceElevated` (in the cards) treatment that matches what every other tab in the app does — re-implementing it with explicit tokens would re-introduce the same kind of override-that-paints-itself-into-a-corner trap CL-44 fell into. **Why this is a CL amendment and not a fresh CL framing:** CL-44 explicitly framed its decision against the surface-vs-cell-level tinting axis, with `castIronBrown` as the chosen surface paint. The post-T-520 world inverts that decision's premise (the `Surface` token now carries the brand brown the user wanted), so the cleanest framing is "the original decision was right *for the world that existed when it shipped*; the T-520 overhaul superseded it by giving every surface the brown palette CL-44 was trying to reach for one tab in isolation." Amending CL-44 in place (rather than authoring CL-54 as a fresh decision tree) would erase the historical reasoning trail, so CL-54 sits on its own and *references* CL-44 as the superseded predecessor. The CL-44 entry stays untouched in `clarifications.md` — readers of the spec history can see CL-44's full rationale, then CL-54's amendment context, and trace how the two decisions composed across the T-430 → T-520 → T-560 sequence. **Why this does NOT amend US-24 itself:** US-24's user-facing intent ("Categories tab should feel like the rest of the app, not a system-grey detour") is preserved verbatim — the *means* of achieving it shifted from "paint Categories brown" to "let the new Surface tokens paint everything." AC-24.1 is the one criterion that names the override directly and gets amended; AC-24.2 (cells stay system-default), AC-24.3 (`.searchable` legibility — now even simpler because the field sits on the standard system grouped surround), AC-24.4 (US-2 + US-19 not regressed), AC-24.5 (six baselines re-recorded), and AC-24.6 (accessibility) all stay valid because the new surface treatment IS the system-default treatment (which inherits the design-system tokens). **What CL-54 does NOT touch:** the `.insetGrouped` list style choice (US-19 / T-340 contract — unchanged), the `.searchable` placement (`.navigationBarDrawer(displayMode: .automatic)` — unchanged), the row composition (US-19 / AC-19.2 — unchanged), the L1 `CategoriesTests.filtered(...)` unit-test suite (no logic change), the `CategoryListViewModel` (no flow change), the `CategoryRecipesView` and its snapshot baselines (different screen, out of scope), the `DODColor` enum or its asset catalog (no token edits — T-520 already shipped the surface re-tint), any other tab's surface (Feed / Saved / Search each maintain their own surface treatments, each of which already inherit `DODColor.surface` post-T-520 by-construction), the `castIronBrown` token itself (still used by `RecipeCard`'s time chip, `OfflineBanner`, `Snackbar`, and the Search filter chip — those call sites are unchanged), the `WidgetSnapshot` wire format, the deep-link grammar, or any analytics event. **Considered:** keep `castIronBrown` as the Categories surface and amend T-520 to leave Categories alone. Rejected — the user's framing was explicit ("every page should match"), and re-introducing a per-tab override after T-520's overhaul would re-create the same inconsistency T-560 is trying to eliminate, just inverted (Categories would be the only tab still on `castIronBrown` while the rest use the new `Surface` tokens — visually different from the user's goal). **Considered:** ship CL-54 as an inline edit to CL-44 (replacing its **Decision:** clause with the new outcome). Rejected — the CL log is an append-only decision history per the spec's documented convention (CL entries are timestamped and authored at the time the decision was made; editing a prior CL's rationale in place would muddy the historical trail). The "amends CL-44" framing is the documented superseded-decision pattern (mirrors CL-49's "amends AC-20.3" handling for T-350's no-results glyph reversal, where T-500's CL-49.3 superseded the carve-out without rewriting T-350's CL or AC). **Considered:** apply a different brand color (e.g. `BurntOrange`, `WarmGold`) instead of removing the override. Rejected — the goal is consistency with the other tabs, not "Categories has its own accent color." Every other tab's surface is `Surface` post-T-520, so Categories should match. **Considered:** introduce a new `DODColor.surfaceCategories` token tuned for the Categories tab specifically. Rejected for the same reason CL-44 rejected adding a `surfaceBrown` token — adding a token to solve a one-tab problem over-fits the design system to a single screen, and the user's framing is "every page should match," not "Categories should have its own bespoke token." **Spec/trace:** US-24 (the user-story intent is preserved; AC-24.1 is amended in this PR); CL-44 (the prior accepted decision this CL supersedes); CL-51 (the T-520 overhaul that made this amendment possible — re-tinted `Surface` / `SurfaceElevated` to the brand palette across the entire app); CL-31 ("no new tokens for this kind of fix" framing carries forward). Implementing PR: T-560. **Coordination note:** T-570 (dark foreground refinement) and T-550 (Settings page) are running on parallel branches in this same round. T-570 touches `DODColor.label` / asset catalog dark-mode foreground tokens — different file from T-560's `CategoryListView.swift`. T-550 touches a new Settings feature package — also different from T-560's territory. Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL slot (T-560 reserved CL-54; T-570 and T-550 will pick CL-55 / CL-56 / CL-57 as they land).

- **CL-57 (US-33 / T-580, 2026-05-27)** — **Search-tab "Clear All" adopts the brand-orange `DODColor.accent` token to match the gear-icon tint; per-term recent removal ships via a SwiftUI `.contextMenu` long-press destructive action wired through a new `SearchViewModel.removeRecentSearch(_:)` method.** Two small Search-tab polish items from round 7 bundled into one PR — both touch `SearchView.swift`'s `RecentSearchesView`, both are S/XS scope. **(1) Color match.** The Recipes-tab gear icon (US-32 / T-550, `FeedView.swift:58`) renders `Image(systemName: "gearshape")` with NO explicit `.foregroundStyle` / `.tint` modifier, so it inherits the app-level `.tint(DODColor.accent)` propagated from `RootView.swift:127` + `RootView.swift:161` (the `accent` token resolves to `#C56A24` per `Resources/Colors.xcassets/Accent.colorset/Contents.json`, identical RGB to `BurntOrange.colorset`). The "Clear All" button in `IdleSuggestionsView.recentsSection` was styled with `DODColor.castIronBrown` (`#3D2B1F`-equivalent dark brown — the recipe-card time-chip + offline-banner + snackbar token) when US-29 / T-500 introduced it, because at the time the round-6 design intent was "match the warm brown filter-chip palette." Round-7 user feedback reframes that intent: the Clear All affordance should match the *gear icon's* orange, not the filter chip's brown, because the gear icon is the user's mental anchor for "global app action affordance" and Clear All is the same kind of affordance (a global wipe). Swapping `castIronBrown` → `accent` is the minimal token change that produces the user-requested visual match. The bulk wipe-all behavior (calling `viewModel.clearRecentSearches()` per CL-49.2) is unchanged — this CL only retints the existing button. **(2) Per-term context menu.** The round-7 user request is "long-press a recent search, see a Clear action with trash icon, removes only that one term." This is the standard iOS context-menu pattern (Mail's swipe → Trash, Photos' long-press → Delete, Messages' long-press → Delete) but in SwiftUI's `.contextMenu` form. **Decision: each `pill(...)` inside `IdleSuggestionsView.recentsSection`'s `FlowLayout` gets a `.contextMenu` modifier containing exactly one `Button(role: .destructive)` with `Image(systemName: "trash")` + `Text("Clear")`. The button's action calls a new `SearchViewModel.removeRecentSearch(_:)` method that delegates to a new `RecentSearches.remove(_:)` method on the persistent store.** `RecentSearches.remove(_:)` mirrors the case-insensitive dedupe rule from `RecentSearches.record(_:)` (`current.removeAll { $0.caseInsensitiveCompare(trimmed) == .orderedSame }`) — a no-op if the term doesn't match any stored entry, otherwise removes just that entry and writes back. The view-model wrapper updates the view-bound `recentSearches` array via the same `recents.recent()` re-read pattern `clearRecentSearches()` uses, so the FlowLayout re-renders on the next observation tick. **Why a destructive role on the menu item:** `Button(role: .destructive)` is the SwiftUI canonical way to communicate "this action removes data" — it paints the label red automatically on iOS 15+ and surfaces the right VoiceOver hint ("Destructive action"). The trash glyph reinforces the destructive semantics visually. **Why "Clear" and not "Delete" or "Remove":** the bulk action one line up is "Clear All," so the per-term action reads as the natural singular ("Clear All" → "Clear"). Matches Mail's "Clear" Trash-folder confirmation copy precedent. "Delete" connotes data destruction beyond the recents-cache surface (e.g., the user's recipe library) and "Remove" reads as undoable. **Why a context menu rather than swipe-to-delete:** the recent searches render as pills in a `FlowLayout`, not list rows.
