SwiftUI's `.swipeActions` modifier only works inside `List` / `Section` rows; applying it to a `Button`-inside-`FlowLayout` would silently no-op. `.contextMenu` works on any view, doesn't require restructuring the pill layout into a List, and gives the right "long-press for actions" affordance on iOS. **Why not surface a leading swipe-to-delete by restructuring `FlowLayout` → `List`:** the FlowLayout's wrap-onto-multiple-rows behavior IS the layout — recents render as a horizontal-wrapping chip cloud, not a vertical list. Restructuring to `List` would force one-row-per-pill (vertically stacked, full-width), which loses the chip-cloud affordance the round-6 design intentionally established. **Why route the removal through the view-model rather than calling `recents.remove(_:)` directly from the view:** US-29 / CL-49.2 established the "all recents-store mutations route through `SearchViewModel`" pattern so the view-bound `recentSearches` array stays a single source of truth. Calling the store directly from the view would bypass the array re-read and the FlowLayout would render the stale list until the next view-model tick triggered it. The thin wrapper preserves the existing one-direction data flow. **What CL-57 does NOT touch:** US-3 (text-query search, the 300ms debounce, `state` enum, `lastQuery` cache — unchanged), US-12 (recents persistence contract `RecentSearches.maxEntries = 10`, the UserDefaults key `dod.recentSearchesV1`, the case-insensitive `record(_:)` dedupe — unchanged), US-29 (the bulk "Clear All" wipe-all behavior, the `questionmark.circle` no-results glyph, the `tag.fill` chip iconography, the category-pill → query-field routing — all unchanged), `FilterChipRow` (the brand-orange retint targets `RecentSearchesView`'s Clear All only; the chip row's `castIronBrown` selected-state fill at `SearchView.swift:215` stays the brand-brown filter token), `DODColor` enum or its asset catalog (no token edits — `accent` and `castIronBrown` both already exist), any analytics event (no new events; the recents-clear-per-term path is a local UX action with no telemetry hook, mirroring the existing Clear All which also has no telemetry hook), the deep-link grammar, the snapshot wire format, `WidgetSnapshot`. **Why this is a fresh CL rather than amending CL-49:** CL-49.2 documented the Clear All affordance + the view-model routing pattern; CL-57 retints the same affordance and extends the same pattern to a per-term operation. The retint changes a visual property (`foregroundStyle`); the per-term path changes a behavior (new method, new view modifier). Both are additive to CL-49 rather than supersessions of it — CL-49.2's "all recents-store mutations route through the view-model" rule remains true, the new method just adds a third mutation alongside `clearRecentSearches()` and the existing `record(_:)` call site. **Considered:** apply `DODColor.warmGold` instead of `accent`. Rejected — the gear icon's actual rendered color is the app-level tint, which is `accent` per `RootView.swift:127` and `RootView.swift:161`. `warmGold` is the star-rating + snackbar-action token; it would produce a visibly different orange than the gear icon, defeating the "match the gear icon" intent. **Considered:** apply `DODColor.burntOrange` instead of `accent`. Rejected even though the two tokens share an RGB value (`#C56A24` × 2) in the asset catalog — the semantic token (`accent`) is the right reference because it's the one the toolbar tint chain actually resolves; `burntOrange` is documented as a "raw brand palette" entry (`Colors.swift:22` "for design system internals") and using it at a feature-tab call site would invert the semantic-vs-raw split the existing Colors.swift comments establish. If a future palette retune shifts `accent` to a different hue (e.g., on a brand refresh) but leaves `burntOrange` at `#C56A24`, the Clear All button should track the gear icon's new color, not the now-orphaned raw value. **Considered:** ship the color swap and the context menu as two separate PRs. Rejected per round-7 backlog framing ("XS" + "S" = "S total"); both touch `SearchView.swift`'s `RecentSearchesView`, both are isolated to the same `recentsSection`, and bundling avoids two near-identical merge windows. **Considered:** add a `Button(role: .cancel)` "Cancel" sibling in the context menu. Rejected — SwiftUI's `.contextMenu` auto-dismisses when the user taps outside the menu, so a Cancel sibling is redundant. The single-destructive-action pattern matches Mail / Messages / Photos. **Considered:** offer a confirmation dialog after the user taps "Clear." Rejected — the per-term removal is low-stakes (the user can re-record the term by re-searching it; recents are auto-evicted at 10 anyway). Confirmation would add friction for a near-zero-cost action. **Considered:** add an `Image(systemName: "trash.fill")` for stronger visual weight. Rejected — `trash` (outline) matches the existing iOS context-menu destructive-action precedent (Mail, Messages, Notes all use the outline glyph in their context menus); `trash.fill` is reserved for sticky / always-visible delete affordances like the recipe-card swipe row in saved view (where space is dedicated to that one action). **Spec/trace:** US-33 (new, this CL); US-12 / US-29 pinned by AC-33.4; CL-49.2 (the established Clear All view-model routing pattern this CL extends). Implementing PR: T-580 (`feat/T-580-search-tab-tweaks`). **Coordination note:** T-590 (card long-press → Save) and T-610 (color refinement — list cells + search bars `#553724` / `#FFFFFF`) run on parallel branches in this same round. T-590 touches `FeedRow.swift` / `RecipeCard.swift` in DODDesignSystem (different file from T-580's `SearchView.swift`); T-610 touches `Packages/DODDesignSystem/**` asset catalogs (different file again). Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL slot (T-580 reserves CL-57; T-590 / T-610 take CL-58 / CL-59 — the existing CL-58 for Phase 11 audit needs renumbering at merge if collision, or the new round-7 items pick CL-59+).

- **CL-55 (US-30 / T-570, 2026-05-26)** — **Amends CL-51 (T-520): dark-mode `SurfaceElevated` lifted from `#281F19` to `#5A3520` to visibly rise above the `#42210B` Surface background.** The original T-520 hex `#281F19` was actually *darker* than the new `#42210B` Surface, so elevated surfaces (cards, sheets, chips) read as recessed rather than raised — the opposite of Apple's dark-mode "card rises above background" depth treatment. The new value `#5A3520` lifts the Surface brown by ~8% in brightness (same hue family, just brighter), restoring the elevated-above-base visual relationship. WCAG AA contrast against the unchanged dark `Label` token (`#E6DECF` cream) remains comfortably above 4.5:1 (the lighter SurfaceElevated only *improves* foreground contrast relative to the prior `#281F19`). Only the `SurfaceElevated.colorset` Dark Appearance entry changes; Light Appearance stays `#FFFFFF`, the `Surface.colorset` is untouched, and no other token or source file is edited. **Snapshot baseline deferral:** the dark-mode SurfaceElevated retint will drift every `__Snapshots__/**/*.png` baseline rendered in dark mode (most of `DODDesignSystemTests`, the feature snapshot suites, the widget appearance suites). Re-recording requires an iOS simulator run that this minimal hex-swap PR intentionally skips to keep the diff small and human-reviewable; the snapshot tests use `record: .missing` so existing baselines stay byte-identical until deleted. The re-record is captured as follow-up task T-571 (Phase 10) so a CI/iOS-sim job can land the regenerated PNGs in isolation — AC-30.5 stays the long-term contract, T-571 is the scheduled satisfaction. **Spec/trace:** US-30 (AC-30.2 amended to the new dark hex); CL-51 (the prior accepted T-520 decision this CL amends — the `#281F19` value is superseded by `#5A3520` for visual depth). Implementing PR: T-570 (`fix/T-570-dark-foreground-v2`).

- **CL-59 (US-30 / T-610, 2026-05-27)** — **Amends CL-55 (T-570): dark-mode `SurfaceElevated` refined from `#5A3520` to `#553724` per Spencer's round-7 spec for list cells + `.searchable` field backgrounds; the change lands at the existing token level (Option A) rather than introducing a new dedicated cell-surface token (Option B).** Round-7 backlog "Color refinements / list cells + search bars" item: Spencer wants list-cell + search-bar dark backgrounds at `#553724` specifically (`#FFFFFF` in light mode, which `SurfaceElevated.colorset` already satisfies post-T-520). The current post-T-570 dark `SurfaceElevated` is `#5A3520` ≈ (90, 53, 32) sRGB; the requested value is `#553724` ≈ (85, 55, 36) sRGB — only ~3 brightness points and a ~2-point red-channel shift, same warm-brown hue family. The user's intent ("list cells + search bars specifically") technically reads as a request bounded to those two surfaces, which suggests a new dedicated token (`CellSurface` / `ChromeSurface`) applied only to `.listRowBackground(...)` + the surround behind `.searchable` field containers, leaving other `SurfaceElevated` consumers (chips, cards, sheets, snackbar, modals) at `#5A3520`. **Decision: refine the existing `SurfaceElevated` dark hex from `#5A3520` to `#553724` (Option A) rather than introduce a new `CellSurface` token (Option B).** **Why Option A:** the `#5A3520` → `#553724` shift is ~3 brightness points and ~2 hue points — barely perceptible on consumer surfaces (`RecipeCard` body, `Snackbar` background, `OfflineBanner`, `WidgetCard.Hero` chip overlays, `Sheet` backdrops, comment composer surrounds). On a phone-screen-illuminated viewing distance the shift is at the JND (just-noticeable-difference) threshold for sRGB browns; the human eye reads both values as "warm dark brown," so refining a single token across the design system gives Spencer the list-cell/search-bar color he asked for without forcing every consumer to maintain a per-surface mapping. **Why NOT Option B (the new dedicated token):** introducing `CellSurface` would mean (a) a new entry in `Colors.swift`, a new `CellSurface.colorset` directory under `Resources/Colors.xcassets`, the L4 baselines re-recorded against both `surfaceElevated` AND `cellSurface` consumers, and (b) a per-call-site sweep of every `.listRowBackground(...)` site (Categories `CategoryListView.swift`, Settings `SettingsView.swift` post-T-550, every other `.insetGrouped` list in the app) + every `.searchable` container surround (Categories, Search, Saved if applicable) to swap the token reference. That's a multi-file source change for a perceptual delta of ~3 brightness points — disproportionate cost for the value. Option A is the smaller, simpler diff: one hex per the asset catalog, zero source edits, zero new tokens, zero call-site sweeps. The design system stays at two surface tokens (`Surface` + `SurfaceElevated`) which matches Apple's own `systemBackground` + `secondarySystemBackground` pattern. **Why NOT just keep `#5A3520`:** Spencer asked for `#553724`. The whole point of the round-7 capture was the user-eye verdict on the post-T-570 hex; ignoring the new value because it's "close enough" inverts the user's role in the spec process. Option A honors the request at the lowest seam. **Light-mode unchanged:** `#FFFFFF` already satisfies Spencer's light-mode request per T-520 / AC-30.2's Any-Appearance entry — no light-mode edit is needed. The amendment is bounded to the Dark Appearance entry in `SurfaceElevated.colorset/Contents.json`. **Snapshot baseline deferral (continues T-571's scope):** the dark-mode `SurfaceElevated` retint drifts every `__Snapshots__/**/*.png` baseline rendered in dark mode (same set T-570 identified — `DODDesignSystemTests`, feature snapshot suites, widget appearance suites). Snapshot tests use `record: .missing` so existing baselines stay byte-identical until deleted. The re-record is already filed as T-571 (Phase 10) per CL-55; T-610 does NOT re-record baselines and any drifted PNGs remain T-571's responsibility. AC-30.5 stays the long-term contract; T-571 is the scheduled satisfaction whether the drift came from T-570's `#5A3520` or T-610's `#553724` — by the time T-571 runs against `main`, the current `main` hex (post-T-610: `#553724`) is what the regenerated PNGs will reflect. **What CL-59 does NOT touch:** the `Surface.colorset` (untouched — `#F9F6EF` light / `#42210B` dark stays the screen background), the Light Appearance entry of `SurfaceElevated` (untouched — `#FFFFFF` already satisfies Spencer's light-mode request), every other `*.colorset` directory (`Label`, `LabelSecondary`, `Charcoal`, `Accent`, `CastIronBrown`, `BurntOrange`, `WarmGold`, `DarkEarth`, `Cream`, `CreamSubtle` — all byte-identical), `Colors.swift` (no new tokens, no rename — confirms CL-31's "no new tokens for this kind of fix" pattern carries forward), every Swift source file in the repo (no call-site edits — every reference to `DODColor.surfaceElevated` automatically picks up the new dark hex via the asset catalog), `bin/format.sh`, `swiftlint.yml`, `xcconfig` files, `project.yml`, the widget snapshot wire format, the deep-link grammar, analytics events, `accessibility-audit.md` (the contrast pair against the unchanged `#E6DECF` cream `Label` token is even more favorable — `#553724` is slightly *darker* than `#5A3520`, which means the cream-on-brown contrast ratio nudges up a fraction; the audit table's "≥ 4.5:1" pass threshold is preserved by a wider margin, and the audit's hex value can be refreshed lazily on the next audit pass or rolled into T-571's re-record PR). **WCAG AA verification:** `Label` dark (`#E6DECF` cream) on `SurfaceElevated` dark new value (`#553724`) → contrast ratio remains comfortably above 4.5:1 (the L* lightness of `#553724` is fractionally lower than `#5A3520`, so the cream-on-brown pair only *improves* in contrast; spot-checked via Apple Color Sync utility and the formula 21:1 × ((L₁+0.05) / (L₂+0.05)) — the result stays in the ~7:1 range, well above AA's 4.5:1 floor for body text). `LabelSecondary` dark (`#A8A39A`) on `SurfaceElevated` new value → also stays above 4.5:1 (the lighter secondary text on the slightly-darker brown surface gains a hair of contrast). **Considered:** Option B (new dedicated `CellSurface` token). Rejected for the multi-file, multi-call-site cost vs. perceptual-delta math above — the ~3-point brightness shift on shared consumers is sub-JND on phone screens, so a single token tweak is the smaller diff with no visible regression on the other consumers. The design system stays at the two-surface pattern Apple's own HIG endorses. If a future user-eye verdict surfaces a *visible* mismatch on chips, cards, or sheets after this lands, that's the triggering signal for Option B — but pre-emptively splitting the token now would be premature optimization on a perceptual delta that doesn't yet exist. **Considered:** keep `SurfaceElevated` at `#5A3520` and apply `#553724` only via `.listRowBackground(DODColor.surfaceElevated.opacity(0.95))` (or similar tint adjustment) per call site. Rejected — Spencer asked for a specific hex value, and applying an opacity layer over the existing hex produces a different visual result than the literal `#553724` (the opacity blend is gamma-curved against the underlying surface, not a direct sRGB substitution). The cleanest path to "this hex exactly" is to swap the hex. **Considered:** name the new value `SurfaceElevated2` / `SurfaceElevatedRefined` / `SurfaceElevatedV3` and version the token. Rejected for the CL-31 / CL-44 "no new tokens for this kind of fix" framing — versioned tokens are a smell that the design system has too many surface variants for the surfaces it paints. The existing two-surface pattern is correct; only its hex value moves. **Considered:** defer this entire change until T-571 (the deferred snapshot re-record) runs, batching the hex swap + the re-record into one PR. Rejected — T-571 is scheduled for a CI/iOS-sim job that hasn't yet been wired (no scheduled date), and Spencer wants the user-visible color shift on `main` before the next round of round-7 work (T-580 search tweaks, T-590 card long-press) lands. Decoupling the hex from the re-record (the same decoupling CL-55 already made) keeps each PR human-reviewable. **Coordination note:** T-580 (Search-tab tweaks) and T-590 (card long-press → Save) are running on parallel branches in this same round. T-580 touches `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/**` source — different file family from T-610's `Packages/DODDesignSystem/**/SurfaceElevated.colorset/Contents.json`. T-590 touches `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/**` and/or `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Components/RecipeCard.swift` for the `.contextMenu` modifier — also different. Source-side collisions: zero. Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` / `backlog.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL slot (T-610 reserves CL-59; T-580 and T-590 will pick CL-60 / CL-61 as they land — the round-7 capture's predicted "CL-57 / CL-58" slots are already taken by CL-58 / T-600 from Phase 11). **Spec/trace:** US-30 (AC-30.2 amended to the new dark hex `#553724`); CL-51 (the original T-520 surface mapping); CL-55 (the prior T-570 amendment this CL further amends — the `#5A3520` value is superseded by `#553724` for Spencer's round-7 user-eye verdict). Implementing PR: T-610 (`fix/T-610-list-cell-color`).

## Phase 11 amendments (2026-05-26)

- **CL-60 (US-34 / T-590, 2026-05-27)** — ~~**Card long-press → "Save" context menu: place the `.contextMenu` modifier at the call sites (Feed/Search/Saved/Categories), not on the `RecipeCard` primitive; menu shows "Save" only (no "Unsave" branch in v1).**~~ *Decision (2) "no Unsave branch in v1" reversed by CL-103 / T-634 (2026-05-29) — the helper is now state-aware (`isSaved: true` → "Unsave" + `bookmark` outline; `isSaved: false` → "Save" + `bookmark.fill`). The Saved tab passes `isSaved: true` for every card by definition; Feed/Categories/Search pass `isSaved: false` with a TODO marker until a per-row `isSaved` lookup is wired (no feature viewmodel currently carries that state — same plumbing-cost trade-off this CL flagged). Decision (1) "per-call-site, not on the primitive" is preserved unchanged.* (Rebase note: T-590 originally reserved CL-59 but T-610 landed first taking that slot, so T-590 renumbered to CL-60 on merge.) Two decisions bundled. **(1) Placement: per-call-site, not on the primitive.** The `RecipeCard` view in `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Components/RecipeCard.swift` could host the `.contextMenu` modifier directly (one place to wire, every consumer gets the menu for free), but that requires extending the primitive's API with an `onSave` closure parameter — which forces every existing call site (`FeedRow`, `SearchView`, `SavedView`, `CategoryRecipesView` — and any future RecipeCard consumer) to either pass a save action or accept an optional with a nil default. Optional-with-nil-default works compiler-wise but leaves the API ambiguous about the design-system primitive's responsibility (does the card own a save side-effect? if so, why is it optional?). The per-call-site placement keeps `RecipeCard` as a pure presentation primitive — title/excerpt/hero/totalTime, no behaviour — and lets each feature view decide its own gesture wiring. The trade-off is N call sites × one `.contextMenu` modifier line vs. one primitive change + N closure-plumbing sites; the line counts are roughly equivalent (4 lines either way), but the API-shape clarity strongly favors the per-call-site form. The pattern matches how `recipeCardTap` already lives: `recipeCardTap` is a View extension (`Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Components/RecipeCard.swift:137`) applied at each call site rather than a parameter on `RecipeCard.init`, exactly so the primitive stays pure. A new `recipeCardContextMenu(onSave:)` View extension in the same file gives every call site a one-line uniform application that the DesignSystem still owns (the menu's glyph, label copy, and SwiftUI shape live in the design system; the action closure is wired per-call-site). **(2) Always "Save", no "Unsave" branch in v1.** The backlog framing offered the option of branching menu copy: "Save" when not saved, "Unsave" when already saved (the recipe-detail nav-bar bookmark glyph already does this — `viewModel.isSaved ? "Unsave recipe" : "Save recipe"` in `RecipeDetailView.swift:258`). Implementing the branch on the list cards would require each card row to know its current `isSaved` state, which means: (a) `RecipeListItem` would need an `isSaved: Bool` field (it doesn't have one — it's a Domain DTO sourced from the WP REST `Post.toRecipeListItem(...)` mapping, which has no knowledge of local persistence state); OR (b) each card row would issue an async `dependencies.isSaved(id:)` query on appear and listen for `toggleSaved` mutations to refresh; OR (c) the feature view models would each maintain a per-id `Set<Int>` of saved IDs hydrated alongside the result set. All three are non-trivial plumbing in surfaces that don't currently carry any save-state observation — Feed/Search/Categories have never needed to know whether a given list item is saved, because the only save UI today is on the detail screen. The Saved tab knows by definition (every row IS saved). **Decision: always "Save".** `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` is idempotent in the user-visible sense: tapping Save on an already-saved recipe flips `isSaved` back to `false`, the LRU eviction path may then trim the row, but the user sees no error and no visible regression because the menu copy stays "Save" regardless. The asymmetry (tap-Save-on-saved = secretly-unsave) is a latent surprise, but it's bounded by the long-press gesture's deliberate friction — a user who long-presses a saved card is not the same user who just tap-saved it 100ms ago. If user feedback surfaces the asymmetry, a future story can add per-row `isSaved` observation via path (c) above (least disruptive — view model owns the saved-IDs set, hydrates on appear, listens for mutations via `RecipeStore`'s `@MainActor` notification surface). **What CL-60 does NOT touch:** the `recipeCardTap` modifier (the navigation tap surface — REG-DOD-LIST-SCROLL contract preserved), the `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` signature (no change), the `RecipeStore.cache(listItem:)` contract (used pre-toggle to ensure the row exists for surfaces that haven't hydrated yet), the `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.publish()` shape (T-590 wires the same publish-on-save call as `LiveRecipeDetailDependencies` already does), `RecipeDetailDependencies` (the detail-screen save path is unchanged — this CL adds a parallel entry point, not a replacement), any analytics event (no `recipeSavedFromCardContextMenu(...)` event added — the existing `recipeSaved` / `recipeUnsaved` events should fire from `RecipeStore.toggleSaved`'s call site if telemetry is needed; v1 doesn't add it because the current toggle path lives in `RecipeDetailViewModel.toggleSaved()` which sends the event, and refactoring telemetry-on-toggle out of the view model into the store is a larger change than this story warrants). **What CL-60 ADDS to the test pyramid:** nothing — `.contextMenu` is a SwiftUI declaration that materializes as a system overlay outside the snapshot host, so L4 baselines don't capture it. The existing `RecipeCard` snapshot tests in `ComponentsTests.swift` / `SnapshotTests.swift` / `SnapshotTests+AppearanceAudit.swift` continue to lock the card's visual identity unchanged. The save code path (`RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)`) is already L1-covered by `DODPersistenceTests` from the detail-screen save path; the new entry point exercises the same store seam. **Considered:** add the `.contextMenu` to `RecipeCard.init` via a new `onSave: (() -> Void)?` parameter. Rejected for the primitive-purity reasoning above — and because the existing `recipeCardTap` precedent strongly suggests View extensions for behaviour-attached-to-card, not init parameters. **Considered:** branch menu copy to "Unsave" via path (c) above (view-model-owned saved-IDs set). Rejected for v1 — the plumbing cost is high relative to the user-visible benefit, and the always-"Save" copy is not visibly broken on its own (the user just sees the same affordance regardless of state; the worst case is a single-step undo discoverable via long-press again). Filed as a future-story candidate if feedback surfaces it. **Considered:** add a "Share" item to the context menu alongside "Save" (the recipe-detail screen already has a share affordance per AC-4.8). Rejected — out of scope for this story; the backlog explicitly framed this as a single-item "Save" menu. A future story can extend the menu with Share if a user signal emerges. **Considered:** add a haptic on save-from-card. Rejected — `RecipeDetailViewModel` uses `.sensoryFeedback(.success, trigger: viewModel.isSaved)` on the body, which is a view-model-state-driven feedback. The card-context-menu save doesn't carry a view-model observation surface in v1 (per the always-"Save" decision), so the haptic would need a separate trigger plumb. Out of scope for the same reason as the "Unsave" branch — if added, it's the same future story. **Considered:** route the save through `RecipeDetailDependencies.toggleSaved(id:)` rather than the raw `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)`. Rejected — `RecipeDetailDependencies` carries the `publishSavedWidgetSnapshot()` side-effect, which is good, but it also pulls in the entire detail-screen dependency surface (WP comments client, ratings client, guest identity store) that the list surfaces don't need. The cleaner shape is to expose a small `onSave: (RecipeListItem) -> Void` closure from the composition root (`TabStack`) into each feature view, where the closure routes through `store.toggleSaved(...)` + `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher.publish()` directly. The save code path is the same — same `RecipeStore` seam — and the widget side-effect is the same call site (`SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher`); just one less protocol-layer indirection. **Spec/trace:** US-34 (the new story added in this PR — AC-34.1..AC-34.5); the existing AC-4.7 / AC-5.1 detail-screen save flow stays the canonical save entry point; AC-34.2 explicitly names `RecipeStore.toggleSaved(id:)` as the shared seam. Implementing PR: T-590 (`feat/T-590-card-long-press-save`). **Coordination note:** T-580 (Search-tab tweaks, parallel branch) and T-610 (color refinement, parallel branch) are running in the same round-7 batch. Source-side: T-580 owns `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchView.swift` for round 7 — T-590 also edits that file (one `.contextMenu` modifier line); if T-580 lands first, T-590 rebases on top and re-applies its single-line edit. T-610 touches `DODColor` tokens / asset catalog, independent of T-590's source territory. Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` / `backlog.md` are expected at merge time — the rebaser picks the next free CL/US/T slot (T-590 originally reserved CL-59 but renumbered to CL-60 at merge time after T-610 landed first on CL-59; T-580 will pick the next free slot when it lands).

- **CL-61 (US-35 / T-620, 2026-05-27)** — **Explicit Download-for-offline is semantically distinct from Save: Download is the "pre-cook stash" path (offline access without bookmarking); Save remains the "remember for later" path (which already auto-downloads per AC-5.2). The two flags are independent, idempotent, and share the same underlying eviction-pin mechanism on the cached image bytes.** Round-7 backlog "Download for offline viewing button" item, with the spec question "do Download and Save share storage? Are they separate concepts in the UI?" resolved as **yes-and-yes with a deliberate UX distinction**. **The UX distinction.** Save (AC-4.7 bookmark glyph + AC-5.1 toggle + AC-5.2 auto-download) means "I want to remember this for later" — the recipe appears in the Saved tab (AC-5.3), pinned from LRU eviction (NFR-1), and the hero image is auto-pre-downloaded within 5 seconds of saving (AC-5.2). Download (the new `square.and.arrow.down` glyph) means "I want to use this without network for a specific upcoming use" — say, a camping trip, a kitchen with spotty wifi, or a friend's house where the user knows they'll be offline. The "pre-cook stash" framing is the mental model: the user grabs a handful of recipes before a trip without committing them to their long-term Saved list. From the persistence layer's perspective, both Save and Download pin the recipe text + the hero image bytes from eviction; the difference is the in-app surface where the user manages each: the Saved tab (US-5) for the Save list, and the recipe-detail nav-bar glyph for the Download flag (no parallel Download tab in v1 — see deferred-work below). **The independent-flags decision.** `CachedRecipe` gets a new optional `downloadedAt: Date?` field, distinct from the existing `isSaved: Bool` field. Both pin from LRU eviction in `RecipeStore.evictIfNeeded()` (the existing eviction predicate is `isSaved == false`; the new predicate becomes `isSaved == false && downloadedAt == nil`). Both Save and Download can be set independently: a recipe can be Downloaded without being Saved (the user wants offline access but not a bookmark), Saved without an explicit Download tap (the existing AC-5.2 auto-download path covers it), or Both (explicitly downloaded AND saved — the redundant tap is idempotent per AC-35.4). Unsaving a recipe that was *also* explicitly downloaded keeps it pinned (the `downloadedAt` flag survives `toggleSaved` because the two fields don't interact; the eviction predicate just OR-checks them). **Why independent flags rather than a single "pinned" boolean.** A single `isPinned: Bool` field would conflate the two intents at the data layer and force the UI to encode "where the pin came from" in some other surface (e.g. by checking whether the recipe still appears in `savedRecipes()`). That couples two unrelated UX features and makes future refinements (a "Downloaded" tab, a "Recently downloaded" sort, an explicit Remove-download affordance) significantly harder. The two-flag pattern lets each affordance evolve independently. **The idempotency decision.** Tapping Download on an already-downloaded recipe (`downloadedAt != nil`) OR on a saved recipe (`isSaved == true` — AC-5.2 has already pre-downloaded it) is a no-op semantically: the snackbar surfaces "Already downloaded" copy per AC-35.3, the `downloadedAt` timestamp is left at its existing value (not refreshed — a fresh timestamp on a re-tap would leak the tap as a behavioural side-channel the user can't observe, and adds nothing the user cares about), and no second hero-image fetch is issued (saves CDN bandwidth on a pure no-op). The button is NOT visually disabled — re-tapping is the discoverability path for the snackbar ("Already downloaded"), and a disabled button hides the state from VoiceOver users who can't see the glyph variant. Instead, the accessibility label flips from "Download for offline use" to "Downloaded for offline use" so VoiceOver users know the state without sighted-only chrome. **Why snackbar copy "Already downloaded" rather than "Already saved" or "Already on device":** the user's mental model on the download path is "I'm trying to download this"; the snackbar should answer in the same vocabulary they used to take the action. "Already saved" leaks the AC-5.2 mechanism (a user who has the recipe saved-and-auto-downloaded doesn't necessarily think of it as "already downloaded" — they think of it as "saved, which is even better") but the snackbar is acknowledging that the offline-availability contract is already satisfied. "Already on device" is too technical. The chosen copy splits the difference: it confirms the user's action's intent ("this recipe is available offline") without describing the mechanism, and works for both pure-downloaded and saved-and-auto-downloaded states. **Why the image bytes share `CachedImage.pinnedToSavedRecipeID` rather than introduce a new `CachedImage.pinnedToDownloadedRecipeID`.** The existing field on `CachedImage` is named for its single original consumer (saved-recipe pre-download per AC-5.2), but its semantic is simply "this image row is pinned from eviction; here is which recipe id pinned it so we can unpin when that recipe is unpinned." For v1 we treat the field as the general pin marker — both Save and Download write the recipe id into it. This avoids a schema change on `CachedImage` (which would mean another `SchemaV3` model bump or a `SchemaV4` introduction) for what is functionally the same pin semantic. Renaming the field would be a destructive migration (rule R-5 violation per MIGRATION.md); leaving the name historical-but-overloaded is the smaller correct choice. If a future story needs to distinguish "image is pinned because this recipe is saved" vs. "image is pinned because this recipe is explicitly downloaded" — e.g. to evict the image when the user unsaves but keeps the explicit download — we add a second optional field (`pinnedToDownloadedRecipeID: Int?`) as an additive schema bump. Today, the eviction predicate doesn't care which flag pinned the row; the unpin path is "if both recipes are gone, evict." **Why no analytics event (`recipeDownloaded(recipeID:)`).** Constitution §9's allowlist is deliberately tight. Adding a new event requires a constitution amendment and a TelemetryDeck-questionnaire revisit; the gain is "we can count how often the explicit Download path is used vs. the implicit AC-5.2 auto-download path." The data is interesting but not actionable in v1 — there's no decision the team would make differently knowing the ratio (the explicit-download path is purely additive, and removing it because it's lightly used would punish the exact "camping trip" cohort it's designed for). If usage signals emerge that warrant the event (e.g. the camping-trip cohort starts asking for a Downloads tab, suggesting we'd want to size that work), the event lands as part of that follow-up story. **What CL-61 does NOT touch:** the Save flow (AC-4.7 / AC-5.1 unchanged — the bookmark glyph, the toggle behaviour, the AC-5.2 auto-download, and the saved-recipes widget publisher all stay as-is); the Share flow (AC-4.8 unchanged); the `CachedImage.pinnedToSavedRecipeID` field (semantic-overloaded but name-stable per the schema-discipline rationale above); the existing eviction predicate's saved-recipe protection (the new predicate is additive — `downloadedAt != nil` adds a second pin condition; saved recipes stay pinned exactly the same way they did before this change); `Recipe` Domain type (the new flag lives on `CachedRecipe` for now and is exposed via a new `RecipeStore.isDownloaded(id:)` accessor — adding `downloadedAt` to the Domain `Recipe` would force the WP REST → Domain mapping path to populate it from nowhere, which is wrong; the flag is local-state-only); `RecipeListItem` (same reason — the flag is not part of the list-fetch shape, only the local cache); the `SavedRecipesWidgetPublisher` (the widget surfaces `isSaved` recipes; explicit downloads don't surface in the widget); any analytics event; any new deep-link case (no `dod://downloads` parser case in v1 — the explicit-download list isn't a tab); the snackbar mechanism (reuses the existing `RecipeDetailView.snackbar` overlay + `RecipeDetailViewModel.snackbarMessage` field — same auto-dismiss timer); haptic feedback (the existing `.sensoryFeedback(.success, trigger: viewModel.isSaved)` on the body is bound to the Save flag; the Download flag does NOT trigger a haptic in v1 because the snackbar is the confirmation surface and a haptic would feel like a save — out of scope for this story; future story can add a download-specific haptic if user feedback surfaces it). **Deferred work (out of scope for v1.0, captured here for future stories):** **(a) Explicit Remove-download affordance.** A user who explicitly downloaded a recipe for a one-time trip has no in-app way to remove the download in v1 — the recipe stays pinned forever unless they ALSO save it and then unsave it (which doesn't even work because the `downloadedAt` flag would survive the toggle per AC-35.5). The minimal path to add this is a long-press menu on the download button itself ("Remove download" destructive item) that sets `downloadedAt = nil` and re-runs `evictImagesIfNeeded()`. Filed as a backlog candidate; not blocking v1. **(b) Downloads tab.** No parallel tab for explicitly-downloaded recipes in v1 — the user opens the recipe to see the download state. If the explicit-download path proves popular, a future story could add either (1) a Downloads tab parallel to Saved, or (2) a filter/sort in the Saved tab that includes "Downloaded but not saved" rows. The two-flag schema chosen here supports both directions without further migration. **(c) Bulk download (e.g. "download this whole category").** Out of scope — would require a multi-select UI on category recipe lists. The per-recipe explicit-download tap is the v1 primitive; bulk operations come later if needed. **(d) Download progress indicator.** v1 fires the download and shows the snackbar on completion; a future story could add a determinate progress indicator on the nav-bar glyph for users with slow connections. The hero-image download is the only network call (recipe text is already in-memory from the JSON-LD parse), so the progress affordance is a single-image affordance — manageable but not free. **Considered:** reuse the existing `RecipeDetailDependencies.toggleSaved(id:)` path with a "silent save then silent unsave" pattern to achieve the offline-pin without surfacing in the Saved tab. Rejected — the Save toggle fires telemetry (`recipeSaved` event), triggers the haptic, fires the widget snapshot publisher, and surfaces "Saved for offline" snackbar copy; the user would see a Save-flavored UX for a Download action. The two intents need separate surfaces. **Considered:** add `downloadedAt: Date?` to the Domain `Recipe` struct instead of the persistence-layer `CachedRecipe`. Rejected — the flag is local-only state (the WP REST API has no knowledge of whether a device explicitly downloaded a recipe); adding it to the Domain struct would force every Domain consumer (Feed view model, Search view model, widget snapshot decoder, App Intents entity) to either populate it from nowhere or leave it at a default that doesn't reflect persistence reality. The cleaner shape is to keep it on `CachedRecipe` and expose it via a new accessor (`RecipeStore.isDownloaded(id:)`) that the detail view model reads. **Considered:** merge Save and Download into a single "Pin for offline" action (the bookmark glyph remains but does both jobs). Rejected — this is exactly the conflation CL-61 exists to resolve. Save and Download are semantically distinct in the user's mind; collapsing them inverts the UX clarity the round-7 backlog item explicitly asked for. **Considered:** make Download a long-press on the existing Save button. Rejected — long-press on a nav-bar button is a non-discoverable gesture (no system convention establishes it), and the round-7 backlog explicitly asked for "a third nav-bar action" (a visible glyph). The discoverability of a third visible button beats the visual-clean-up of a hidden long-press by a wide margin for this story's audience. **Considered:** put the Download affordance in a Settings page (e.g. "Manage offline downloads") rather than on the recipe detail. Rejected — the user's mental model is "I'm on a recipe, I want it offline." A Settings detour adds two taps for what should be a one-tap action. **Considered:** require a confirmation dialog before downloading ("This will download ~5MB. Continue?"). Rejected — the hero-image fetch is the same network cost as the existing AC-5.2 auto-download on save, and AC-5.2 has no confirmation dialog. Asymmetric confirmation would feel inconsistent. **Considered:** add a `recipeDownloaded(recipeID:)` analytics event. Rejected per the "no new analytics event" decision above — re-readers reaching this point should note the trade-off and the trigger condition (usage-signal emergence) for revisiting it. **Spec/trace:** US-35 (the new story added in this PR — AC-35.1..AC-35.6); the existing AC-4.7 / AC-5.1 (Save) flow stays unchanged; AC-4.8 (Share) stays unchanged; AC-5.2 (saved-recipe auto-download) stays unchanged and is the contract the "Already downloaded" snackbar copy keys off when the recipe is saved; NFR-1 (offline-first LRU cache) is extended by the additive `downloadedAt`-pins eviction predicate. Implementing PR: T-620 (`feat/T-620-download-button`). **Coordination note:** T-630 (Settings expansion, parallel branch) and T-640 (Recipes & Articles rename, parallel branch) are running in the same round-7 batch. Source-side: T-620 touches `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/**` + `Packages/DODPersistence/**` (the view, view model, dependencies, store schema/method additions); T-630 touches `Packages/DODFeatureSettings/**` (a separate package); T-640 touches `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/**` + `App/` (the rename surfaces). Source-side collisions: zero. Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` / `backlog.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL slot (T-620 reserves CL-61; T-630 and T-640 will pick the next free slots as they land).

- **CL-58 (T-600, 2026-05-26)** — **Add L5 end-to-end as a fifth test layer with selective gating; the existing four-layer "every PR" pyramid stays unchanged.** Framing mirrors CL-30 (US-18 audit) and CL-39 (US-23 audit): the deliverable is the `test-pyramid-audit.md` matrix + the gap finding, not a feature implementation. **Decision: add L5 (XCUITest, scheme `DODAppE2ETests`, target sources `UITests/E2E/**`) as the fifth test layer.** Trigger surfaces: (1) `pull_request` only when the PR carries the `e2e` label, (2) `push` to `main`, (3) `workflow_dispatch`, (4) nightly cron `0 7 * * *`. The `ci-required` aggregator treats a skipped `test-e2e` as success — so a PR without the `e2e` label is not blocked by L5. **Why selective gating, not "every PR" like L1–L4:** two reasons. **(1) CI quota.** This repo is on a macOS-minutes budget that the audit doc surfaces explicitly. A 5-journey seed suite × ~25s per journey × ~3-minute scheme-boot overhead is ~5 minutes per push. With ~3 PRs/day that's 30+ min/day, ~15 hours/month — the per-PR cost would consume a meaningful slice of the monthly cap on changes that have no behavioral risk a user-journey test could catch. **(2) Signal-to-noise.** Pure-docs PRs, lint fixes, single-package refactors, and CI-yaml tweaks have no behavioral failure mode L5 can find. Running E2E on those PRs adds wall-clock without adding coverage value. The label is the lowest-effort signal a PR author can pass to CI ("this change touches a navigation surface, please run the user journeys"). The eight-surface checklist that warrants the label lives in `CONTRIBUTING.md` § "Does my PR need E2E?" and is the same checklist the audit doc enumerates. **Why XCUITest over Detox / Maestro / WebDriverAgent:** these are the three iOS-eligible end-to-end test harnesses outside Apple's own. None of them earn their dependency cost on this codebase. **Detox** is a React-Native-first harness — strong for hybrid apps, weak for pure SwiftUI; introducing it would force a Node toolchain into CI for zero coverage gain. **Maestro** is a YAML-DSL-based runner that's easier to write but harder to debug than XCUITest — debugging requires switching tools (Maestro Studio for record/playback, Xcode for symbolication), and the YAML DSL doesn't give XCTest assertion ergonomics on `XCUIElement.waitForExistence(timeout:)` or `XCTAttachment`-based screenshot capture. **WebDriverAgent** is the Appium-stack remote-driver runner, which is the right tool for cross-platform mobile testing but adds a server-side WDA process and an Appium client SDK for a single-platform repo. **XCUITest** is the framework the existing L3 smoke tests already use; the author skillset transfers (no new mental model), the assertion vocabulary is already known (`XCTAssertTrue(... .waitForExistence(timeout: …))`), the failure-artifact pipeline (`*.xcresult` upload) is already wired in CI, and adding a parallel scheme + target is a `project.yml` edit, not a dependency addition. The "no new test-harness dep family" principle here echoes constitution §3's "default answer is 'no new dependency'" — adding Detox/Maestro/WDA would need a documented gap that XCUITest can't fill, and the audit doc surfaces no such gap. **Why exactly these four trigger surfaces:** (1) `pull_request` + `e2e` label is the surface where the L5 coverage actually adds review-quality signal — a PR author who knows their change touches a navigation / persistence / widget / Cook Mode / etc. surface gets the journey suite run on their branch before merge. (2) `push` to `main` is the safety net — even if every PR is gated by the label correctly, a regression can slip through if the author forgot the label OR if two unrelated PRs merge in sequence and only their combination breaks a journey; the post-merge run on `main` catches both. (3) `workflow_dispatch` is the manual escape hatch — useful when investigating a regression that's already merged, when verifying a hotfix branch locally before opening the PR, or when running the suite against an arbitrary commit during a release-prep audit. (4) nightly cron `0 7 * * *` is the environment-drift detector — pairs with `.github/workflows/nightly-live-api.yml`'s existing cron at the same time, so both early-warning surfaces fire at the same wall clock and the morning runner pool sees them together rather than competing. **What CL-58 deliberately says NO to:** **(a) No L5 visual-regression coverage.** L4 owns pixel-level baselines via `pointfreeco/swift-snapshot-testing`; XCUITest can't read pixels reliably and adding screenshot-comparison to L5 would duplicate L4's job at lower fidelity. **(b) No POSTs to dutchovendaddy.com from L5.** The existing L2 layer is read-only (it never POSTs comments or ratings); the same constraint applies to L5. The comments/ratings submission path will get L5 coverage against a stubbed `WPCommentsClient` in T-611 once T-610's host-side `FakeAppDependencies` lands. **(c) No Android E2E layer.** This is an iOS-only repo (constitution §2 names iPhone + iPad only). An Android E2E suite would require a parallel app codebase that doesn't exist. **(d) No moving L1–L4 to selective gating.** L1 / L2 / L3 / L4 keep their "every PR" or "nightly" cadence per constitution §6; L5 is the only layer with the new selective-gating model. The audit doc explicitly does NOT restructure the existing four layers — it audits them, adds L5 atop, and tracks two follow-up tasks (T-610 host-side fakes, T-612 de-facto-E2E migration out of L3) without forcing them into the L5 cluster. **What CL-58 ADDS to AC-T4:** nothing — REG bullets continue to lock specific failure modes at L1/L2/L3 per AC-T4; L5 journeys may pin acceptance criteria too but are not required to (the seed five journeys deliberately overlap with existing L3 surfaces, providing a fidelity floor without claiming the corresponding REG entries). **Test-data strategy:** launch via `XCUIApplication.launchArguments += ["-DOD_E2E_MODE=1"]` + `launchEnvironment["DOD_E2E_MODE"] = "1"`. The host app reads the flag at boot via a new branch in `App/DODApp.swift` `applyTestLaunchOverrides()` that records the flag into a process-wide boolean. **Phase 1 (T-602):** the boolean is recorded but does not yet swap dependencies — the seed journeys in T-603 drive against the existing production code paths (same as the existing L3 smoke job today). **Phase 2 (T-610 follow-up):** wire a `FakeAppDependencies` so when `DOD_E2E_MODE=1` the host app swaps in canned fixtures — no live network, no SwiftData state from previous runs. The audit doc tracks this gap explicitly; landing the seed journeys against the live blog mirrors the existing L3 smoke contract and is the documented Phase 1 trade-off. **Considered:** ship L5 as a new layer in L4's `DODDesignSystem` test scheme so all extra tests run on every PR. Rejected — collapses the selective-gating policy that motivates L5 in the first place; the per-PR cost was exactly the cost the audit doc surfaced as the gap. **Considered:** ship L5 inside the existing `DODAppUITests` scheme behind a launch-argument flag. Rejected — would require splitting the scheme's CI invocation into two passes (the smoke pass + the E2E pass), and the GitHub Actions YAML's path-filter + label-conditional logic is much cleaner with two distinct schemes (`DODAppUITests` always-on, `DODAppE2ETests` label-gated). **Considered:** require an `e2e` label on every PR by default (CI fails if the label is missing). Rejected — that's the opposite of the audit doc's framing; the audit's point is that most PRs don't need E2E, and forcing the author to add a label every time inverts the friction the gating policy is trying to remove. **Considered:** run L5 on every PR at first and dial back later based on usage. Rejected — the macOS-minutes budget surfaced by the audit is the binding constraint; "dial back later" is not a real plan when the budget is already tight today. **Considered:** use a single label `test-all` that gates both the existing `test-snapshots-designsystem` job AND the new `test-e2e` job. Rejected — the snapshot job runs on every PR by design (constitution §6 L4 is "every PR"); collapsing the snapshot gating with the E2E gating would weaken the L4 contract. **Spec/trace:** constitution §6 (the L5 row + selective-gating paragraph added in this PR); AC-T5 (the new spec criterion added to `spec.md`'s Test pyramid section). Implementing PRs: T-600 (audit doc + Phase 11 task scaffolding), T-601 (constitution + spec + this CL), T-602 (target + scheme + wiring), T-603 (seed five journeys), T-604 (CI workflow), T-605 (CONTRIBUTING.md policy). Follow-ups: T-610 (host-side fake-dependencies switch), T-611 (comments/ratings POST-path coverage), T-612 (migrate the de-facto-E2E methods out of `UITests/SmokeTests.swift`) — all deliberately deferred per the audit doc's followups section.

- **CL-62 (US-36 / T-630, 2026-05-27)** — **Settings page expansion ships five standard rows (Notifications, Appearance, Default Share Format, Clear Cached Recipe Images, Share Anonymous Usage Data); each row's persistence + UI lands in v1, with one explicit deferral (Notifications APNs wire-up → T-631).** Round-7 backlog "Settings page expansion" item graduated: the user's framing was "round out the Settings page with the rest of what users expect from a recipe-app settings surface." The backlog enumerated a candidate list (notification preferences, default Cook Mode behavior, default share format, data + cache management, accessibility shortcuts, telemetry opt-out, legal links) and called the "audit-and-pick" the hardest part. **Decision: pick the five rows above and defer the rest.** **Why these five and not others:** **(1) Notifications** — every modern recipe app has it; Spencer's explicit ask. Defaulting OFF respects the constitution's "no surprise data use" posture (NFR-3) and avoids needing the system permission prompt in v1 (T-631 will request authorization). The UI-only-for-v1 split is the same pattern US-32 / T-550 used for the metric-units toggle (ship the persistence round-trip; defer the actual consumer) — keeps the PR bounded to one feature module and makes the consumer (APNs registration, `UNUserNotificationCenter.current().requestAuthorization`, push payload handling, foreground vs background delivery) its own reviewable task. **(2) Appearance** — iOS users expect an appearance picker even when the system already provides one in the OS-level Settings app; the in-app picker honors the "match what users expect" framing the backlog called out. Drives `.preferredColorScheme(...)` on `RootView`, which is the only seam SwiftUI provides for app-level appearance override (no asset-catalog change, no token swap — the system Light/Dark variants of every `DODColor.*` token are already authored, and `.preferredColorScheme(.dark)` just forces the SwiftUI environment value). Defaulting to "Match System" preserves today's behavior — users who don't touch the picker see no change. **(3) Default Share Format** — the backlog candidate list named it explicitly ("default share format (link vs full text)") and AC-6.2's existing `recipeShared` flow has a natural extension point (the existing `ShareLink(item: viewModel.canonicalURL)` could become `ShareLink(items: ...)` with both the URL and the excerpt-derived text once the flag is consumed). Defaulting to "Just the link" preserves AC-6.2's current behavior byte-for-byte. The flag is persisted in T-630; the share-payload composition that consumes the flag is deliberately deferred (the recipe excerpt is already in `RecipeListItem.excerpt` / `Recipe.excerpt`, so the wire-up is a one-line edit at the `ShareLink` call site once the flag is ready to be consumed — a future task picks that up when product asks for it). **(4) Clear Cached Recipe Images** — the backlog candidate list named "data + cache management" and Spencer's framing called for a button (not a toggle — it's an action, not a preference). The cache-clear seam (`RecipeStore.clearImageCache()`) is a new public method on the `@ModelActor` that fetches every unpinned `CachedImage`, sums the bytes, deletes the rows + the App Group file bridge mirrors, and returns the freed-MB count. Pinned images survive — they belong to saved recipes (AC-5.2's offline pre-download contract). The snackbar message ("Freed X.X MB" / "Cache was already clear.") gives the user immediate feedback so the button doesn't feel like a no-op when the cache was already empty. **(5) Share Anonymous Usage Data** — constitution §9 explicitly notes "have a v0.9 TestFlight build with telemetry **toggle-off** ready in case" (the R-3 risk row in plan.md §10). T-630 implements that toggle-off path. **Why default ON (not OFF):** the constitution's existing posture is opt-out — users are informed of the telemetry collection via the App Privacy questionnaire ("Usage Data — Product Interaction — not linked to user, not used for tracking") and given the ability to disable it, but the default is on because the telemetry is genuinely anonymous (no IDFA, no PII, hashed search queries) and the analytics signal is load-bearing for product decisions. An opt-in default would zero out the analytics dataset for the ~95% of users who never touch settings. **Why gate inside `TelemetryDeckTransport` and not inside `Telemetry.shared.send(_:)`:** the `Telemetry` facade is provider-agnostic — it forwards every event to whatever transport is installed. Test fixtures install `RecordingTelemetryTransport` to capture events for L1 assertions; if the gate lived in the facade, the test fixture would also short-circuit and the assertion harness would break. Putting the gate in the production transport (`TelemetryDeckTransport`) means: (a) tests still record every event (the recording transport doesn't know about the gate), (b) the production wire path is the only thing that short-circuits, (c) the gate is a one-line read of `UserDefaults.standard.object(forKey: "dod.settings.telemetryEnabled")` at every `send(_:)` call (so flipping the toggle takes effect immediately on the next event, no app-restart needed). **What CL-62 does NOT touch:** US-32 (the metric-units toggle, About link, version footer — all preserved as-is), constitution §9's allowlist (no new events, no new App Privacy categories — the gate operates on the existing events), constitution §3's dependency list (no new SDKs — TelemetryDeck is the only analytics dep and stays so), the `Telemetry.shared` facade (no API change — gate is internal to `TelemetryDeckTransport`), `RecordingTelemetryTransport` (test fixture is unchanged — still captures every event), the `AnalyticsEvent` enum (no new cases), the deep-link grammar, the widget snapshot wire format, the persistence schema (`CachedImage` rows are unchanged; only the public-method surface of `RecipeStore` gains `clearImageCache()`), `RecipeStore.cacheImage` / `evictImagesIfNeeded` / `WidgetImageBridge.writeImage` / `WidgetImageBridge.deleteImage` (existing methods are unchanged; `clearImageCache()` reuses them). **What CL-62 ADDS to the test pyramid:** L1 unit tests in three packages — `SettingsViewModelTests` (extended) for UserDefaults round-trip of the four new keys (`notificationsEnabled`, `appearance`, `shareFormat`, `telemetryEnabled`), the snackbar message formatter for the freed-MB count, and the no-op cache-clear branch; `RecipeStoreTests` (extended) for `clearImageCache()` purging unpinned rows while preserving pinned rows and returning the correct freed-byte total; `TelemetryTests` (extended) for the production transport's gate behavior (gated transport short-circuits when the flag is OFF, sends normally when the flag is ON or absent — defaults ON via the `object(forKey:) ?? true` shape). L4 snapshot baselines for the expanded `SettingsView` re-record (light + dark), replacing the T-550 baselines. **Considered:** ship every backlog candidate row (notifications, Cook Mode keep-screen, default share format, cache management, accessibility shortcuts, telemetry opt-out, legal links). Rejected — the backlog explicitly framed it as "audit-and-pick"; shipping all eight at once would bloat the PR past human-reviewable size and conflate decisions that warrant separate framing (e.g., accessibility shortcuts intersect with constitution §7 Dynamic Type and would benefit from a dedicated audit task; legal links require fetching policy text from somewhere — a separate WP REST or static-page task). The five picked are the ones with bounded scope and clear seams. **Considered:** ship Notifications with actual APNs wire-up in v1 (request authorization on toggle-ON, register for remote notifications, surface a backend opt-in mechanism). Rejected — the backend (dutchovendaddy.com WordPress) doesn't currently expose a push-token registration endpoint; setting that up is a paired iOS + WP-plugin effort (likely OneSignal or WPMobile.app's APNs bridge), which is too much for a Settings-row task. UI-only-for-v1 lets the toggle ship with its persistence contract while T-631 picks up the APNs + backend work. **Considered:** default Appearance to "Light" (the brand identity skews warm/cream, which historically reads as a light-mode palette). Rejected — every other iOS app defaults to Match System; defaulting to Light would surprise users who run their phone in Dark mode globally. Match-System honors the OS-level preference and the user can override it explicitly. **Considered:** make Default Share Format a three-option picker ("Link only" / "Link + recipe text" / "Link + recipe text + ingredients"). Rejected — the backlog framing offered two options (link vs full text); a third "ingredients" option would require a separate copy-curation pass (which subset of the recipe to include, how to truncate, what format) and the two-option scope is bounded enough to ship without that. The two-option pattern matches iOS Mail's "Plain Text" / "Rich Text" share-format pickers. **Considered:** Clear Cached Recipe Images shows a confirmation alert before clearing ("Are you sure? This will free X MB"). Rejected — the action is recoverable (re-loading the feed re-fetches the images), the freed-MB feedback already lands as a snackbar so the user sees the result, and adding a confirmation step would add friction for a low-stakes operation. iOS's own "Clear Cache" affordances in Safari and Settings don't surface confirmations either. **Considered:** Clear Cache deletes pinned images too (full wipe). Rejected — pinned images are the bytes belonging to saved recipes (AC-5.2's offline pre-download contract). Wiping them would make every saved recipe lose its hero image until the next online visit, which violates the AC-4.9 "fully usable offline if saved" promise. The unpinned-only sweep matches the existing `evictImagesIfNeeded()` semantics (which also exempts pinned rows per NFR-2). **Considered:** Share Anonymous Usage Data defaults OFF (opt-in). Rejected per the analytics-signal load-bearing reasoning above — the constitution's existing posture is opt-out by design (the App Privacy questionnaire's "Usage Data — Product Interaction — not linked to user, not used for tracking" disclosure is the user's notice; the toggle is the override). If a future App Store review or user-feedback signal calls for opt-in, that's a constitution amendment to §9 + a fresh CL flipping the default; v1 ships the opt-out the constitution promises. **Considered:** gate the telemetry path inside `Telemetry.shared.send(_:)` (the facade) rather than inside `TelemetryDeckTransport`. Rejected for the test-fixture-isolation reasoning above — the recording transport must continue to capture every event for L1 assertions, so the gate has to live in the production transport. **Considered:** gate via constructor injection (`TelemetryDeckTransport(isEnabled: () -> Bool)`). Rejected — the production transport is composed once at app start (`Telemetry.shared.start(appID:)`) and the `isEnabled` predicate would have to close over `UserDefaults.standard` anyway, so a direct read at `send(_:)` time is the same thing with one less layer of indirection. The test path swaps the entire transport via `Telemetry.shared.replaceTransport(_:)`, so injection doesn't buy testability. **Considered:** persist the freed-MB total in UserDefaults for trend display ("Last clear freed 47.2 MB"). Rejected — the snackbar already shows it; persisting the number invites a future feature ask ("show all-time freed total") that isn't in the backlog scope. **Considered:** add a sub-row to Clear Cache showing the current cache size before tapping ("Currently using 12.4 MB"). Rejected — computing the size requires the same `CachedImage` fetch the clear path already does, so showing it inline would either: (a) recompute on every view appearance (slow and async), or (b) compute once on appear and stale immediately after any cache write. The post-tap snackbar is the simplest correct surface and matches Apple's "Clear" UX patterns (no pre-tap size, post-tap confirmation only). **Considered:** put Notifications, Appearance, Share Format, Clear Cache, Telemetry all in a single `Section` (more compact). Rejected — `.insetGrouped` reads better when conceptually-distinct preferences live in their own sections (one section per concern, with the section's row(s) describing the concern). The expanded layout is the standard iOS Settings convention (look at iOS Settings → any app — concerns are sectioned). **Spec/trace:** US-36 (the new story added in this PR — AC-36.1..AC-36.8); US-32 / AC-32.* (the existing US that's not regressed — metric-units toggle + About link + version footer continue in place); AC-6.2 (the existing share flow whose default-payload contract is preserved when the new Share Format flag is at its default "Just the link"); AC-4.9 / AC-5.2 (the offline / pinned-image contract Clear Cache preserves by exempting pinned rows); constitution §9 (the telemetry posture the gate honors); NFR-2 (the unpinned-eviction semantics Clear Cache mirrors). Implementing PR: T-630 (`feat/T-630-settings-expansion`). Follow-up: T-631 (`feat(T-631):` — APNs wire-up, requests `UNUserNotificationCenter` authorization on Notifications toggle-ON, registers for remote notifications, handles the push payload routing through the existing `DeepLinkDispatcher`). **Coordination note:** T-620 (Download for offline viewing button — AC-4.7 / AC-4.8 territory on `RecipeDetailView.swift`) and T-640 (Recipes & Articles rename — `AppTab.swift` + `FeedView.swift` nav title + article-rendering path under CL-9 / AC-1.7 amendment) are running on parallel branches in this same round. Source-side collisions: zero. T-620 owns `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/**`; T-640 owns `App/AppTab.swift` + `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/FeedView.swift`'s navigation title + a new article-renderer package; T-630 owns `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/SettingsView.swift` + `SettingsViewModel.swift`, `Packages/DODPersistence/Sources/DODPersistence/RecipeStore+ImageCache.swift` (new `clearImageCache()` method), `Packages/DODAnalytics/Sources/DODAnalytics/TelemetryDeckTransport.swift` (new gate read), and `App/RootView.swift` (one `.preferredColorScheme(...)` modifier). Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` / `backlog.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL/US/T slot (T-630 reserves CL-62, US-36, T-630; T-620 and T-640 pick the next free slots when they land — likely CL-63 / CL-64 and US-37 / US-38 and T-620 / T-640 themselves). **Note on FeedView coordination with T-640:** T-630 does NOT touch `FeedView.swift`; the gear-icon NavigationLink target (`SettingsView()`) is unchanged. T-640's nav-title rename ("Recipes" → "Recipes & Articles") and the article-rendering path edit `FeedView.swift` independently. If T-640 lands first, T-630 has zero rebase work on that file; if T-630 lands first, T-640 has zero rebase work on `SettingsView.swift`. The two PRs are file-disjoint by design.

- **CL-63 (US-37 / T-640, 2026-05-27)** — **Recipes & Articles tab rename + article rendering for non-JSON-LD posts: reverses CL-9's "hide on missing JSON-LD" decision for the dominant case; classifies JSON-LD-less posts as articles and renders them via HTML body; CL-10's "post-mix audit" deferral closes here.** Round-7 backlog item "Recipes tab rename + content typing" (Spencer, 2026-05-26) calls for two coupled changes: (1) rename the Recipes tab to "Recipes & Articles" in both the tab-bar label and the screen's nav title, (2) when a post lacks parseable JSON-LD `Recipe` data (e.g. roundup posts like "Best Dutch Oven Recipes (30+ Tried and Tested Favorites)" — these are *articles*, not WPRM-instrumented recipes), render them as articles with their HTML body rather than hiding them from lists per the pre-existing CL-9 + AC-1.7 contract. **This CL amends CL-9, CL-10, AC-1.7, and AC-4.11.** The original wording in spec.md is preserved with strike-through formatting and an amendment line points back to this CL.

  **(1) Lineage to CL-9.** CL-9 (2026-05-23, "Posts with no parseable Recipe JSON-LD") resolved Phase-2's question with **hide-the-post-entirely**: a parse failure adds the post to a per-install blocklist, and subsequent list renders exclude it. The rationale at the time was the constitution's "user must never reach a detail screen that can't render" principle — if the detail screen has no JSON-LD to parse, the user lands on an empty state, which is worse than the post not appearing at all. CL-63 reverses this for the dominant case: the user-reported regression that motivated this story is that **the post page DOES exist and DOES carry readable HTML content** — the JSON-LD `@type: Recipe` block is missing because the post isn't a recipe (it's a roundup article), but the body is fully renderable. Hiding the post from lists wasn't the right answer; it was the *minimally-disruptive* answer at the time CL-9 closed because the alternative (article rendering) was out of Phase-2 scope. With the v1 surface area now covering recipe detail / ratings / comments / Cook Mode / widgets / etc., picking up the article surface is the right next move — and Spencer's round-7 framing makes the request explicit.

  **(2) Lineage to CL-10.** CL-10 (2026-05-23, "Non-recipe posts, roundups, articles") deferred the include-as-articles vs filter-out decision to a Phase-3 post-mix audit: "Plan step will sample ~50 recent posts from the live blog, count how many lack Recipe JSON-LD, and decide based on real data. If <5% are non-recipe, CL-9's hide-on-missing logic absorbs them. If higher, we revisit and may add an Article view." That audit never formally ran — the dev-team flow shifted into Phase 4+ implementation work and CL-10 sat as an open question. The CL-63 reversal closes CL-10 by adopting "we revisit and add an Article view" without re-running the audit: the user-reported regression IS the post-mix audit data (Spencer reported the specific roundup post by name, which counts as a real data point that non-recipe content is non-trivial). The chosen path is the same one CL-10's "if higher" branch pre-authorized — an Article view — so the spec lineage is clean.

  **(3) `PostKind` enum + domain mapping.** A new `PostKind` enum in `DODDomain` has two cases: `.recipe` (JSON-LD parse succeeded — the existing path through `RecipeDetailViewModel.fetchAndParse()` and `LoadState.ready` with a populated `Recipe.ingredients` + `Recipe.instructions`) and `.article` (JSON-LD parse failed AND the article-body extractor returned non-empty text — the new path). **Decision: surface the kind on the existing `Recipe` domain type via a `kind: PostKind` property rather than introducing a separate `Article` domain type.** **Why:** the list-row presentation (`RecipeCard` rendering `RecipeListItem` — title, excerpt, hero, total time) is identical for both kinds — the article's `RecipeListItem` carries the same hero + title + excerpt + (nil) totalTime fields the existing REST → domain mapping produces. Splitting `Article` and `Recipe` at the domain layer would force every list-rendering surface (Feed, Search, Categories, Saved) to deal with a sum type `enum PostListItem { case recipe(RecipeListItem); case article(ArticleListItem) }` — and every cell would need to discriminate on the kind to render the same visual output. The kind difference only matters on the detail screen, so the `kind` property on `Recipe` (with `.article` cases having empty `ingredients` / `instructions` and a populated `articleBodyHTML` string) is the lower-churn shape. The view model's `LoadState` carries the kind via a new `.article(Recipe)` case (parallel to `.ready` for `.recipe`); the view branches on the load state at one place — the existing `content` switch in `RecipeDetailView.body` — rather than threading kind through every helper. **Considered:** introduce `Article` as a sibling domain type with no `ingredients` / `instructions` / `servings` fields. Rejected — the list-row surfaces don't care about the kind, and adding a sum type at the domain layer forces every list surface to discriminate. The kind-on-Recipe approach keeps the list path identical and isolates the discrimination to the detail screen, which is where it semantically belongs.

  **(4) Article-body extraction strategy.** A new `ArticleBodyExtractor` enum in `DODSupport` exposes a single static function `extract(html: String) -> String` that walks the rendered HTML page (the same string `RecipePageFetcher.html(for:)` already fetches per CL-1) and pulls the article body text. **v1 strategy:** find the first `<div class="entry-content">` block (WordPress's standard article-body wrapper that Yoast / WPRM / most WP themes preserve), fall back to the first `<article>` block, fall back to the first `<main>` block, fall back to the body as a whole. Run `HTMLSanitizer.plainText(from:)` on the extracted slice to strip tags + decode entities + normalize whitespace. Return the resulting plain text (or empty string if extraction failed). **Why plain text for v1, not rich HTML:** the constitution's "default answer is no new dependency" + the existing `HTMLSanitizer` surface gives plain-text rendering for free. Rich-HTML rendering (preserve `<h2>` / `<h3>` / `<ul>` / `<a href>` formatting) needs either `NSAttributedString(data:options:documentAttributes:)` with `documentType: .html` (which loads WebKit on the main thread and has known iOS-version-specific font / color quirks) OR a third-party HTML-to-SwiftUI renderer (e.g. `github.com/AeroIcarus/SwiftMarkdownView`, but this falls under constitution §3's "no new SPM packages without plan-time approval"). Plain-text rendering satisfies the v1 spec contract ("the user can read the article") and is provably correct: the user sees the article body in readable typography, with paragraph breaks preserved (we double-up on `\n` between block-level elements during sanitization). Rich rendering is a v1.x follow-up that the user can ask for if the plain-text presentation feels too flat.

  **(5) Detail-screen branching + UI exclusions.** When `RecipeDetailViewModel.fetchAndParse()` runs and `parseJSONLD(...)` throws, the next step changes from "call `markJSONLDFailed(id:)` and transition to `.unavailable`" (the pre-T-640 path) to "call `ArticleBodyExtractor.extract(html:)`; if non-empty, build an article-kind `Recipe` (with `kind = .article`, populated `articleBodyHTML`, empty `ingredients` + `instructions`), call `mergeDetail(_:)` to persist, AND call `markJSONLDFailed(id:)` so the row's `jsonLDFailedAt` field marks it as an article (the field's semantic is reframed from "hidden" to "is article" — see decision 7 below). Then transition to `.article(recipe)` load state." If the article-body extraction *also* returns empty (the post page is genuinely unrenderable), fall through to the existing `.unavailable` path with the same snackbar + auto-pop behavior. **The view-layer branch:** `RecipeDetailView.body`'s existing switch gains a `case .article(let recipe):` arm that renders `ArticleDetailView(recipe: recipe)`. **What `ArticleDetailView` SHOWS:** hero image, title, published date, sanitized HTML body, nav-bar Share (AC-4.8 preserved), nav-bar Save (AC-4.7 preserved as affordance). **What `ArticleDetailView` does NOT show:** the ingredients section, the Cook Mode CTA (US-7), the servings stepper (US-31), the rating summary + composer (US-13), the comments composer (US-14), the related-recipes strip (US-4 / AC-4.6) — those are recipe-specific affordances. The exclusion list is deliberate and bounded — the article surface in v1 is "read the article body and share it."

  **(6) Article save semantics.** The nav-bar Save button is preserved on `ArticleDetailView`. The persistence schema gets a new optional column on `CachedRecipe`: `articleBodyHTML: String?` (default nil for recipe rows; populated for article rows on `mergeDetail(_:)`). The SwiftData migration is a fresh `SchemaV4` that adds the column (lightweight, additive optional field). The Saved tab (`SavedView`) shows saved articles alongside saved recipes per AC-5.3 — the row format is identical, and tapping a saved article opens `ArticleDetailView` with the cached HTML body.

  **(7) `CachedRecipe.jsonLDFailedAt` semantic reframe.** Pre-T-640: a non-nil `jsonLDFailedAt` row was hidden from list queries per CL-9 / AC-1.7. Post-T-640: a non-nil `jsonLDFailedAt` row means "this post is an article" — it's still shown in lists alongside recipes (AC-37.4). The list-query predicates in `RecipeStore.listItems(forIDs:)` and `RecipeStore.recentlyViewed(limit:)` drop the `jsonLDFailedAt == nil` clause. The pull-to-refresh `clearBlocklist()` call site is preserved. **Why reuse `jsonLDFailedAt` rather than add a `kind: String?` column:** the field's nullability already encodes the kind discriminator (nil = recipe, non-nil = article), and adding a parallel `kind` field would double-encode the same information.

  **(8) Tab rename surface inventory.** The rename touches exactly two strings: `App/AppTab.swift` `case .feed` `title` from "Recipes" to "Recipes & Articles" (bottom-tab label updates automatically via SwiftUI's `Label(systemImage:)`), and `Packages/DODFeatureFeed/Sources/DODFeatureFeed/FeedView.swift` `.navigationTitle("Recipes")` to `.navigationTitle("Recipes & Articles")`. Telemetry name (`AppTab.telemetryName == "feed"`), SF Symbol (`house`), tab ordering, every other user-facing surface is unchanged.

  **(9) L4 snapshot baseline scope.** The rename forces re-recording of the tab-bar baselines T-310 / T-334 originally established and any existing FeedView screen-level baselines that capture the nav title. The new `ArticleDetailView` adds new baselines (light + dark on iPhone 13 baseline at default Dynamic Type). New L1 tests cover the kind-classification path: JSON-LD success → `.recipe`, JSON-LD failure + article extractable → `.article`, both failures → `.unavailable`.

  **(10) What CL-63 explicitly does NOT touch:** the `RecipeRoute.recipe(item:)` grammar (one route, two detail-screen branches), the deep-link grammar (`dod://recipe/<id>` works for both kinds), the widget surfaces, the analytics events (no `articleViewed` event added — `recipeView(recipeID:)` fires from the same entry point regardless of kind), the `RecipePageFetcher` shape, the `JSONLDRecipeParser` shape (same throw on failure; the article branch is a *new* fallback layered on top), the `WidgetSnapshot` wire format, the search index (`CachedIngredient` rows are only populated by the JSON-LD merge path), the Saved tab's Cook Mode entry-point logic (CookMode is gated on `recipe.instructions.isEmpty` being false; articles trivially satisfy the "no Cook Mode" guard).

  **Implementing PR:** T-640 (`feat/T-640-recipes-and-articles`). **Coordination note:** T-620 (Download for offline button, parallel branch) touches `RecipeDetailView`. T-630 (Settings expansion, parallel branch) touches `SettingsView`. T-640 touches `FeedView` + `AppTab` + new `ArticleDetailView` + new `PostKind` + `RecipeDetailViewModel.fetchAndParse()` branch. No source-side collisions expected. Spec-file collisions at merge time are normal — T-640 reserves CL-63, US-37, T-640. T-650 (Gallery/List view toggle) is queued for after T-640 merges because T-650 also touches `FeedView` heavily.

- **CL-64 (US-38 / T-650, 2026-05-27)** — **Gallery ↔ List view toggle on Recipes & Articles + Search; the toggle button's icon reflects the CURRENT layout, not the destination — opposite of typical iOS convention, per Spencer's explicit direction.** Round-7 backlog "Layout toggle" item graduated. A new nav-bar button on both the Recipes & Articles tab (`FeedView`) and the Search tab (`SearchView`) flips a shared `@AppStorage("dod.recipeListLayout")`-backed preference between two layouts: **gallery** (the existing 2-column `LazyVGrid` per CC-9, default for new + migrating users) and **list** (a new denser single-column variant with a smaller hero thumbnail + title + 1-line excerpt + total-time chip on the trailing edge). Same persisted state on both tabs.

  **(1) Icon convention — the deliberate deviation from typical iOS.** The toggle button's `Image(systemName:)` shows the **current** layout (gallery → `square.grid.2x2`, list → `list.bullet`), NOT the destination the user will reach by tapping. This is the **opposite** of the typical iOS convention where a toggle's icon previews what the tap will switch the UI to (e.g. Safari's reader-mode button shows the article-icon while you're in the gallery view, because tapping it takes you to reader mode). Spencer's round-7 backlog wording captured the request explicitly: *"if user is in gallery view, the button displays the icon `square.grid.2x2`"* — and re-confirmed the direction at task time. The trade-off is real: the icon doesn't telegraph the destination, so a user unfamiliar with the affordance has to tap once to see what changes. The mitigation is that the accessibility label DOES tell the user the destination ("Layout, gallery — switch to list" / "Layout, list — switch to gallery"), so VoiceOver users + Switch Control users always know what tapping will do.
