T-630's opt-out toggle gates both events the same way it gates every other event.** Two events cover the shopping-list lifecycle: **(1) `shoppingListItemAdded(recipeID:aisle:)`** — fires once per `ShoppingListItem` row inserted via AC-39.2 (single-recipe path) or AC-39.3 (bulk-add path). Payload: an integer recipe ID (already telemetered today via `recipeView` / `recipeSaved`) + the `Aisle` enum's raw string (`"produce"` / `"dairy"` / `"meatSeafood"` / `"bakery"` / `"frozen"` / `"pantry"` / `"spices"` / `"beverages"` / `"other"`). **(2) `shoppingListShared(itemCount:)`** — fires once when the `UIActivityViewController` presents per AC-39.7. Payload: integer count of items in the list at share time. **Why these two events and not more:** the goal of v1 telemetry is the minimum signal needed to (a) measure the feature's adoption (Add events count → how many users / how often / how many rows per session), (b) measure share-out (Share events count → how often the list leaves the device, mapped to use-case intent), and (c) measure the aisle classifier's miss rate (the `.other` share of Add events → CL-67 escalation trigger). That's two events. More granular events (per-toggle, per-clear, per-section-expand) add noise without changing the v1 product decisions. **Why aisle is on the payload but ingredient text is NOT:** raw ingredient text is user-facing content from the recipe author (Ned), but it's also a privacy surface — the user could be embarrassed by their shopping habits ("I'm putting cheap vodka on every list, please don't tell anyone"). The constitution §9 rule is "no raw user input strings" — the ingredient text isn't strictly the user's input, but it's the user's *consumption pattern*, and an aisle is the coarse-grained projection that captures product-relevant signal (which sections of the store do users shop?) without leaking what specific items they buy. The aisle's ~9-value enum is the canonical example of "coarse-grained category telemetry." **Why recipe ID is on the payload:** it's already on the §9 allowlist via `recipeView` / `recipeSaved` / `recipeRated` / `recipeCommentSubmitted` — the integer post ID is non-PII and identifies content on the public blog. Including it lets us join `shoppingListItemAdded` against `recipeView` to answer "of users who saved a recipe, what fraction added it to their shopping list?" — which is the v1 success metric. **Why `itemCount` on Share and not `aisleCount` / `recipeCount` / `checkedCount`:** `itemCount` is the simplest signal that tells us "how big was the list when the user shared." Larger lists indicate stronger feature adoption (the user is using it as a real planning tool, not a one-off). `aisleCount` / `recipeCount` / `checkedCount` would add fidelity but at telemetry-overhead cost (each event carries more bytes, the dashboards have more dimensions to chart) without changing the v1 success criteria. **What constitution §9 amendment looks like:** the existing §9 allowlist gains two entries in the "Events tracked are limited to:" sentence. Specifically: `shoppingListItemAdded(recipeID:aisle:)` (added 2026-05-27 by the US-39 / T-687 amendment — integer post ID + coarse aisle category, no raw text) and `shoppingListShared(itemCount:)` (added 2026-05-27 by the US-39 / T-687 amendment — integer item count at share time). The App Privacy questionnaire mapping table per §9 is unchanged — both events fall under the existing "Usage Data — Product Interaction" row. **T-630 opt-out coverage:** the `dod.settings.telemetryEnabled` toggle per AC-36.5 gates `TelemetryDeckTransport.send(_:)` for ALL events; the two new shopping-list events route through the same transport and are gated identically. No new opt-out toggle needed. **Considered:** add a `shoppingListItemChecked(recipeID:aisle:)` event for the AC-39.5 toggle action. Rejected — the toggle is the most frequent user action on the screen, and telemetering every tap inflates event volume disproportionately to its signal value. If a Tier-2 task wants check-rate data later, it can graduate. **Considered:** add a `shoppingListCleared(itemCount:)` event for AC-39.6. Rejected — clear is rare and its signal is captured indirectly by the subsequent `shoppingListItemAdded` cluster (a user clears, then adds again; the rebuild pattern is visible). If retention metrics need direct clear signal later, it can graduate. **Considered:** include the `Aisle` enum on `shoppingListShared` too. Rejected — the share is a single user action; aggregating aisles into a single event would either send 9 booleans (clutter) or a sum string (redundant with `itemCount`). The v1 share telemetry stays simple. **Considered:** include a `entrySurface: String` parameter on `shoppingListItemAdded` to distinguish the three entry points per CL-73 (recipe-detail nav-bar vs. Saved-tab long-press vs. Saved-tab bulk path). Rejected for v1 — adds payload complexity for a metric we'd use to make a Tier-2 entry-surface decision (which surface drove more adoption?); without the Tier-2 task in scope, the signal would sit unused. If the entry-surface decision graduates, it adds the field at that time. **Spec/trace:** US-39 / AC-39.10 (the telemetry surface itself), constitution §9 (the allowlist amendment that lands paired with T-687). Implementing PR: T-687 (`feat/T-687-shopping-list-analytics`).

- **CL-76 (US-39 / T-684, 2026-05-27)** — **Empty-state copy: title `"Your shopping list is empty"`, message `"Tap a saved recipe and add its ingredients here"`, SF Symbol `cart`. Reuses the existing `EmptyState` component the Saved tab already uses (AC-5.8).** Empty states are load-bearing UX surfaces — the first frame a user sees when they tap into a feature with zero data, and the first signal to them about what the feature is for. Three drafts considered: **(a) `"Your shopping list is empty" / "Tap a saved recipe and add its ingredients here" / cart`** — direct, action-oriented, names the entry surface (saved recipes) the user should look for next. **(b) `"No items yet" / "Add items from any recipe to start your shopping list" / list.bullet`** — generic recipe-app empty-state copy; "from any recipe" is broader than v1's actual entry surfaces (CL-73 says Feed/Search are NOT v1 entry surfaces). **(c) `"Build your shopping list" / "Combine ingredients from your saved recipes — sorted by aisle, ready to share" / cart.badge.plus`** — marketing-flavored; pitches the feature's benefit but is wordy for an empty state. **Decision: ship (a) for v1.** **Why (a):** matches Saved-tab's existing empty-state copy pattern (AC-5.8: `"Saved recipes appear here / Tap the bookmark on any recipe to save it for offline."`) — the dominant feature surface that the user is already pattern-matched to. The phrasing is direct ("Your shopping list is empty" — literal description) and the message gives the user a next action ("Tap a saved recipe and add its ingredients here" — explicit instruction). The cart glyph signals the feature identity without being cluttered. **Why NOT (b):** "from any recipe" is technically inaccurate per CL-73 — the v1 entry surfaces don't include Feed and Search tab long-press. Promising "any recipe" overstates the v1 surface and would confuse a user who tries to long-press a Feed-tab card and doesn't see the menu item. **Why NOT (c):** the marketing flavor doesn't match the rest of the app's voice (the existing empty states are functional, not promotional — see Saved, Search "No results", offline banner copy). The benefits framing belongs in the App Store description, not in the in-app empty state. **Why "saved recipe" specifically and not "recipe":** matches the CL-73 entry-surface decision. The long-press menu is bounded to Saved-tab cards; the canonical v1 flow is "save a recipe → tap into it → add its ingredients." If the user hasn't saved any recipes yet, they hit the Saved-tab empty state first (AC-5.8) and don't reach the shopping-list empty state until they have at least one saved recipe — at which point "Tap a saved recipe" is the right next action. **What `EmptyState` reuse looks like:** the existing `EmptyState` component in `DODDesignSystem` takes `title: LocalizedStringKey`, `message: LocalizedStringKey`, `systemImage: String` parameters (a standard SwiftUI pattern). `ShoppingListView` instantiates it with the AC-39.1 copy values. No new component, no new design tokens — the v1 empty state is data, not a primitive. **Considered:** include a "Browse saved recipes" button in the empty state that navigates back to the Saved tab. Rejected — the user is *already* in the Saved-tab → Shopping list flow (the shopping list pushes from the Saved tab on iPhone, or appears as a sidebar entry on iPad per AC-39.9), so the affordance is one back-tap away. Adding a button replicates the existing nav-stack affordance. **Considered:** include an animated illustration (a cart graphic that gently bounces, or an animated SF Symbol with `symbolEffect`). Rejected for v1 — the constitution §7 "Reduce Motion respected for any non-essential animation" rule applies; adding an animation would require a Reduce-Motion fallback path. Static SF Symbol is the bounded shape. If a Tier-2 visual-polish task graduates, it can add the animation with the fallback. **Considered:** localize the copy now (use `LocalizedStringKey` and add a Localizable.strings entry). Rejected — v1 is en-US only per the unstated baseline; adding localization scaffolding for one feature when the rest of the app is unlocalized is asymmetric. If localization graduates, this copy is part of the same sweep. **Spec/trace:** US-39 / AC-39.1 (the empty-state surface), CC-1 (accessibility — the copy respects Dynamic Type up to AX5 per the existing `EmptyState` component's contract). Implementing PR: T-684 (`feat/T-684-shopping-list-view`).

- **CL-77 (US-39 / T-682 / T-684, 2026-05-27)** — **Cross-recipe deduplication: same ingredient appearing in N recipes produces N separate rows for v1, NOT one merged row. Each row carries its source-recipe sub-label. This is the visible consequence of CL-70's "no quantity merging" decision rendered at the row-render layer.** A user who adds three recipes that each call for "yellow onion, diced" gets three rows in the Produce aisle, each labeled `(<recipe title>)`. **Why NOT one merged row:** deduplication by ingredient name is a fragile heuristic: "yellow onion" and "yellow onion, diced" and "2 cups diced yellow onion" are arguably the same ingredient but render with different strings; collapsing them into one row requires a canonicalization step (lowercase, drop the quantity prefix, drop the descriptor suffix, find the head noun). Building that canonicalizer is the same scope as the CL-70 quantity-merging work — i.e. (b) on the CL-67 escalation path. v1 doesn't ship the canonicalizer, so v1 doesn't ship dedup. **Why three rows is acceptable UX:** the recipe-title sub-label on each row gives the user the multiplication source, and the AC-39.4 within-aisle sort order (descending `addedAt`) means three onion entries added in succession naturally cluster visually — the user sees a tight stack of "diced yellow onion" rows with three different recipe titles and mentally rolls up "I need a couple of big yellow onions for the week." **What user feedback would trigger merging:** if dad's actual use surfaces the "this list has 12 'onion' rows from a single week of cooking and it's noisy" pain point, the canonicalization graduates as part of the CL-70 / CL-67 / quantity-merging cluster. The activation work is documented as the same chain. **Considered:** ship a "merge by name" toggle in Settings so power users can opt into a fuzzy-dedup behavior. Rejected for v1 — adds a Settings row for a feature we haven't built (the canonicalizer doesn't exist), and "shipping the toggle but not the behavior" creates a misleading affordance. **Considered:** ship a one-shot "Combine duplicates" button on the Shopping List view that the user can tap manually. Rejected — same problem (no canonicalizer to call), and adds a button users won't understand. **Considered:** dedupe by exact-string match only (no canonicalization — just `recipeIngredient.text == recipeIngredient.text` comparison). Rejected — exact-string match catches almost nothing in practice (different recipes use different strings for the same ingredient — the WordPress JSON-LD `recipeIngredient` arrays vary by recipe author's choice of wording even when the ingredient is identical). The dedup miss rate would be high; the user would see "duplicates collapsed" sometimes and "duplicates not collapsed" other times with no clear pattern, which is worse UX than "duplicates never collapsed." **Considered:** dedupe within the SAME recipe (a recipe that lists "1 cup diced yellow onion" twice — which happens occasionally when an author splits ingredients between marinade and pan-fry — collapses to one row). Rejected — also requires canonicalization; the same-recipe case is rare enough to not justify the parser cost, and the user can manually toggle the duplicate row as "I have this" once they grab the onion. **Spec/trace:** US-39 / AC-39.2 (per-ingredient row insertion), AC-39.4 (aisle rendering). Implementing PR: T-684 (the view-model's insert path documents the no-dedup decision; the persistence layer T-682 stores one row per call regardless).

- **CL-78 (US-39 / T-684 / T-685, 2026-05-27)** — **iPad layout: same `NavigationSplitView` pattern as the Saved tab (CC-8). Shopping list participates in the Saved tab's existing split, not as a top-level tab.** Two candidate iPad shapes: **(a) Shopping list as a content-column destination from the Saved-tab sidebar** — the Saved tab's sidebar (left column) gets a new "Shopping list" row at the top of the list, above the saved recipes. Tapping the row populates the content column (middle) with `ShoppingListView`. The detail column (right) is unused on this screen (the shopping list doesn't have a "detail" — a row tap doesn't open anything; the toggle is the only row interaction). **(b) Shopping list as a top-level iPad sidebar entry** — promotes Shopping List to a peer of Recipes & Articles / Categories / Saved / Search in the iPad sidebar; the user picks it from the same level as the four canonical tabs. **(c) Shopping list as a modal sheet from the Saved tab toolbar** — a modal sheet presentation regardless of size class; on iPad it sits as a centered overlay rather than participating in the split. **Decision: ship (a) for v1.** **Why (a):** matches the iPhone story — the Saved tab is the home base for "my saved stuff," and the shopping list is "my saved stuff in service of a shopping trip." The mental model is consistent across compact and regular size classes; on iPhone the shopping list pushes onto the Saved tab's stack, on iPad it lives in the same split as the Saved tab's content. **Why NOT (b):** the iPad sidebar's four-tab layout is a deliberate match to the iPhone tab bar (AC-16.1); adding a fifth top-level entry on iPad only would split the iPad and iPhone navigation models. The Shopping List feature is not differentiated enough from "saved stuff" to deserve a peer slot at that level. **Why NOT (c):** modal sheets are the wrong shape on iPad — they obscure the underlying surface and don't take advantage of the iPad's screen real estate. The whole point of `NavigationSplitView` on iPad per CC-8 is to keep multiple surfaces visible simultaneously; sheeting away from that pattern for one feature is inconsistent. **What the iPad sidebar entry looks like:** the Saved tab's sidebar (rendered by `SavedView` in its iPad layout) renders a new "Shopping list" `Label("Shopping list", systemImage: "cart")` at the top of the sidebar list, above the saved recipe rows. Tapping selects it and the content column shows `ShoppingListView`. The detail column shows a placeholder (probably "Pick a row to see details" or similar) — but since the shopping list doesn't have a row-level detail, the detail column can stay collapsed. **Why the sidebar entry is at the TOP of the saved-recipes list and not at the bottom or as a toolbar item:** top is the canonical "navigation entry" placement (mirrors how Apple Mail's sidebar puts "Inbox" / "Drafts" / "Sent" / "Archive" above the per-mailbox listing). Putting it inline with the saved recipes (mid-list) would imply it's just another saved item, which is wrong; putting it at the bottom buries it. Top is the affordance. **Why both iPhone toolbar AND iPad sidebar entries exist (and not just one shared pattern):** the entry surfaces follow the per-size-class navigation idiom — iPhone uses a tab bar + push navigation, so the entry is a toolbar button that pushes; iPad uses a split view + sidebar, so the entry is a sidebar row that selects. The same destination (`ShoppingListView`) is reached differently; that's correct adaptive navigation per CC-8. **Considered:** route the shopping list through the iPad sidebar's existing Saved-tab row as a long-press menu or contextual sub-entry. Rejected — sidebar long-press menus are non-standard on iPadOS and would surprise users. The "Shopping list" row above the saved recipes is the bog-standard pattern. **Considered:** show the shopping list inline at the top of the Saved tab's content column (above the saved recipes) as a collapsible section. Rejected — couples two surfaces (the saved-recipes list and the shopping list) into one content view, which is wrong: the shopping list has its own per-aisle structure that doesn't compose with a recipe list. They're different content shapes. **Considered:** introduce a new iPad-only "Cooking" tab that hosts both the shopping list and the saved recipes. Rejected — same argument as (b) above (top-level inflation); also forks the iPad and iPhone IA further. **Spec/trace:** US-39 / AC-39.9 (iPad split layout), CC-8 (the `NavigationSplitView` pattern). Implementing PR: T-684 (the iPhone shape) + T-685 (the iPad sidebar entry surface).

- **CL-80 (US-39 / T-680a, 2026-05-27)** — **Shopping List logic-core split: the pure-logic layer (aisle classification + a fraction-aware aggregator) ships ahead of the UI as a small, fully-unit-tested DODSupport sub-task (T-680a); the SwiftUI surface follows as T-680b.** The US-39 implementation plan (T-681..T-689) is a nine-PR cluster; the round-8 batching note carves the first slice (T-680a) down to two pure-value types so it lands quickly and de-risks the rest. **What T-680a ships:** (1) `IngredientAisleClassifier` (the CL-67 static keyword-map classifier) and (2) `IngredientAggregator` — both in `Packages/DODSupport`, both pure (value-in/value-out, no view, no persistence, no network), both golden-unit-tested. T-680b builds `ShoppingListView` + the `ShoppingListItem` `@Model` + the design-system primitives on top (the T-681..T-689 entries remain the full plan; T-680a is the logic core extracted forward). **Classifier: static keyword map, no ML (per CL-67).** The classifier is a `[String: Aisle]` literal (~60 common ingredient stems) plus the `default → .other` fallback, matched case-insensitively as a substring against the lowercased ingredient line. This is the deliberate good-enough-for-v1 strategy CL-67 selected over the tagged-dictionary, fuzzy-match, and WP-side alternatives; the `.other` share of `shoppingListItemAdded` events is the documented escalation trigger to a richer classifier. The keyword set co-locates in the classifier file as a static dictionary literal (NOT a JSON resource — same rationale as CL-67). **Where the `Aisle` enum lives in T-680a vs. the eventual T-681 home:** CL-67/T-681 plan `Aisle` to live in `DODDomain`; T-680a co-locates the enum *inside* `IngredientAisleClassifier.swift` in DODSupport so the logic core is a single self-contained drop with no cross-package coupling beyond DODSupport's existing surface. When T-681/T-680b land the persistence + UI, the enum can be hoisted to `DODDomain` (or kept where it is) as a mechanical move; T-680a does not pre-commit that placement. The case set matches CL-74's raw-value strings (`produce` / `dairy` / `meat` / `pantry` / `spices` / `other` for the logic-core v1; the full nine-case set with `meatSeafood` / `bakery` / `frozen` / `beverages` is the persistence/render concern T-680b reconciles against CL-74). **Aggregator: merges same-unit lines only, keeps everything else split.** `IngredientAggregator.aggregate(_:)` takes `[RecipeIngredient]` (the DODDomain raw-text type), parses each line's leading quantity + unit, and **sums quantities only when both the unit AND the remaining ingredient name match** — `2 cups flour` + `1 cup flour` → `3 cups flour`, reusing `FractionRenderer.renderQuantity(_:)` (T-440) for the cook-friendly output (`½ cup` + `¼ cup` → `¾ cup`). When units differ (`2 cups onion` + `1 lb onion`), when a line has no parseable leading quantity (`Salt and pepper to taste`), or when the names differ, the lines stay as **separate** `AggregatedItem`s — the aggregator never force-merges. Each `AggregatedItem` carries its classified `Aisle` so the result is groupable/sortable by store aisle via the classifier. **Relationship to CL-70 / CL-77 (the "no quantity merging, per-recipe rows" decisions):** CL-70 and CL-77 govern the *shipped v1 Shopping List UI* — three recipes that each call for onion render as three per-recipe rows with source-recipe sub-labels, no arithmetic. `IngredientAggregator` is a **pure-logic capability**, not a UI wiring decision: it implements the same-unit-summation math (CL-70's option (b)) as a reusable, tested utility so the capability exists and is locked by golden tests, WITHOUT changing the v1 UI contract. Whether T-680b's `ShoppingListView` calls the aggregator (a future "Combine duplicates" affordance, or an aggregated share-text variant) or keeps the CL-77 per-recipe-row rendering is a T-680b/UI decision that this clarification does not pre-empt; the v1 shipped default remains per-recipe rows per CL-77 unless a later CL flips it. Building the math now (a) de-risks the eventual CL-70 graduation by proving the fraction arithmetic is sound, and (b) gives the share-text formatter (AC-39.7) an optional rolled-up rendering path if T-680b wants one. The aggregator does **not** include the cross-unit density-aware normalization CL-70 struck as option (c) — different units stay split, exactly as CL-70 specified. **Why a separate T-680a and not fold into T-681:** the prior monolithic attempt at the whole US-39 cluster stalled; carving the two pure types into a sub-30-minute, fully-testable, push-early slice means the logic core survives even if the larger UI work needs continuation. **Spec/trace:** US-39 / AC-39.4 (aisle grouping — the classifier feeds it), AC-39.7 (share text — the aggregator offers an optional rolled-up path), AC-39.12 (no network egress — both types are pure on-device); CL-67 (classifier strategy), CL-70 + CL-77 (the per-recipe-row v1 UI contract the aggregator does not override). Implementing PR: T-680a (`feat/T-680a-shopping-logic`); UI follow-up: T-680b.

- **CL-82 (US-39 / T-680b, 2026-05-27)** — **`ShoppingListView` grouping default, ephemeral check / "already-have" state, and mock-data-for-now scope.** T-680b is the UI-only first slice of the US-39 surface, carved down (like T-680a) so the view + view-model land fast and previewable without dragging in persistence, entry surfaces, recipe-picker, or share. This CL records the decisions that scope T-680b. **(1) Grouping default — aisle-grouped sections, per-recipe rows (NOT aggregated/merged).** AC-39.4 specifies the render is sectioned by store aisle in a deterministic store-walk order, and the US-39 body + AC-39.2 + CL-70 + CL-77 all lock the v1 cross-recipe dedup rule as **per-recipe rows**: three recipes that each call for "yellow onion, diced" produce three separate rows, each labeled with its source recipe — there is no quantity summation in the shipped v1 UI. So `ShoppingListView` groups rows into aisle sections but does NOT merge duplicate ingredients; the `IngredientAggregator` (T-680a) remains an *available-but-not-default* capability per CL-80, not wired into this view. Empty aisles render no section (AC-39.4). **Aisle case set used by T-680b's first slice:** T-680b groups by the `Aisle` enum **as actually shipped by T-680a** — `IngredientAisleClassifier.Aisle` in `DODSupport`, the six-case logic-core set (`produce` / `meat` / `dairy` / `pantry` / `spices` / `other`), in that enum's `allCases` declaration order (the same store-walk order `IngredientAggregator` uses). The full nine-case render set (AC-39.4's `meatSeafood` / `bakery` / `frozen` / `beverages`) and the planned hoist of `Aisle` into `DODDomain` (T-681) are deferred to the persistence/entry-wiring slice (T-680c / T-681) as a mechanical, no-behavior-change reconciliation — T-680b consumes the type that exists today rather than blocking on the rename, exactly the latitude CL-80 reserved. The section header in this slice is rendered inline in `ShoppingListView` (aisle display name + an AC-39.4-style per-aisle SF Symbol glyph mapped for the six shipped cases); the standalone `AisleSectionHeader` DesignSystem primitive lands with T-680c when the component is shared with persistence-backed rendering. **(2) Check + "already-have" state is ephemeral in this slice.** AC-39.5 (the per-row toggle) and AC-39.8 specify the *shipped* feature persists `isChecked` via SwiftData so a checked row survives a relaunch. T-680b ships **no persistence** — the check state and the "already-have" state live in `ShoppingListViewModel` as in-memory `Set<ID>` and reset on view-model re-init. This is a deliberate scope cut, not a contradiction of AC-39.8: the persistence layer (the `ShoppingListItem` `@Model` + `@Query`) is T-680c's concern, and when it lands the view-model's ephemeral sets are swapped for the persisted `isChecked` round-trip with no change to the view's binding shape. The view exposes two distinct affordances per the T-680b brief: a leading checkbox circle (`circle` / `checkmark.circle.fill`, AC-39.5's toggle — "check off as I shop", applies the strikethrough), and a separate trailing "I already have this" swipe action that removes the row from the still-need list; both are ephemeral here. **(3) Mock data drives the view.** So the view is self-contained and previewable ahead of the entry surfaces (T-680c), `ShoppingListViewModel` is initialized in this slice from a static fixture of three recipes' worth of ingredient lines (`ShoppingListViewModel.mock`), classified through the live `IngredientAisleClassifier`. The production initializer (from `[Recipe]` or persisted `[ShoppingListItem]`) lands with T-680c's wiring. **(4) Package placement for this slice.** The full plan (CL-73 / T-684) houses `ShoppingListView` in a new `DODFeatureShoppingList` package; T-680b lands the view + view-model **inside the existing `DODFeatureSaved` package** (which already depends on `DODSupport` + `DODDesignSystem` + `DODDomain`, the only deps this slice needs) to avoid standing up a new package + Project.yml target + xcodegen wiring for a mock-data-only view. T-680c performs the package extraction when it adds persistence + the Saved-tab entry button + the recipe-picker + share. **What T-680b does NOT do (all T-680c):** no Saved-tab entry button, no `cart.badge.plus` recipe-detail action, no recipe-picker sheet, no `UIActivityViewController` share, no SwiftData persistence, no analytics events, no navigation wiring. This slice ends at "the view renders aisle-grouped mock rows correctly in light + dark, with working ephemeral check + already-have toggles." **Spec/trace:** US-39 / AC-39.1 (empty state — `EmptyState` with the `cart` glyph + AC-39.1 copy), AC-39.4 (aisle grouping + store-walk order + per-aisle glyphs), AC-39.5 (the per-row check toggle + strikethrough), AC-39.11 (VoiceOver row labels — `"<ingredient>, <aisle>, from <recipe>"`); CL-70 + CL-77 (per-recipe rows, no merge), CL-80 (the logic-core the classifier comes from + the `Aisle`-placement latitude). Implementing PR: T-680b (`feat/T-680b-shopping-list-view`); persistence + entry + share follow-up: T-680c.

---

**Phase 12 summary.** Twelve CLs (CL-67 through CL-78) resolve the design questions raised by the round-3 Shopping List backlog entry; a later thirteenth (CL-80, appended above) carves the logic-core slice T-680a forward. Three explicit deferrals (CL-68 location-based notifications, CL-71 pantry inventory, CL-72... actually CL-69 multi-list — adjusted; CL-72 share format is shipped) are out of v1 scope with documented activation triggers. The remaining nine ship v1 paths. The implementation plan is T-680 (this spec PR) + T-681..T-689 (the 9 implementation PRs); coordination notes on each CL above call out parallel files where relevant. **Coordination across round-8 parallels:** T-660 (tab-bar truncation — already merged), T-670 (phantom searches — Spencer, in-flight), T-690 (Voice Mode — round-3 dad, still pending graduation). T-680's spec edits touch `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` / `backlog.md` (this PR); T-681..T-689 each touch their own implementation file family per the task entries. No source-side collisions with T-670 (which owns `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/**`) or T-690 (which will own `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/**` Cook Mode surface when it graduates). Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` / `backlog.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL/US/T slot — T-680 reserves CL-67..CL-78, US-39, T-680..T-689; T-690 (when it graduates) picks the next free slots after that.

---

## Phase 13 amendments (Voice Mode, 2026-05-27)

- **CL-79 (US-40 / T-690a / T-690b, 2026-05-27)** — **Cook Mode Voice Mode synthesizer, voice/locale, and audio-session decisions.** The round-3 dad "Cooking Voice Mode" backlog entry graduates as US-40, split into T-690a (the standalone `VoiceReader` core) and T-690b (the Cook Mode wiring + four App Intents + analytics). This CL resolves the four design questions the reader core raises.

  **(1) Speech engine: on-device `AVSpeechSynthesizer`, not a cloud TTS service.** **Decision: `AVSpeechSynthesizer` (AVFoundation).** The candidate alternatives were **(a) `AVSpeechSynthesizer`** — Apple's on-device text-to-speech, free, no network, no entitlement, available since iOS 7 and on both iOS and macOS; **(b) a cloud TTS API** (e.g. a server-side neural-voice service) — higher voice quality but adds a network round-trip per step, a per-character cost, a new privacy surface (step text leaves the device), and a hard dependency on connectivity *while the user is mid-recipe in a kitchen*; **(c) pre-rendered audio shipped with each recipe** — impossible, the recipe corpus is the live WordPress blog, not a fixed bundle. **Why (a):** it is the only option consistent with the backlog framing ("Uses on-device `AVSpeechSynthesizer` so no network round-trip, no subscription cost, no privacy surface") and constitution §9's "no raw user input strings to the network" posture — the step text never leaves the device. Cook Mode is used in a kitchen where connectivity is unreliable; an on-device engine keeps Voice Mode working offline (parallel to US-35's download-for-offline story). Voice quality is "good enough" — the personal-voice / enhanced-voice download is a system affordance the user controls in iOS Settings, and `AVSpeechSynthesizer` automatically picks up any enhanced voice the user has installed. **Why NOT (b):** the cost, the latency mid-step, and especially the privacy regression (sending recipe step text to a third-party TTS server) are all disqualifying for a feature whose entire selling point is "on-device, no subscription, no privacy surface." **Why NOT (c):** the corpus isn't bundled.

  **(2) Voice + locale: the system-default voice for `Locale.current`, no in-app voice picker in v1.** **Decision: resolve the voice via `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice(language:)` from the device's `Locale.current` language code (e.g. `"en-US"`), and fall back to the platform default voice when no localized voice is available.** Each utterance is an `AVSpeechUtterance` whose `.voice` is set to that resolved voice (leaving it `nil` is also acceptable — the synthesizer then uses the system default, which is the same outcome for the common case). The candidates were **(a) system-default voice for the current locale** — zero configuration, respects whatever voice + language the user has set system-wide, picks up enhanced/personal voices automatically; **(b) an in-app voice picker** — let the user choose voice + rate + pitch in a Voice Mode settings panel; **(c) a hardcoded en-US voice** — simplest but wrong for non-US-English users. **Decision: (a) for v1.** **Why (a):** it is the least-surprise default — the user's system voice setting (Settings → Accessibility → Spoken Content → Voices) is the single source of truth, so a user who has already chosen a preferred voice + speaking rate for VoiceOver / Spoken Content gets that same voice in Cook Mode for free. It also keeps the T-690a reader core free of any persisted preference. **Why NOT (b):** a per-feature voice picker duplicates an OS-level affordance, adds a settings surface + persistence + a snapshot-tested UI, and is not justified for v1 — it can graduate later if user feedback asks for an in-app rate control. **Why NOT (c):** hardcoding en-US breaks for any non-US-English device and contradicts the "respect the system" posture. **Speaking rate:** v1 uses `AVSpeechUtterance`'s default rate (`AVSpeechUtteranceDefaultSpeechRate`); no custom rate is set, so the user's system Spoken-Content rate (where it applies) and Apple's default govern pacing. A custom in-app rate slider is the natural extension if (b) is ever revisited.

  **(3) Audio session: `.playback` category + `.duckOthers` option, deactivated with `.notifyOthersOnDeactivation`.** **Decision: activate `AVAudioSession.sharedInstance()` with `category: .playback`, `mode: .default`, `options: [.duckOthers]` before the first utterance; deactivate with `.notifyOthersOnDeactivation` on `stop()`.** The candidates were **(a) `.playback` + `.duckOthers`** — the app's speech plays even when the ringer/silent switch is set to silent (correct: the user explicitly turned Voice Mode on, they want to hear it), and other audio (music, podcasts) **ducks** (drops to a low volume) for the duration of the utterance then returns to full; **(b) `.ambient`** — respects the silent switch and mixes silently with other audio, but `.ambient` would be muted by the hardware silent switch, defeating the feature when the user has their phone on silent (common in a kitchen); **(c) `.soloAmbient`** — the default category, *stops* other audio rather than ducking it — too aggressive, the user listening to a podcast while cooking would have it killed every time a step is read; **(d) `.playback` + `.mixWithOthers`** — plays over other audio at full volume without ducking, which makes the step hard to hear over loud music. **Decision: (a).** **Why `.playback`:** the user opted into Voice Mode deliberately; the step reading should be audible regardless of the silent switch, exactly as a podcast or navigation prompt is. **Why `.duckOthers` (not stop, not mix):** ducking is the polite, expected behavior for a transient spoken prompt (it mirrors turn-by-turn navigation and Siri) — the user's background music dips while the step is read, then comes back. Stopping (`.soloAmbient`) is hostile to the cook listening to music; mixing without ducking (`.mixWithOthers`) makes the step inaudible. **Why `.notifyOthersOnDeactivation` on teardown:** when Voice Mode is turned off (or the reader stops), deactivating with this option tells other apps their audio can return to full volume immediately, so the ducked podcast doesn't stay quiet. **Lifecycle:** the session is activated lazily on the *first* `speak(_:)` (not eagerly when the reader is constructed) so merely having a `VoiceReader` instance never disturbs other audio; it is deactivated on `stop()`. **Platform guard:** `AVAudioSession` is iOS-only — the activation/deactivation is wrapped in `#if os(iOS)` (mirroring the ActivityKit guard in `SystemCookLiveActivityController` per constitution §2) so the package compiles + tests on the macOS `swift test` slice. The reader's speak/stop/pause/resume state machine is exercised on both platforms via the injected mock synthesizer; only the real audio-session side-effect is compiled out on macOS.

  **(4) Testability seam + Voice-Mode-off-by-default.** **Decision: `VoiceReader` depends on a `SpeechSynthesizing` protocol (methods `speak(_:) / stop() / pause() / continueSpeaking()`, plus `isSpeaking` / `isPaused` state), with a thin adapter conforming `AVSpeechSynthesizer` to it; L1 tests inject a mock.** This mirrors the `CookLiveActivityController` protocol seam (US-11 / CL — the `SystemCookLiveActivityController` production conformance vs. the test stub) that lets `CookModeViewModel` build + test on macOS without ActivityKit. The mock records calls + flips its own `isSpeaking` / `isPaused` flags so the reader's contract — speak → speaking, stop → stopped, pause → paused, resume → speaking, and **speak-while-speaking stops the prior utterance first** (AC-40.7) — is asserted with zero real-audio dependency. **Voice-Mode-off-by-default + not-persisted (T-690b):** Voice Mode is **off** every time Cook Mode is entered and is **not** persisted across cook sessions. **Why:** an app that unexpectedly starts talking when the user opens a recipe (because they left Voice Mode on three sessions ago) is startling and potentially embarrassing (in a quiet room, on a speaker). Requiring a deliberate per-session toggle-on is the safe default; the cost is one extra tap per cook session, which is acceptable for a hands-free feature the user is consciously choosing to use. A "remember Voice Mode" preference can graduate later behind a Settings toggle if users ask. **Trace:** US-40 / AC-40.1..AC-40.8; the reader core (AC-40.2 partial, AC-40.4, AC-40.6, AC-40.7) lands in T-690a, the toggle + re-read driver + four intents + `voiceModeToggled` analytics (AC-40.1, AC-40.3, AC-40.5, AC-40.8) land in T-690b. Implementing PRs: T-690a (this CL's reader-core decisions) + T-690b (wiring + intents + analytics).

- **CL-81 (US-40 / T-690b, 2026-05-27)** — **In-app Voice Mode wiring scope: T-690b's first slice is the Cook Mode toggle + read-aloud driver + the four control methods on `CookModeViewModel`, exercised in-app and by L1 tests; the Siri App Intent exposure and the `voiceModeToggled` analytics event are deferred to a follow-up.** CL-79 split US-40 into T-690a (the standalone `VoiceReader` core) and T-690b (the full Cook Mode wiring + four `RecipeAppIntents` + `voiceModeToggled` analytics + §9 amendment). The round-8 batching note carves T-690b down to a small, completable in-app slice so the user-visible Voice Mode lands and de-risks the Siri layer: this PR ships (1) the Voice Mode toggle on `CookModeView`'s top bar per AC-40.1 (`speaker.wave.2` / `speaker.wave.2.fill`, accessibility identifier `cook-mode-voice-toggle`, label "Voice Mode", off-by-default + not-persisted per CL-79); (2) the re-read-on-step-change driver in `CookModeViewModel` per AC-40.3 — `goNext()` / `goBack()` (which the on-screen Next/Previous controls and swipe gestures already call) re-`speak(_:)` the new step whenever Voice Mode is on, including the "All done — enjoy your meal" completion line on `isFinished`; and (3) the four hands-free **control methods on the view model — `advanceStep()`, `previousStep()`, `repeatCurrentStep()`, `pauseVoice()`** — wired to `VoiceReader` and exercised in-app + by L1 tests (mock synthesizer asserts: toggle on → speaks current step, step change → re-speaks the new step, repeat → re-speaks the same step, pause → pauses, toggle off / Cook Mode exit → stops). **Why the four methods exist now but Siri is deferred:** the App Intent layer (AC-40.5) is the larger, riskier surface (App Shortcuts registration, phrase donation, targeting the active Cook Mode session through the SiriKit lifecycle). Building the view-model command surface first means the intents become a thin adapter that calls already-tested methods — the intents add SiriKit plumbing in the follow-up (**T-690c**) without re-litigating the navigation/read-aloud behavior. The methods route through the same `goNext()` / `goBack()` the on-screen controls use (AC-7.4), so a future Siri command and an on-screen tap are guaranteed identical. **What this slice does NOT include (deferred to T-690c):** the four `RecipeAppIntents` + App Shortcuts donation (AC-40.5) and the `voiceModeToggled(recipeID:enabled:)` analytics event + constitution §9 allowlist amendment (AC-40.8). The AC-40.8 §9 amendment lands paired with the event in T-690c, keeping this PR's egress surface unchanged (Voice Mode is fully on-device — no network at all) and its constitution diff empty. **AC-7.6 coverage:** `endCookMode()` calls `VoiceReader.stop()` (releasing the ducked audio session) and clears `isVoiceModeEnabled`, so exiting Cook Mode never leaves a voice reading into a closed surface — symmetric to the idle-timer + Live Activity teardown already in `endCookMode()`. **Spec/trace:** US-40 / AC-40.1 (toggle), AC-40.3 (re-read on step change), AC-40.5 (the control methods the intents will call — the in-app half only; Siri exposure is T-690c), consuming the T-690a `VoiceReader` core (AC-40.2 / AC-40.4 / AC-40.6 / AC-40.7). CL-80 was taken by T-680a's Shopping-List-logic-core split, so this Voice-Mode-wiring CL takes CL-81. Implementing PR: T-690b (`feat/T-690b-voice-wiring`); Siri intents + analytics follow-up: T-690c.

- **CL-83 (US-40 / T-690c, 2026-05-27)** — **Voice Mode Siri intents reach the live Cook Mode session through a `VoiceCommandBus` singleton (mirroring the US-10 `DeepLinkDispatcher`); the `voiceModeToggled` + `voiceCommandFired` analytics events carry only closed-set values, no free text; constitution §9 amended.** This is the Siri/analytics follow-up CL-81 deferred from T-690b. The four `CookModeViewModel` control methods (`advanceStep()` / `previousStep()` / `repeatCurrentStep()` / `pauseVoice()`) already exist and are L1-tested (T-690b); T-690c exposes them to SiriKit and adds the voice telemetry.
  **(1) How the intents reach the *live* Cook Mode view model.** The problem: an `AppIntent.perform()` runs **outside** any SwiftUI view tree and has no handle to the `@State`-owned `CookModeViewModel` that `CookModeView` constructs. US-10's `OpenRecipeIntent` solved the analogous problem for *navigation* by posting a `DeepLinkIntent` onto the `@Observable` `DeepLinkDispatcher.shared` bus that `RootView` observes; but Voice Mode commands don't navigate — they must drive the **already-presented** session's step state in place. **Decision: a new `VoiceCommandBus` singleton in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail`, mirroring the `DeepLinkDispatcher` pattern but holding a `weak var handler: (any VoiceCommandHandler)?` instead of a pending-value the root view drains.** `CookModeViewModel` conforms to `VoiceCommandHandler` (the four methods *are* the protocol); `beginCookMode()` sets `VoiceCommandBus.shared.handler = self` and `endCookMode()` clears it (`handler = nil`). Each intent's `perform()` calls `VoiceCommandBus.shared.dispatch(.next/.previous/.repeat/.pause)`; the bus's `nonisolated dispatch(_:)` hops to `@MainActor` (exactly like `DeepLinkDispatcher.dispatch`) and forwards to `handler?.advanceStep()` etc. **Why a weak handler, not a pending-command queue:** voice commands are imperative and only meaningful while Cook Mode is foreground — there is no "consume later" semantics the way a deep link is consumed once on the next render. A `weak` reference means a torn-down Cook Mode session is automatically a no-op (the handler is `nil`), and Siri firing "next step" from the lock screen with no active session does nothing rather than crashing or replaying into a stale VM. **Why the bus lives in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail`, not the App target:** the VM (which must register) lives there, and the package already depends on `DODAnalytics`; the App-target intents already `import DODFeatureRecipeDetail`, so the dependency direction is satisfied and the bus + the register/unregister wiring are L1-testable inside the package with a fake handler. **Why the intents take no `recipe` parameter** (unlike `OpenRecipeIntent`): a voice command acts on whatever step the live session is on — there is nothing to disambiguate, so the intents are parameter-less and `openAppWhenRun` is left at its default (they don't need to launch the app — if Cook Mode isn't up there's no session to drive). **(2) Phrase registration.** The four intents register on the **existing** `DODShortcuts` `AppShortcutsProvider` (US-10 / REG-10) alongside the three recipe intents — `AppShortcutsProvider` allows up to the system phrase budget and one provider per app, so we extend the existing one rather than adding a second. Phrases (each suffixed `in \(.applicationName)`): `NextStepIntent` — "next step" / "next" / "go forward"; `PreviousStepIntent` — "previous step" / "go back" / "back"; `RepeatStepIntent` — "repeat step" / "repeat that" / "say that again"; `PauseVoiceIntent` — "pause reading" / "pause". **Every utterance must contain `\(.applicationName)`** — the `appintentsmetadataprocessor` build step fails the export with "Invalid Utterance. Every App Shortcut utterance should have one '${applicationName}' in it." otherwise. This requirement applies to **all** phrases, not only those that interpolate a `@Parameter` (an earlier reading of the US-10 note had it backwards — the build error caught it); the `\(.applicationName)` token scopes the phrase to this app and Siri still matches the bare command verb once the shortcut is donated. **(3) Telemetry — two events, both closed-set, no free text (AC-40.8).** `voiceCommandFired(command:)` fires from each intent's `perform()`; `command` is a `VoiceCommandName` — a closed `String`-raw enum (`next` / `previous` / `repeat` / `pause`) — so the wire payload can only ever be one of four fixed strings, **never** the user's raw spoken phrase (which the intent layer never even sees — SiriKit matches the phrase and hands us a typed intent). `voiceModeToggled(on:)` fires from `CookModeViewModel.setVoiceMode(_:)` with a single boolean (on/off). Neither event carries a recipe id (the prior `voiceModeToggled(recipeID:enabled:)` shape sketched in CL-81 / the T-690b task entry is simplified to `voiceModeToggled(on:)` here — the recipe id adds nothing the `cookModeStarted(recipeID:)` event doesn't already capture, and dropping it keeps both Voice events to a single primitive value). **Constitution §9 allowlist amended** (US-40 authorized the Voice Mode events; this CL lands the amendment paired with the events per the "amendment lands with the event" convention): both `voiceModeToggled(on:)` and `voiceCommandFired(command:)` added as "Usage Data — Product Interaction" device-state/usage events with no free text and no PII — the App Privacy questionnaire mapping table is unchanged. **Why the command name is allowlist-safe despite being a string:** it is a fixed enum drawn from a closed set the app defines, not user input — the same posture as `widgetOpened`'s `kind` ("featured"/"saved") and `screenView`'s `name` ("feed"/"recipe_detail"), both already on the allowlist. **(4) Verification.** Siri intents can't be exercised in the sim without a device, so the verification is the per-intent L1 tests (a fake `VoiceCommandHandler` registered on the bus asserts each intent dispatches to the right method) + the analytics payload-shape tests (assert the closed enum-string set, no free text) + a clean `xcodebuild`. **PR carries the `e2e` label** per CONTRIBUTING.md (it touches App Intents). **Spec/trace:** US-40 / AC-40.5 (the four Siri intents — the in-app control surface they call landed in T-690b), AC-40.8 (`voiceModeToggled` + `voiceCommandFired` analytics + §9 amendment). CL-81 took the prior slot and T-680b is running in parallel; this CL takes the next free slot (CL-83, renumbered from CL-82 on merge — CL-82 was taken by T-680b's ShoppingListView clarification). Implementing PR: T-690c (`feat/T-690c-voice-intents`).

---

**Phase 13 summary.** One CL (CL-79) resolves the four design questions the round-3 dad "Cooking Voice Mode" backlog entry raises: the on-device `AVSpeechSynthesizer` engine choice, the system-default voice + `Locale.current` (no in-app picker in v1), the `.playback` + `.duckOthers` audio-session category/option, and the testability seam + Voice-Mode-off-by-default. US-40 is split into **T-690a** (the standalone `VoiceReader` core — `AVSpeechSynthesizer` wrapper + audio-session management + `SpeechSynthesizing` protocol seam + L1 mock-based tests; touches no Cook Mode UI, registers no intents, adds no analytics) and **T-690b** (the Cook Mode toggle + re-read-on-step-change driver + four `RecipeAppIntents` + the `voiceModeToggled` analytics event + the constitution §9 allowlist amendment). **Coordination across round-8 parallels:** T-680 (Shopping List — dad, in-flight on a new `DODFeatureShoppingList` package) runs in parallel; T-690a owns only `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/Sources/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/VoiceReader.swift` + its test file, so there are no source-side collisions with T-680 (different package) or with T-685's later RecipeDetail toolbar extension (a different region of the same package — `RecipeDetailView.swift`, not the new `VoiceReader.swift`). Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `spec.md` / `tasks.md` / `backlog.md` are expected at merge time and the rebaser picks the next free CL/US/T slot — T-690a reserves CL-79, US-40, T-690a/T-690b.

## Phase 14 amendments (Shopping List entry + picker + share, 2026-05-27)

- **CL-85 (US-39 / T-680c, 2026-05-27)** — **Shopping List entry-point placement, recipe-picker behavior, share mechanism, and how real saved recipes flow into the T-680b view.** T-680b (CL-82) shipped `ShoppingListView` + `ShoppingListViewModel` driven by mock data, with the Saved-tab entry button, the recipe-picker, and the share surface all explicitly deferred to T-680c. This CL records the four decisions that make US-39 reachable end-to-end and complete the story. **Pre-assigned CL number CL-85** (not next-free) to avoid collision with the parallel T-690c Voice-Mode-Siri work, which reserves its own slot.

  **(1) Entry-point placement — a Saved-tab toolbar button, "Make Shopping List".** **Decision: a single trailing-edge toolbar button on `SavedView`'s nav bar (SF Symbol `cart`, label "Make Shopping List", accessibility identifier `saved-make-shopping-list`) that presents the recipe-picker sheet (decision 2).** AC-39.3's full design describes a selection-*mode* on the Saved grid (per-card leading checkbox circles + a "Build shopping list" toolbar toggle that turns the grid itself into a multi-select surface, plus a long-press-menu secondary entry per AC-34.1) and AC-39.9 adds an iPad sidebar "Shopping list" row. The candidates for this completion slice were **(a) a toolbar button that opens a dedicated picker *sheet*** (the saved recipes rendered as a multi-select list inside a modal), **(b) the full AC-39.3 in-grid selection mode** (mutate the existing `RecipeCard` grid into checkbox-select), and **(c) a per-recipe-detail `cart.badge.plus` action only** (AC-39.2, no bulk path). **Decision: (a) for this slice.** **Why (a):** it lands the bulk multi-recipe path (the higher-value flow — "combine several recipes into one list") as a self-contained additive change to `SavedView` (one toolbar button + one `.sheet`), without re-plumbing the `RecipeCard` grid's tap/context-menu gestures (which already carry DOD-NAV-1 + DOD-LIST-SCROLL regression locks — adding a selection-mode overlay risks both). A dedicated picker sheet is also clearer for the user than an in-place mode toggle with no obvious affordance, and it keeps the entry button's behavior identical on iPhone + iPad (the sheet adapts via the system form-sheet presentation) so AC-39.9's split-view sidebar row can layer on later without reworking this entry. **Why NOT (b) now:** the in-grid selection mode is a larger surface (per-card checkbox state, a mode toggle, the gesture-coexistence work against the two regression locks) and is deferred as a UX refinement — the picker sheet satisfies "add from multiple saved recipes" (AC-39.3's intent) without it. **Why NOT (c) only:** the per-detail single-recipe action is a complementary entry (a natural T-685 follow-up on `RecipeDetailView`), but on its own it omits the multi-recipe combine that is US-39's headline ("combine the ingredients from one or more saved recipes into a single grouped shopping list"). The button is rendered whenever the Saved list is in its `.loaded` state (hidden in idle/loading/empty/error — there are no saved recipes to pick from in those states, mirroring the AC-39.1 hide-when-empty posture for the shopping-list-side toolbar buttons).

  **(2) Recipe-picker behavior — a modal multi-select sheet, "Build List" enabled at ≥1 selection.** **Decision: `ShoppingListBuilderSheet` renders the Saved view-model's already-loaded `recipes` as a `List` of toggle rows (tap anywhere on a row flips its selection; a leading `circle` / `checkmark.circle.fill` mirrors the AC-39.5 row-toggle idiom for visual consistency), with a "Build List" confirmation button in the sheet's toolbar that is disabled until at least one recipe is selected.** Selection state is local `@State` (`Set<Recipe.ID>`) in the sheet — ephemeral, discarded on cancel. On "Build List" the sheet hands the selected `[Recipe]` back to `SavedView` via a closure; `SavedView` pushes `ShoppingListView` onto its existing `NavigationStack` (decision 4). The sheet reuses the Saved view-model's in-memory `recipes` array (no second fetch — the Saved tab has already loaded them via `SavedViewModel.refresh()`), so the picker is instant and works offline against the pinned saved-recipe cache (consistent with REG-23 / AC-39.12 — no network egress on the shopping-list path). A "Cancel" button dismisses without building. Recipes with **zero ingredients** (article-kind posts per US-37, which always have empty `ingredients`) are still selectable but contribute no rows — they are not filtered out of the picker (keeping the picker list identical to the Saved grid the user already knows), they simply add nothing to the resulting list; the headline-count messaging is out of scope for this slice (the snackbar of AC-39.2/AC-39.3 is a T-685/T-687 concern).

  **(3) Share mechanism — SwiftUI `ShareLink` wrapping generated Markdown text, NOT `MFMessageComposeViewController`.** **Decision: a "Share via iMessage" toolbar button on `ShoppingListView`'s nav bar (SF Symbol `square.and.arrow.up`, accessibility identifier `shopping-list-share`) implemented as a SwiftUI `ShareLink(item:)` whose item is the `String` built by `ShoppingListFormatter.shareText(_:)`.** AC-39.7 specifies the share is plain text through the system share sheet (`UIActivityViewController`) and CL-72 explicitly rejects a `MessageUI` framework dependency. The candidates were **(a) SwiftUI `ShareLink`** — the declarative system-share affordance that wraps `UIActivityViewController` for free, routes to iMessage / AirDrop / Mail / Notes / Copy / any installed share target, requires no framework import + no `UIViewControllerRepresentable` bridge, and matches the existing `ShareLink(item: viewModel.canonicalURL)` already used on `RecipeDetailView`; **(b) `MFMessageComposeViewController`** via a `UIViewControllerRepresentable` bridge — opens the *Messages compose sheet specifically*, but pulls in the `MessageUI` framework, needs a delegate + a bridge type + a can-send-text guard, and locks the share to Messages only (no AirDrop / Mail / Copy fallback). **Decision: (a) `ShareLink`.** **Why (a):** it is exactly the mechanism AC-39.7 + CL-72 prescribe (plain-text item, system share sheet, no `MessageUI` dependency), it is the least code (one `ShareLink` view, no bridge), and the system share sheet still surfaces iMessage as the first/most-prominent target — so "share via iMessage" is one tap from the sheet while AirDrop/Mail/Copy stay available. The task brief's "Share via iMessage" label is satisfied by the system sheet routing to Messages; the button label is kept user-facing-friendly ("Share via iMessage") while the underlying affordance is the general share sheet. **Why NOT (b):** `MFMessageComposeViewController` would *contradict* CL-72's no-`MessageUI`-dependency decision, add a bridge + delegate surface, and remove the non-Messages share targets AC-39.7 explicitly wants — the spec demands the general share sheet, not a Messages-only compose. The `ShareLink` is rendered only when the list is non-empty (mirroring AC-39.1's hide-share-in-empty-state rule).

  **(4) How real recipes flow into the T-680b view + the share-text contents.** **Decision: `ShoppingListView` is constructed from the picker's selected `[Recipe]` via the *already-existing* `ShoppingListViewModel(recipes:)` convenience initializer that T-680b shipped** — it flat-maps each recipe's `ingredients` into per-recipe `Item` rows (CL-77 — no cross-recipe merge), classifying each line through the live `IngredientAisleClassifier` (CL-67 / CL-80, the six-case logic-core `Aisle`). No new construction path is added; the entry wiring simply feeds real selected recipes where the mock fixture fed three canned ones, exactly the seam CL-82 reserved ("the production initializer from `[Recipe]` lands with T-680c's wiring"). **Share-text format + the checked/already-have exclusion (a deliberate, recorded deviation from CL-72's "always the full list").** `ShoppingListFormatter.shareText(_:)` builds Markdown: a header line `"Shopping List"`, a blank line, then per-aisle blocks in the AC-39.4 store-walk order (`Aisle.allCases` declaration order, only non-empty aisles) — each block is the aisle display name (e.g. "Produce", "Meat & Seafood") followed by `- <ingredient text> (<recipe title>)` lines, one per row. **The shared text omits two row classes: (i) rows the user marked "I already have this" (`alreadyHaveIDs` — already excluded from the view-model's `visibleItems`, so they never reach the formatter) and (ii) rows the user has checked off as acquired while shopping (`checkedIDs`).** This is an *intentional divergence* from CL-72, which specified the share copy is "always the full list, not just unchecked items." **Why diverge:** the entry/share completion brief (round-8) re-scoped the share to "the still-needed list" — the use-case is handing someone a list of what to *buy*, so items the cook already has (already-have) or has already grabbed (checked) are noise on the shared list. CL-72's "full list" rationale was that the share is a snapshot/record; the round-8 re-scope prioritizes the actionable shopping subset instead. The two telemetry events (AC-39.10) and the no-network contract (AC-39.12 / REG-23) are unaffected — the formatter is a pure `String`-building function that touches no `URLSession`. **If a future pass wants CL-72's full-list snapshot back, the formatter takes a single `includeChecked` flag** (the exclusion lives in one `filter` call). **Spec/trace:** US-39 / AC-39.2 + AC-39.3 (entry → build-list path — the picker-sheet realization of the bulk add), AC-39.7 (share via the system share sheet, plain text, no `MessageUI`), consuming the T-680b `ShoppingListView` + `ShoppingListViewModel(recipes:)` (CL-82) + the T-680a `IngredientAisleClassifier` (CL-80). Completes US-39 end-to-end (Saved tab → picker → aisle-grouped list → share). Implementing PR: T-680c (`feat/T-680c-shopping-entry`).

---

**Phase 14 summary.** One CL (CL-85, pre-assigned) closes out US-39 by wiring the three surfaces T-680b deferred: a Saved-tab "Make Shopping List" toolbar button that presents a multi-select `ShoppingListBuilderSheet`, the picker's "Build List" path constructing a real `ShoppingListViewModel(recipes:)` from the selected saved recipes (reusing the T-680b convenience initializer + the T-680a classifier — no merge, per-recipe rows), and a `ShareLink`-based "Share via iMessage" button on `ShoppingListView` that shares Markdown built by `ShoppingListFormatter.shareText(_:)`. The one recorded deviation from prior CLs is the share-text **exclusion of checked + already-have rows** (CL-72 had said "always the full list") — a round-8 re-scope toward the actionable shopping subset, reversible via a single formatter flag. **Coordination:** T-690c (Voice Mode Siri intents — `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/**`) runs in parallel; this CL is pre-assigned CL-85 to avoid a CL collision. Spec-file collisions on `clarifications.md` / `tasks.md` are expected at merge time. T-680c reserves CL-85, T-680c — no new US (US-39 already covers it; this completes it).
