On top of that, iOS 26's SDK shifted dark-mode anti-aliasing / material rendering, adding sub-pixel drift between the local record SDK (Xcode 26.5) and CI (26.3). **(3) Non-deterministic `TabView` material.** Even after re-record, the eight dark `TabBarSnapshotTests` variants still failed at **~0.2% pixel match between two renders minutes apart on the same machine** — iOS 26's translucent tab-bar material composites non-deterministically when a `TabView` is rendered off-screen for snapshotting. A fourth, latent bug surfaced here: `TabBarSnapshotTests`'s helper defaulted `file: StaticString = #file` (the *concise* `Module/File.swift` form) where every other suite uses the library default `#filePath` (absolute); under `xcodebuild` the concise path made `__Snapshots__` resolve to the ephemeral simulator data dir, so the committed tab-bar baselines were never actually consulted on CI. **Decisions.** **(a) Tolerance everywhere.** `precision: 0.98, perceptualPrecision: 0.97` is applied to every `.image(...)` site including the `darkImage` helper — absorbs the legitimate cross-version sub-pixel drift without being so loose it misses a real chrome change (the 0.97 perceptual figure rejects the `#553724` color change at 0.85, so it still catches color regressions). **(b) Re-record the dark baselines under iOS 26.** The current dark renders were visually confirmed correct (warm `#553724` recipe-card surface, proper light-on-dark contrast) before blessing them — this is a stale-baseline fix, not a regression bless. **(c) Remove the dark tab-bar snapshots; keep light.** Per the decision matrix below, the dark `TabView` material is unsnapshottable under iOS 26; AC-16.5 is amended to light-only and the structural contract (order / icons / labels) stays pinned by `DODAppUnitTests/AppTabTests` + `SmokeTests.test_tabBarOrderMatchesSpec`. The helper's `#file` is changed to `#filePath` so the eight light baselines record to and compare against the source tree. **Decision matrix for the dark tab bar — considered and rejected.** *Raise tolerance further:* rejected — 0.2% pixel match would need precision ≈ 0.0, which disables the test (it would pass against literally any image). *Make the fixture deterministic with an opaque tab-bar appearance (`UITabBarAppearance.configureWithOpaqueBackground()`):* rejected for v1 — it changes what's under test (opaque ≠ the shipping translucent bar), adds fixture complexity, and the visual value is low because the tab bar is **system-rendered chrome**, not DOD-designed pixels; a future task can revisit if dark tab-bar visual cover is wanted. *Quarantine / `XCTSkip` the eight:* rejected — a skipped test reads as covered but isn't; removal with the contract pinned elsewhere is more honest. **What CL-115 does NOT touch:** any source/product code (this is a test-infra + baseline-PNG change only — no `DODColor`, no `RecipeCard`, no `AppTab`, no composition root), the light snapshot baselines for any suite (unchanged — all `_light` tests passed throughout), the SwiftData schema, the CI workflow YAML (#91 already moved it to Xcode 26), the L1/L2/L3/L5 layers. **Considered: delete the whole `TabBarSnapshotTests` suite.** Rejected — the light variants are deterministic and cheap and still catch a font / icon-asset / ordering chrome change in the primary appearance; only the dark variants are unsnapshottable. **Considered: leave the dark baselines stale and just widen tolerance to ~0.5 to "make them pass".** Rejected — that blesses the #42-era colors as truth and would mask the real `#553724` change plus any future dark regression; re-record is the correct way to reflect an intentional color change. **Spec/trace:** AC-16.5 amended in `spec.md` (light-only tab-bar baselines; dark dropped with the non-determinism rationale + lineage marker); REG-28 added to the AC-T4 block (locks the deterministic + cross-version-stable snapshot contract); US-18 / AC-18.1 / AC-18.2 preserved unchanged (dark-mode WCAG-AA coverage for the *components* — EmptyState, RecipeCard, Snackbar, widgets, comments — is retained and now actually green; only the `TabView` chrome lost its dark snapshot); constitution §6 L4 preserved (the visual-regression layer stays, just made reliable). Implementing PR: T-730 (`chore/snapshot-precision-tolerance`). Tolerance injection across seven `*SnapshotTests.swift` files + 31 re-recorded dark PNGs + 8 re-recorded light tab-bar PNGs + 8 removed dark tab-bar PNGs + the `TabBarSnapshotTests` dark-method removal and `#file`→`#filePath` fix + spec amendments; no source-code edit, no schema change, no `e2e` label.

## Phase 17 amendments (Voice Mode quality + voice selection, 2026-05-29)

- **CL-109 (US-40 / T-720, 2026-05-29)** — **Cook Mode Voice Mode quality: the resolver picks the best installed quality tier (premium > enhanced > default) honoring a gender preference, gender-primary then quality-secondary; the Settings picker is deferred to Phase b.** Round-9 backlog captured @adamsned's TestFlight 1.0 (2) complaint that the Cook Mode voice "sounds like a robot" plus a request for a male/female toggle in Settings. **The confirmed root cause.** `SystemSpeechSynthesizer.speak(localizedText:languageCode:)` resolved the voice via `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice(language: languageCode)` per the original AC-40.2 / CL-79 decision. That initializer returns the **`default`-quality (compact, concatenative)** voice for a language — the robotic tier that has shipped since iOS 7 — **even when an `enhanced` Siri voice is already installed on the device**. The `(language:)` initializer picks the language's *default* voice, which is the compact one; the enhanced/premium tiers are only reachable by enumerating `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice.speechVoices()` and selecting on `.quality`. So the fix is not "download better voices" (though Phase b adds that deep-link) — it is "stop asking for the compact voice when a better one is already present." **(1) The selection algorithm — gender-primary, quality-secondary.** `VoiceSelector.bestVoiceIdentifier(from:languageCode:preference:)` filters the catalog to language-family matches, then picks the minimum of a 4-tuple sort key: `(genderRank, qualityRank, exactLocaleRank, identifier)`. `genderRank` is 0 for the requested gender, 1 for `unspecified`, 2 for the opposite gender. `qualityRank` is 0 for premium, 1 for enhanced, 2 for default. `exactLocaleRank` prefers an exact `en-US` match over a prefix `en` match. The `identifier` is the final stable tie-break so selection is deterministic regardless of catalog order. **Why gender-primary, not quality-primary.** The user asked for an explicit male/female choice; honoring it is the point of the feature. A premium voice of the *wrong* gender would violate the explicit preference. The trade-off: when the requested gender has only a compact voice installed (and the other gender has premium), the user gets the compact voice of their gender rather than the premium of the wrong gender. In practice this does not bite the default experience — the default preference is `female` and en-US ships enhanced female voices (Samantha enhanced + the Siri female voice), so the default Cook Mode voice upgrades from robotic to natural immediately with no download and no UI. Phase b's explicit-voice picker + "Download more voices" deep-link is the escape hatch for the minority locale/gender combinations where only compact voices are installed. **Considered: quality-primary then gender.** Rejected — it would give the user the best-sounding voice but could ignore their explicit gender choice, which is the headline ask. **Considered: a manual gender mapping table** (the backlog open question worried Apple's voices "aren't tagged with gender"). Rejected as unnecessary — `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice.gender` has existed since iOS 13 (the iOS 17 deployment target has it), so the catalog *is* gender-tagged; `unspecified` is handled as an acceptable middle-rank fallback rather than excluded. **(2) The AVFoundation-free seam.** The selector + the `VoiceDescriptor` / `VoiceGender` / `VoiceQuality` / `VoicePreference` value types import no AVFoundation, so the algorithm is unit-tested on the package's macOS `swift test` slice with a synthetic catalog (11 L1 cases: premium>enhanced>default, gender-primary, unspecified-fallback, prefix + exact-locale match, no-match→nil, nil-language→all, empty→nil, determinism). `SystemSpeechSynthesizer` projects the live `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice.speechVoices()` onto `[VoiceDescriptor]` via `descriptor(for:)` and feeds the selector. This mirrors the existing `SpeechSynthesizing` mock seam (CL-79) that already lets `VoiceReader`'s state machine test without real audio. **(3) Graceful degradation preserved.** When the selector returns `nil` (no installed voice matches the language), `resolveVoice(languageCode:)` falls back to the prior `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice(language:)` behavior, then to the platform default (the original CL-79 fallback) — so a no-voices locale degrades exactly as before, never worse. **(4) The gender preference store.** `VoicePreferenceStore` reads + writes `dod.voice.preferredGenderV1` (default `female`, unrecognized→`female`). It is device-local, never synced, never telemetered — no new privacy surface (parallel to AC-40.8's on-device posture). **(5) Phase a / Phase b split — why the Settings picker is deferred.** T-720 (this PR) ships the engine with **zero `SettingsView` / DesignSystem touch**, so it carries no conflict with the two open PRs that own `SettingsView` + its snapshot baselines: T-703 / PR #72 (the iCloud Sync section) and PR #86 (the Phase-a design tokens, which re-recorded `SettingsViewSnapshotTests`). T-721 (Phase b) ships the Settings → Voice picker — gender toggle + automatic/specific-voice mode + a "Download more voices" deep-link to Settings → Accessibility → Spoken Content → Voices — once #72 + #86 land, so the three SettingsView changes don't collide. Because the default preference is `female` and the engine reads it at every utterance, the quality upgrade is fully live from T-720 alone; Phase b only adds the *choice*, not the *fix*. **Spec/trace:** US-40 / AC-40.9 (quality tier), AC-40.10 (gender preference + Phase split), AC-40.11 (persistence contract); supersedes AC-40.2's `AVSpeechSynthesisVoice(language:)` resolution + CL-79's system-default-voice decision (the audio-session + interruption + off-by-default decisions in CL-79 are preserved unchanged). Implementing PR: T-720 (`feat/T-720-voice-quality-engine`). Touches only `Packages/DODFeatureRecipeDetail/**` (new `VoiceSelection.swift` + the `SystemSpeechSynthesizer` rewrite + two new L1 test files); no composition-root change, no analytics event (the engine reads a preference, it doesn't emit), no `e2e` label. **Numbering note:** CL-109 / T-720 / T-721 / Phase 17 are chosen to clear the in-flight PRs (#86 reserves CL-103..107 + Phase 16 + T-710..713; #87 reserves CL-108); if merge order collides, the later PR rebases to the next free slots per the established `tasks.md` parallelism convention.

## Phase 18 amendments (Site/app design coordination, 2026-05-29)

- **CL-110 (US-43 / T-710, 2026-05-29)** — **The surface tier reshuffle: primary `Surface` flips to white, `SurfaceElevated` collapses, cream's "everywhere" role narrows to a new `SurfaceWarm` reserved for the Saved tab + empty states + Cook Mode background.** Captured after @adamsned's first real-device install of TestFlight `1.0 (2)` and a live design audit of dutchovendaddy.com (homepage + `/dutch-oven-recipes/`). **The observed gap:** the site uses pure white (`#FFFFFF`) as its body backdrop with cream/beige showing only in the thin section-divider strip; the app shipped `#F9F6EF` cream across every screen. The cognitive cost of toggling between Safari and the app is a "color shift" that hurts the seamlessness the brand earns from the site. **Decision: flip `Surface.colorset` light to `#FFFFFF` and `SurfaceElevated.colorset` light to `#FFFFFF` (collapse), add a new `SurfaceWarm.colorset` light `#FAF6EE` token that inherits cream's old role specifically for surfaces where warmth earns its place** (the Saved tab — "you're at home with your collection"; the empty states — softness when nothing is there; the Cook Mode background — warm under low-light). **Why narrow cream instead of removing it:** the warmth is part of the brand; deleting it would over-correct toward sterile and lose the cooking-at-home register. The fix is to **let the site teach the app where warmth belongs**, not to evict warmth wholesale. **Rejected alternative — "keep cream everywhere":** preserves the current app aesthetic but at the cost of the seamlessness goal the user explicitly named. The site's design language is the anchor; the app should coordinate, not diverge. **Phase a (T-710, this PR)** lands the tokens; **Phase b (T-711)** and **Phase d (T-713)** wire `SurfaceWarm` into the actual screens. **Spec/trace:** US-43 / AC-43.1 + AC-43.2 + AC-43.3. Implementing PR: T-710 (`feat/T-710a-design-tokens-phase-a`).

- **CL-111 (US-43 / T-710, 2026-05-29)** — **Dark-mode parity is intentionally one-sided: the app's dark mode stays warm-brown, the site is light-only, and that asymmetry is correct.** **The observed gap-non-gap:** dutchovendaddy.com is light-mode-only — no system-appearance toggle. The app supports both light and dark; T-330 (Phase 8) shipped the dark palette with warm-brown surfaces (`#42210B` for `Surface`, `#553724` for `SurfaceElevated`) to preserve the low-light cooking-mode read affordance. **Decision: light-mode tokens align to the site (CL-110); dark-mode tokens stay warm.** Specifically: `Surface` dark deepens to `#1B140E` (was `#42210B` — slightly darker brown to read better next to the new `#FFFFFF` light), `SurfaceElevated` dark settles at `#281F19`, `SurfaceWarm` dark mirrors `SurfaceElevated` at `#281F19` so the warm-warm distinction collapses in dark (the cooking-mode register doesn't need it; light mode does). `SurfaceDivider` dark is `#3D2B1F` (a darker variant than the prior `CreamSubtle` dark that just inherited light's hex — see CL-112). **Why one-sided is correct:** the seamlessness goal is "moving between Safari and the app feels continuous." A user reading the site on Safari at 10 PM is on a white screen — they hit the app on a white screen. A user reading the app in dark Cook Mode at 10 PM is in their own moment; there's no Safari context to coordinate with. **Rejected alternative — "match white in dark too":** breaks Cook Mode legibility, fights every iOS dark-mode convention, and serves no observed user need. **Spec/trace:** US-43 / AC-43.1 + AC-43.2 + AC-43.3 (each token's dark variant). Implementing PR: T-710.

- **CL-112 (US-43 / T-710, 2026-05-29)** — **`creamSubtle` renames to `surfaceDivider`: the role-explicit name + the dark-variant fix.** **The observed defect:** the prior `CreamSubtle.colorset` carried `#E6DECF` in both light AND dark appearances (it never picked up a real dark variant when T-330 shipped the rest of the dark palette). That meant dividers became invisible-against-warm-brown in dark Cook Mode — the cream-on-brown contrast was zero. Combined with CL-110's surface-tier reshuffle, "Cream Subtle" stopped describing the role the token actually played (a thin section divider / sticky-header tint, not a warm backdrop). **Decision: rename `CreamSubtle.colorset` to `SurfaceDivider.colorset` (light `#E6DECF` unchanged, dark fixed to `#3D2B1F` to restore the dark-mode divider read); rename the Swift token `DODColor.creamSubtle` to `DODColor.surfaceDivider`.** Public API breaking change. **Why a rename and not a re-doc:** the prior name actively misled — a future contributor reading "creamSubtle" would assume it's a warm backdrop and use it where `SurfaceWarm` belongs (CL-110). Renaming locks the role at the call site. **Call site scope:** two files touched — `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Colors.swift` (declaration) and `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Tests/DODDesignSystemTests/ColorsTests.swift` (resolution test). **Zero feature-package call sites** (the token was registered but never consumed by any view code), so no downstream rebuild is blocked by the rename. **Spec/trace:** US-43 / AC-43.4 + AC-43.11. Implementing PR: T-710.

- **CL-113 (US-43 / T-710, 2026-05-29)** — **The typography weight + design-family shift: display tokens go from `.semibold` to `.bold` to match the site's section-header weight; ~~`heading` + `caption` adopt SF Rounded for the friendlier card-and-chip register~~ (SF Rounded family swap reverted by CL-128 / T-650, 2026-05-31 — joint @adamsned + @spencer0706 decision); a new `brand` token reserves the wordmark moments for Phase c.** **The observed gap:** the site's section headers ("Memorial Day Recipes", "Popular Cast Iron Recipes") render at a heavier weight than the app's `DODType.displayMedium` (`.semibold`) — they read with more gravity. The app's card title (`.heading` = `.system(.headline)`, default design) reads slightly sharper than the site's friendlier register. The site doesn't directly model "chip" copy, but the visual analog (the numbered "Popular" badges from Phase c) wants the rounder digit shapes of SF Rounded. **Decision (three moves):** **(1)** `DODType.displayLarge` and `displayMedium` shift from `.semibold` to `.bold` — closes the section-header weight gap. **(2)** ~~`DODType.heading` switches design family from `.default` to `.rounded` and `DODType.caption` switches from `.default` to `.rounded` with weight `.medium` — friendlier register for card titles + chips + time stamps without changing the font family asset.~~ **(SF Rounded family swap on `heading` + `caption` reverted by CL-128 / T-650, 2026-05-31; the `.medium` weight on `caption` stands — only the family-design portion of move (2) reverses.)** **(3)** A new `DODType.brand` token (~~`.system(size: 22, design: .rounded, weight: .bold)`~~ → `.system(size: 22, weight: .bold)` per CL-128 / T-650, 2026-05-31 — SF Rounded family swap reverted; size 22 + `.bold` weight stand) is reserved for "DUTCH OVEN DADDY" wordmark moments — splash, About, share-sheet preview cards. **No new font asset.** Constitution §3 default-no on third-party deps respected; SF Pro + SF Rounded are Apple-system. **Why `.bold` not `.heavy`:** Apple's `.bold` matches the site's perceived weight at default Dynamic Type sizes without the over-stylized look `.heavy` carries at AX5. ~~**Why rounded only on `heading` + `caption`, not `body`:** body text reads better in the default geometric family; rounded is friendlier-for-glance but lower-for-read. The two consumers that benefit (cards + chips) are glance surfaces.~~ (Rationale superseded by CL-128 / T-650 — the rounded-on-heading/caption move proved less compelling on-device than the original argument anticipated; uniform System font reads better across the app.) **L4 baseline impact:** every snapshot that resolves any of the four shifted tokens re-records (AC-43.9). **Spec/trace:** US-43 / AC-43.6 + AC-43.7 + AC-43.8 (AC-43.7 + AC-43.8 partially reversed by CL-128 / T-650 — see that entry for the full reversal trail). Implementing PR: T-710 (original); CL-128 / T-650 reverts the family-swap portion.

- **CL-114 (US-43 / T-711..T-713 — Phase b/c/d punt by design, 2026-05-29)** — **What Phase a does NOT ship + what each future phase still needs to decide.** Phase a's scope is the foundational token + typography layer — the unconditional surface-color + font-weight flip that every screen picks up on next launch without any view-code change. **Phase b (T-711 — magazine `RecipeCard` variant)**, **Phase c (T-712 — nav masthead + `DODBadge.Numbered`)**, and **Phase d (T-713 — Recipe Detail surface polish)** are NOT in this PR. **Decision: the four open questions captured at backlog-entry time stay open and migrate to the per-phase clarifications when they ship.** Specifically: **Phase b's open call** is **the brave Move-6** — does the gallery `RecipeCard` keep its time chip + excerpt or strip them so the photo + title do all the work (matching the site)? Recommended: ship Move-6 only on the magazine variant and keep the dense `RecipeCard.ListRow` unchanged; the Settings → Layout toggle decides per user. **Phase c's open call** is the nav-bar masthead asset — reuse `App/AppIcon.icon/Assets/DOD Master.png` at 32pt circular, or commission a dedicated thinner-stroke nav variant? Recommended: reuse the existing asset for v1; revisit when the asset shows up too dense in toolbar context on real device. **Phase d's open call** is the Recipe Detail hero aspect — does the detail screen also adopt the site's 3:4 portrait, or keep the iOS-native landscape for parallax + ingredient-list cohabitation? Recommended: portrait at the top with a parallax-aware crop, but verify on real device first. **Phase b/c/d's shared open call:** does the `DODFeed.layoutVariant` flag live on as a permanent Settings option or sunset after Phase b's A/B proves out? Recommended: permanent (users with strong format preferences keep their choice). **Why punt these:** each is a brave-call where dad + Spencer want to see the artifact on real device before locking. The flag is the seam. **Spec/trace:** US-43 (the reserved AC-43.12..AC-43.20 ceiling), backlog.md round-9 "Site ↔ app design coordination" graduation marker. Implementing PR: T-710 (this PR commits Phase a; Phase b/c/d ship under T-711/T-712/T-713 with their own CL entries).

- **CL-122 (US-12 amendment / T-644, 2026-05-30)** — **Cook-time filter chip replaced by an Apple-Timer-style two-wheel min/max range picker; the `≤` / `1 hr+` bucket labels are gone in favor of natural duration copy.** **The user request, verbatim:** "Replace the Search-page cook-time filter chip menu with an Apple-Timer-style two-wheel range picker (left wheel = min duration, right wheel = max duration)." The pre-T-644 surface was a `Menu` with four buckets (`≤ 15 min` / `≤ 30 min` / `≤ 60 min` / `1 hr+`) — a coarse "at-most" cap that didn't let the user say "show me only the 30-to-60 minute recipes" and that surfaced the math symbol `≤` in user-facing copy. **The new shape — model.** `CookTimeBucket` enum is deleted (no call site outside the chip menu + tests). `SearchFilters` gains two optional Int fields: `cookTimeMinSeconds: Int?` (nil = no lower bound, inclusive) and `cookTimeMaxSeconds: Int?` (nil = no upper bound, inclusive). `SearchFilters.apply(...)` filters items where `(cookTimeMinSeconds ?? 0) <= totalSeconds <= (cookTimeMaxSeconds ?? .max)`; items whose `totalSeconds` is unknown and the range is active still apply the existing "missing = MISS" rule (the T-637 / CL-106 hydration path fills the gap on cache miss — that contract is **unchanged**). `isAllDefault` is true only when both bounds are nil and every other filter is at its default. **Inverted-range edge case** (min > max): `apply()` correctly returns an empty set because no `totalSeconds` can satisfy both predicates — the empty-results state handles it visually; no UI enforcement of "min ≤ max" so the picker stays simple. **The new shape — UI.** New `CookTimeRangeSheet` view rendered as a `.presentationDetents([.medium])` half-sheet from a `Button`-replacing-the-old-Menu chip. Inside the sheet, two `Picker`s side by side in an `HStack`, both `.pickerStyle(.wheel)` — left wheel is **Min**, right wheel is **Max**. Both wheels share a value list whose first entry is `"Any"` (= nil bound for that side) followed by 5/10/15/20/30/45/60/75/90/120/150/180/240-minute durations. A single "Apply" button commits the selection on dismiss; a "Reset" link clears both wheels to Any. Drag-down dismisses without applying (no auto-commit on selection change — Apple-Timer pattern). Section headers above each wheel read "Min" and "Max" in centered secondary text. Accessibility labels are "Minimum cook time" / "Maximum cook time" / "Apply cook time filter". A new accessibility identifier `dod.search.cookTimeWheelMax` is added to the right wheel so the L5 E2E can deterministically scroll it. **The chip label states (four cases, no `≤` symbol anywhere).** Both nil → `"Any time"` (the existing default copy — preserved verbatim). Only min set → `"<min> or more"` (e.g. `"30 min or more"`, `"1 hr 30 min or more"`). Only max set → `"<max> or less"` (e.g. `"45 min or less"`, `"1 hr or less"`). Both set with `min != max` → `"<min>–<max>"` with an en-dash (e.g. `"30 min–1 hr 30 min"`). Both set with `min == max` → `"<value>"` alone (e.g. `"45 min"`). `isOn` is `cookTimeMinSeconds != nil || cookTimeMaxSeconds != nil`. **The duration formatter.** New pure helper `CookTimeFormatter.label(seconds:)` in `Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchFilters.swift` (lives next to the model so both the wheel rows and the chip label consume one source of truth): `seconds < 3600 → "\(s/60) min"`, `seconds == 3600 → "1 hr"`, `seconds % 3600 == 0 → "\(hours) hr"`, otherwise `"\(hours) hr \(minutes) min"`. Nil / zero is caller-handled (chip shows "Any time"; wheel shows "Any" entry). **Why the bucket model goes away rather than co-existing.** The min/max range is a strict superset — every bucket is expressible as a max-only range (`under30` = max 30 min, `overHour` = min 1 hr 1 min) — and keeping both surfaces would split the chip-label code path and the test surface. The bucket enum has zero non-UI call sites (verified via grep — only `SearchView.cookTimeChip`, `SearchFilters.apply`, and the test files reference it), so delete is cheaper than deprecate. **Why inverted-range is silently empty instead of UI-blocked.** Two reasons. **(a)** A clamp on the right wheel based on the left wheel's value (or vice versa) would force the wheel to scroll on the user under their finger when they crossed the boundary — exactly the Apple-Timer behavior the user said NOT to add. **(b)** The empty result set is a clear self-correcting signal ("oh, I picked min > max — let me re-roll") that doesn't need a separate validation chrome. The trade-off lands on simpler UI over earlier feedback. **What this does NOT change.** The chip-row idle gating (T-637 / CL-106 part 1 — `state != .idle` `@ViewBuilder` switch in `SearchView`) is unchanged; only the chip's body swaps from `Menu` to `Button`. The cook-time hydration path (T-637 / CL-106 part 2 — `kickOffCookTimeHydrationIfNeeded(...)` in `SearchViewModel`) still fires when either bound is set and the visible items' `totalSeconds` is missing from `lastTotalSecondsByRecipe`; the guard updates from `filters.cookTime != nil` to `filters.cookTimeMinSeconds != nil || filters.cookTimeMaxSeconds != nil` and the rest of the fan-out (20-item cap, fire-and-forget Task, reapplyFilters on landing) is byte-identical. The `dod.search.cookTimeChip` accessibility identifier is **preserved** (the same chip handle still opens the new sheet) so the existing T-638 / CL-107 negative-assertion test for the idle-state chip-row gating is unchanged. The title-precision filter (T-642 / CL-120 / REG-29), the category-name match path (T-643 / CL-121 / REG-30), the rotating Try slate (T-639 / CL-117), the recent-searches store (US-12 / AC-12.4), the telemetry hash (AC-3.6), and the local-ingredient v1-deferral (CL-120) are all untouched. The `categoryID` / `recentlyViewedOnly` slots on `SearchFilters` (CL-105 — kept on the model for forward compat after T-636 removed their UI surfaces) survive byte-identical. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Keeping `CookTimeBucket` and adding a "Custom range…" entry to the menu** — preserves the buckets-as-discoverable-defaults but ships two parallel surfaces (menu and sheet) and two parallel label-format paths; the user asked for the wheel-picker as the primary affordance, not as an escape hatch under a menu. **(b) Snap-on-wheel-release clamping (auto-fix min > max)** — Apple's own Timer app doesn't clamp; user expectation is for the wheel to stay where they put it. **(c) Three-wheel hours / minutes / both layout** — overkill for a 0–4 hour range whose natural rounding is 15- and 30-minute steps; the curated value list (13 entries) is easier to spin than two unconstrained wheels. **(d) Min-only wheel + "or more / or less / exact" mode switcher** — adds a third UI control to discriminate intent the two-wheel layout already encodes via the Any sentinels on each side. **(e) Putting the formatter in `DODSupport` instead of `DODFeatureSearch`** — the formatter is only consumed by Search today; lifting it to the support layer is premature abstraction. If a future surface (Recipe Detail's total-time chip, the Cook Mode hero label, a shopping-list item duration display) wants the same format, move it then with the consumers it acquires. **Spec/trace:** US-12 amendment (AC-12.2 — the chip semantics flip from "bucket" to "min/max range" — the four label states + the wheel-picker sheet contract); AC-12.3 still holds verbatim (filter composes; hydration still fires on cache miss). New **REG-31** locks the "no `≤` / `≥` symbols in cook-time copy; max-only narrows; sheet renders two wheels with Min on the left" contract. Implementing PR: T-644 (`feat/T-644-cook-time-wheel-range`). Test additions: **5 new L1 cases** in `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchFiltersTests` (max-only narrows, min+max range, both-nil passthrough, inverted-range empty, missing-totalSeconds-is-still-MISS); **5 new L1 cases** in a new `CookTimeFormatterTests` suite (30 min / 1 hr / 1 hr 30 min / 2 hr / 4 hr boundaries); **5 new L1 cases** for the chip-label computation (a new pure helper `cookTimeChipLabel(min:max:)` in the same file as `CookTimeFormatter` — both nil, only min, only max, both, min==max). The existing `SearchViewModelT637Tests.cookTimeFilterHydratesUncachedItemsAndNarrows` + `filterMutationAfterLatestRecipesReAppliesAgainstLatestSet` rewrite to use `cookTimeMaxSeconds = 30 * 60` instead of `.cookTime = .under30`; the hydration contract is asserted unchanged. The existing `SearchFiltersTests.cookTimeBucketAdmitsAtMostThatTotal` / `overHourBucketIsTheStrictComplement` / `filtersCompose` rewrite to the min/max model; `SearchResultMergerTests.composeCategoryAndCookTimeFiltersForFreshRESTResults` rewrites to use `cookTimeMaxSeconds = 60 * 60` / `15 * 60` instead of the bucket enum. The four `FilterChipRowSnapshotTests` baselines re-record because the selected-state chip label changes from `"≤ 30 min"` to a min/max-derived string (the host fixture switches to `SearchFilters(cookTimeMaxSeconds: 30 * 60)`). The L5 `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests.test_search_cook_time_filter_narrows_results` rewrites for the new sheet: tap the chip (same `dod.search.cookTimeChip` identifier), wait for the sheet, scroll the right wheel (the new `dod.search.cookTimeWheelMax` identifier) to `"30 min"`, tap "Apply", assert results narrow with the same `XCTNSPredicateExpectation` polling-wait pattern T-638 / CL-107 codified. The `e2e` label IS applied because the search journey contract is user-facing (the filter sheet replaces a menu the user has been tapping every day). **Numbering note:** CL-122 / T-644 is the next free CL slot above CL-121 (T-643). REG-31 is the next free REG slot above REG-30 (T-643 / CL-121).

- **CL-121 (US-12 amendment / US-29 amendment / T-643, 2026-05-30)** — **Category-name match path added alongside the T-642 title-match path so typed queries that name a known WP category surface that category's posts, restoring the "Dessert Recipes" navigation flow T-642 inadvertently broke.** **The regression T-642 introduced.** T-642 / CL-120 added a strict title-precision filter on top of WP REST `?search=<q>`: the merger keeps only posts whose `title.rendered` matches the query via `TitleSearchMatcher` (exact / substring / plural-swap / Levenshtein-1). That fixed the Nacho-Bug body-only false positives. **Side-effect surfaced same day** (2026-05-30, manual verify against `dutchovendaddy.com`): typed queries that name a category — `"Dessert Recipes"`, `"Chicken"`, `"Side Dish"` — now return 0–5 results instead of the dozens the user expects. Root cause: the recipes the user is reaching for are titled `"Apple Crumble"` / `"Cherry Cobbler"` / `"Sticky Toffee Pudding"`, never `"Dessert Recipes"`. Before T-642 those posts surfaced via WP's body/category match; T-642 trimmed that path because it was the source of the Nacho noise. The user-facing breakage: tap the **"Dessert Recipes"** Try pill (or type the phrase) and the result set is empty/wrong; the 53 desserts the category contains never appear. **Live-API truth (2026-05-30):** `GET /wp/v2/categories?per_page=100&orderby=count&order=desc` returns the top categories by post count — **240 Latest Recipes** (id 1590), **53 Dessert Recipes** (id 336), **46 One Pot Dutch Oven Recipes** (id 1435), **32 Chicken and Poultry Recipes** (id 338), **28 Beef and Red Meat Recipes** (id 339), **28 Side Dish Recipes** (id 334), **27 Breads and Pizza Recipes** (id 777), **26 Dutch Oven Camp Recipes** (id 337). All eight are real navigation targets the Try-slate surfaces; none of them have titles individual recipes inherit. **The fix — additive parallel path, no change to T-642's title filter.** `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` fires **two paths in parallel via `async let`** and unions the result. **Path A (unchanged from T-642):** fetch `?search=<query>&per_page=100` → run through `TitleSearchMatcher` → tier-sorted title matches. **Path B (new):** check if the query matches any known category name via a new `CategoryNameMatcher.match(query:in:)` helper. If yes, fetch `?categories=<id>&per_page=100` for each matched category (cap at 2 categories to bound network cost) and include all returned posts. **Merge:** union of A + B, deduplicated by post id. Order: Path A's tier-ranked title matches first (exact → substring → fuzzy, T-642 contract intact), then Path B's category-only contributions in WP's natural date-desc order (REST default for `?categories=` fetches). When both paths return empty, the existing empty-results state fires. **Path B failure does NOT block Path A** — if `?categories=` errors out, the viewmodel logs and renders Path A's title matches alone (graceful degradation, same shape as T-642's pre-fix offline behavior). **`CategoryNameMatcher.match(query:in:)` — the new pure helper.** Lives in `DODSupport` (mirrors `TitleSearchMatcher`'s placement — pure, Foundation-only, reusable by future surfaces like Spotlight indexing or App Intents without a feature-package dependency). Normalizes both sides identically to `TitleSearchMatcher.normalize(_:)` (HTML-entity decode via `HTMLSanitizer.plainText`, lowercase, punctuation→space, whitespace collapse) — re-using the same normalizer means `&amp;` / `&#8217;` in category names never leak through, same as title side. **The match rules (in order).** For each category, derive its **"topic"** by stripping a trailing `" recipes"` token from the normalized name — `"Dessert Recipes" → "dessert"`, `"Chicken and Poultry Recipes" → "chicken and poultry"`, `"One Pot Dutch Oven Recipes" → "one pot dutch oven"`. Categories that don't end in `"recipes"` use the full normalized name as the topic (defensive — none on the live API today but the rule shouldn't crash on a future taxonomy that doesn't follow the suffix convention). A category matches when **any** of: (a) normalized query equals the full normalized category name (`"Dessert Recipes"`), (b) normalized query equals the topic (`"dessert"`), (c) normalized query is a substring of the topic and the query is ≥ 4 characters (`"Chicken"` matches `"chicken and poultry"`; the 4-char floor guards against single-word junk like `"the"`), or (d) the topic is a substring of the normalized query (`"Camp Recipes"` → topic `"camp"` is a substring of normalized query `"camp recipes"`, and `"Dutch Oven Camp Recipes"` matches because its topic `"dutch oven camp"` is a substring of `"dutch oven camp recipes"`). **Junk-query guards.** If the normalized query is exactly `"recipe"` or `"recipes"` (single-token, suffix-only) it would match every category in the catalog — explicitly reject so the merger doesn't fan out 53+ posts when the user types a generic word. The 4-character floor on the substring-of-topic rule covers other common junk (`"the"`, `"a"`, `"of"`). **Top-2 cap on matched categories.** When a query legitimately matches multiple categories (`"Dutch Oven"` matches both `"One Pot Dutch Oven Recipes"` and `"Dutch Oven Camp Recipes"`), sort matches by recipe count desc and take the top two. Bound is on network cost — two `?categories=` fetches of ≤ 100 posts each is the wire ceiling for any single typed query under this path, and the result set above ~100 posts is unscannable in the search UI anyway. **Why the top-2 cap and not a top-1 cap:** dad routinely chains `"Beef"` queries hoping to see both the beef-and-red-meat category and beef-titled recipes from sibling categories; the second category is genuinely useful, and the per-query wire cost stays under 100 KB worst-case. **Why a top-2 cap and not unlimited:** capping at 2 means a query that grazes 5+ categories (a fabricated example: `"recipes"` itself if not junk-guarded, or a single-character category like `"a"`) cannot fan out 5 × 100-post REST calls. **Why apply normalization once on both sides:** matches like `"DESSERT recipes"` (uppercase) must hit `"Dessert Recipes"`; using the same `TitleSearchMatcher.normalize(_:)` (which is `internal` to `DODSupport` — `CategoryNameMatcher` accesses it via in-module `@testable`-style internal-module access, no public surface change) keeps the contract identical to the title side so `"&amp;"` / `"&#8217;"` / em-dashes normalize once with one rule. **Reuses existing REST surface — no new endpoint.** `WPRestClient.posts(categoryID:page:perPage:)` already exists for the Feed (T-081) and Category Recipes (T-091) screens; this PR adds `SearchDependencies.fetchPosts(categoryID:limit:) async throws -> [RecipeListItem]` that calls `client.posts(categoryID: id, page: 1, perPage: limit)` and propagates the result. No new REST URL is constructed, no new `_embed` flag is added, the `URLCache` bypass (CL-50) is unchanged. **Net wire impact.** Per typed query: Path A is unchanged (~50 KB). Path B fires only when the query matches a known category — worst case 2 × ~100-post pages ≈ ~100 KB for a `"Dessert Recipes"`-class navigation query. For queries that don't name a category (the common case — `"chili"`, `"casserole"`, `"nachos"`), Path B short-circuits at the matcher returning `[]` and contributes zero wire ops. **What this does NOT change.** The title-precision filter (T-642 / CL-120 / REG-29) is byte-identical — the helper, the merger's title-path, the `searchPageSize = 100` constant, the per_page bump, the tier ordering. The "Latest Recipes" Try-pill special case (T-637 / CL-106) is **untouched** — it routes through `surfaceLatestRecipes(limit:)` which never enters `performSearch()`, so the new Path B never fires for that pill (the special case still wins for `Latest Recipes`). The local-ingredient pass v1-deferral (CL-120) is unchanged. The Try-pill flow (US-29 / AC-29.1) is unchanged at the pill level — the pill still calls `selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)` which sets `viewModel.query = name`; the new behavior is one layer down in `performSearch()` where Path B now also surfaces the category posts. The cook-time hydration path (CL-106), the rotating Try slate (CL-117), the URLCache bypass (CL-50 / REG-18) are all unchanged. **Considered and rejected: (a) putting the category fetch on a separate "category" tab/section** — disruptive to the search UX (a query that lands in two tabs is a usability regression dad's existing TestFlight chord doesn't want); the merged-set approach preserves the single-result-list shape the user already knows. **(b) fetching the union via WP's `?search=<q>&_embed=wp:term` and post-filtering on category match** — `_embed=wp:term` doubles the JSON payload per post for a metadata join the client could compute from the category id map it already has; cheaper to fire a parallel `?categories=` call only when we know we need it. **(c) using the WP `wp-json/wp/v2/categories?search=<q>` endpoint to find matching categories instead of a local matcher** — adds a third REST round-trip per typed query, when the category list is already fully loaded into `availableCategories` at startup; the local matcher is both faster and offline-correct (the matcher works after the category list is hydrated, even if the live REST is down between launches). **(d) a top-1 cap on matched categories** — too tight for the `"Dutch Oven"` case where two categories are legitimately relevant; the top-2 cap is the cheapest expansion that covers the observed multi-match queries. **(e) substring-of-topic without the 4-character floor** — `"the"` would match `"Sides and Vegetables (the)"` (hypothetical) or other future categories; the 4-char floor is a small predictable guard with no observed false-negative cost on the live category list. **Spec/trace:** US-12 amendment (AC-12.1 — the merger's result composition now lists Path A + Path B union with the dedup-by-id rule), US-29 amendment (AC-29.1 — the Try-pill tap → typed-query path now surfaces category posts in addition to title matches when the pill name matches a category), new **REG-30** locks the "category-name-matched queries surface that category's posts AND title matches; queries that don't name a category remain title-match-only (Nacho contract preserved)" contract. Implementing PR: T-643 (`fix/T-643-search-category-match`). Test additions: **8 new L1 tests** in `DODSupportTests.CategoryNameMatcherTests` (8-category live-API fixture — `"Dessert Recipes"` matches Dessert, `"dessert"` topic-match, `"Chicken"` substring-of-topic, `"Chicken and Poultry"` full-topic, `"Side Dish"` topic, `"Camp Recipes"` substring-either-direction, `"Nachos"` empty, `"recipe"` junk-rejected, `"the"` under-4-char rejected, `"latest"` matches Latest Recipes, `"DESSERT recipes"` case-insensitive, multi-match top-2 cap, HTML-entity normalization), **4 new L1 tests** in `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchViewModelT643Tests` (pipeline integration — `"Dessert Recipes"` Path B contributes 53 desserts + Path A empty → 53 in `viewModel.items`; `"Chicken"` Path A 5 title hits + Path B 32 category hits → union deduped with title-matches first; `"Nachos"` unchanged from T-642 — Path B empty so 4 title matches stand; Path B failure → Path A results render alone), **1 new L2 (gated live-API) test** in the existing live-API suite asserting `"Dessert Recipes"` typed query returns ≥ 30 results. The `e2e` label IS applied because the search journey contract is user-facing (typing → results) and the pipeline change touches the path every search query traverses. **Numbering note:** CL-121 / T-643 is the next free CL slot above CL-120 (T-642 just merged). REG-30 is the next free REG slot above REG-29 (T-642 / CL-120).

- **CL-120 (US-12 amendment / US-29 amendment / T-642, 2026-05-30)** — **The Nacho Bug fix: the Search pipeline pairs a wider candidate pool with a title-precision filter so typed queries surface only titles that contain the word (with plural/singular + Levenshtein-1 typo tolerance), never body-only WP matches; the local-ingredient pass is v1-deferred.** Graduates the round-9 backlog item "Search is too strict — 'nachos' doesn't find 'Cast Iron Skillet Nachos'" (captured 2026-05-29, @adamsned, TestFlight 1.0 (2) install feedback at `backlog.md:45-64`). **The live-API truth.** A 2026-05-30 curl pass against `https://www.dutchovendaddy.com/wp-json/wp/v2/posts?search=nachos&per_page=100` returned exactly **4** posts whose titles contain "nacho": ids **524** (Super Nacho Dip), **274** (Tater Tot Nachos), **5016** (Pulled Pork Nachos), **736** (Cast Iron Skillet Nachos). Today's app pipeline at `per_page=20` returned 20 results — only 3 of which were real title matches; dropped [736] Cast Iron Skillet Nachos entirely (WP's relevance-ranker buried it past position 20) and surfaced 17 body-matched false positives (Skillet Birria, Skillet Chocolate Chip Cookie, Tequila Lime Chicken, **Cast Iron Bacon Wrapped Pickles**, etc. — bacon-wrapped pickles is the verbatim user-reported false positive). At `per_page=100` the same query returned all 4 title matches plus 19 false positives — the candidate pool widens to catch the missing recipe; the title filter drops the noise. **The fix (two moves, paired).** **(1) Wider candidate pool — recall.** `WPRestClient.search(...)` defaults to a new `searchPageSize = 100` constant instead of the list-endpoint `defaultPageSize = 20`. List endpoints are unchanged (the user scrolls them; widening would cost a real wire-payload spike on every Feed open); the SEARCH path needs the wider pool exactly once per typed-and-debounced query so the post-filter step sees every title-bearing match WP might bury. WP REST caps `per_page` at 100, so this is the maximal single-page widening. Net wire impact: ~50 KB JSON per query vs ~15 KB pre-fix (~3.5× the prior payload) — acceptable for the precision win because the filter discards most of it client-side and the existing `URLCache` bypass (CL-50) is unchanged. **(2) Title-precision filter — precision.** A new pure helper `TitleSearchMatcher` (in `DODSupport`, no dependencies beyond Foundation) returns the strongest match tier between a query and a title: `.exact` (normalized equal) > `.substring` (joined query is a contiguous substring of the normalized title) > `.fuzzy` (plural/singular swap OR Levenshtein-1 typo on at least one query token, with **every** query token required to match for multi-token queries). Normalization: HTML-entity decode (reuses `HTMLSanitizer.plainText(from:)` — WP `title.rendered` carries `&amp;` / `&#8217;` / em-dashes routinely), lowercase, punctuation→space, whitespace collapse. **Plural/singular rule (predictable + cheap).** Each query token has its trailing "s" toggled and the swapped form is substring-checked against the title. Covers "nacho" → "Nachos" and "nachos" → "Nacho" — the user's complaint. The rule is per-token gated for multi-token queries: "super nacho" must NOT fuzzy-match "Just Nachos" because "super"/"supers" appears nowhere in the title even though "nacho"→"nachos" does. **Levenshtein-1 rule.** Each query token must have at least one title token within edit-distance 1 (insertion / deletion / substitution) AND a length-difference ≤ 1 (cheap shortcut so the matrix only builds for plausibly-near pairs). Covers "nahcoz" → "nachos" (single substitution). Distance-2 is **intentionally rejected** — admits too much noise on the small cookbook corpus ("naxxos" must NOT match "nachos" per the round-9 backlog's "two typos is too loose" rule). Iterative Wagner–Fischer (two-row variant) in ~20 lines — no third-party dependency. **The filter is applied in `SearchResultMerger.merge(...)`.** Each REST candidate runs through `TitleSearchMatcher.match(query:title:)` and the merger keeps only the non-nil matches, sorted by tier (`.exact` first, then `.substring`, then `.fuzzy`), stable within tier on the WP source order. The pre-fix merger admitted any REST hit into an "excerpt" tier on the assumption WP had already filtered noise — that assumption broke catastrophically on short common words like "nachos". **v1 deferral: the local-ingredient pass is dropped from the merged set.** Per the user's contract (title precision), and per the broader "Make search way better" backlog entry (which already captures ingredient-search as a future enhancement), the local-ingredient surface is removed from the merger output for v1 of this fix. The `localIngredientResults` parameter survives on the merger signature for source-compat (the call site in `SearchViewModel.performSearch()` is byte-unchanged) and the dependencies still expose `searchIngredients(matching:)` + `cachedListItems(forIDs:)` — a future task can re-enable the tier as a labeled section without changing the seam. The offline edge case (`offline && local-hits → .offline` state instead of `.results`) is the deliberate consequence of the deferral, locked by an updated test. **Why `TitleSearchMatcher` lives in `DODSupport`, not `DODFeatureSearch`.** The matcher is pure and Foundation-only. Future surfaces — Spotlight indexing (US-10), App Intents, share-extension search — will want the same contract. Lifting it into the support layer means every future caller gets the same precision rules without a feature-package dependency. **Considered and rejected: (a) `wp-json/wp/v2/search` endpoint** (WP's relevance-search-suggestion endpoint) — returns thin "subtype": "post" results lacking `title.rendered` HTML / featured-media / categories, so the result set would still need a second `/posts?include=<id>,<id>...` round-trip to hydrate — more wire ops, no recall win. **(b) Stem-based fuzzy** — Porter stemming or similar would cover "running" → "run" cases that plural-swap misses, but adds 200+ lines of stem rules for a cookbook corpus where the relevant fuzziness is overwhelmingly typos and plurals; the simple rule pair covers the user's complaint and the verbatim 10 L1 fixture cases pin the contract. **(c) Levenshtein-2** — admits "naxxos" → "nachos" which the round-9 diagnosis explicitly listed as "too loose." **(d) Server-side WP plugin** — out of constitution scope (the app does not edit the WP install). **What this does NOT change.** The 300ms debounce (AC-3.1), the SHA-256 query telemetry (AC-3.6), the empty-state UI (AC-3.4), the offline path (AC-3.7), the recent-searches store (US-12 / AC-12.4), the filter chips (US-12 / AC-12.2..AC-12.3), the categories-tab path (US-29 / AC-29.1 Try pills — the Try-pill tap still routes through the normal text-search path which now goes through the precision filter), the cook-time hydration path (CL-106 / T-637), the rotating Try slate (CL-117 / T-639), the URLCache/CDN bypass (CL-50 / REG-18). **Spec/trace:** US-12 amendment (AC-12.1 — the merger's ranking now lists title-tier-only as the v1 contract with the local-ingredient pass deferred), US-29 amendment (the title-precision contract reinforces the AC-29.1 "tap a pill, see related recipes" promise), new **REG-29** locks the "search returns only title-matched results — never body-matched-only" contract. Implementing PR: T-642 (`fix/T-642-nacho-bug-title-precision`). Test additions: **17 new L1 tests** in `DODSupportTests.TitleSearchMatcherTests` (exact / substring / case-insensitive / plural-fuzzy / singular-fuzzy / Levenshtein-1 / Levenshtein-2-rejects / verbatim Bacon-Wrapped-Pickles no-match / HTML-entity normalization / multi-token-all-must-match-substring + reject-partial / empty-query / empty-title / tier-order / the 4 known live-API titles / Levenshtein-direct cases / normalize-strips-punctuation-and-entities), **1 new L2 test** in `DODNetworkingTests.WPRestClientSearchTests` (`searchUsesWidePageSizeForTitlePrecision` — pins the per_page=100 bump on the SEARCH path independently of the list endpoints' `defaultPageSize=20`), **2 new L1 tests** in `DODFeatureSearchTests.SearchResultMergerTests` (`bodyOnlyMatchesAreDropped` — the verbatim Bacon-Wrapped-Pickles false-positive contract; `nachoBugFixSurfacesFourKnownTitleMatches` — the live-API end-to-end seal: a candidate set of 4 known nacho titles + 4 body-only false positives surfaces exactly the 4 titles after the filter), plus updates to two existing viewmodel tests where they relied on the pre-CL-120 "any REST hit counts" behavior. The `e2e` label IS applied because the search journey contract is user-facing (typing → results) and the filter touches the merger seam every search path traverses. **Numbering note:** CL-120 / T-642 is the next free CL slot above CL-119 (T-641). REG-29 is the next free REG slot above REG-28 (T-730 / CL-115). The originally-proposed REG-22 in the round-10 spec task brief was already taken by US-22 (lock-screen widget); REG-29 is the corrected slot.

- **CL-119 (US-29 amendment / T-641, 2026-05-30)** — **The rotating "Try" pill pool excludes "Uncategorized" (and any future junk WP categories) via a slug-based exclusion set applied at the `topTrySlatePool` boundary.** Hot-fix follow-up to T-639 / CL-117 — landed the same day after a manual verify against the live `dutchovendaddy.com` WP REST `/wp/v2/categories?per_page=100` returned exactly 28 active categories with "Uncategorized" (slug `uncategorized`, count 1) sitting at the bottom. When the rotating slate lands "Uncategorized" in one of its five shuffled slots, tapping the pill fires `client.search(query: "Uncategorized")` — a literal text search for the word, which is meaningless against the cookbook corpus and weakens the AC-29.7 exploration goal of "tap a pill, see related recipes." **The fix.** Add `static let excludedTryPoolSlugs: Set<String> = ["uncategorized"]` to the `SearchViewModel+T639.swift` extension (mirrors the existing `trySlatePoolSize` / `trySlateVisibleCount` static-on-extension pattern) and apply at the `topTrySlatePool` boundary via `.filter { !Self.excludedTryPoolSlugs.contains($0.slug.lowercased()) }` before the existing `sorted { $0.count > $1.count }.prefix(Self.trySlatePoolSize)` chain. The set holds canonical lowercase WP slugs and the category side is `.lowercased()` at compare time, so the lookup is case-insensitive without needing a custom hasher. **Why slug, not name or id.** Slug is the canonical WP identifier and is byte-stable across category renames (WP preserves the slug on rename unless explicitly republished); the visible `name` is editor-mutable and the `id` is per-install (unstable across the staging / production WP environments). Slug is the right join key for an exclusion list that ships in the app binary. **Why a `Set` and not a single-element check.** The set is the extensibility seam — when @spencer0706 or @adamsned spots another junk category surfacing in the rotation in the future (e.g. an auto-generated "Tag" import, an internal "Drafts" taxonomy), the fix is to add the slug to the set in one place rather than refactoring the filter logic. **Pool-size impact.** Live WP returned 28 categories on 2026-05-30 with Uncategorized at the bottom. Excluding it shrinks the pool from 28 → 27, comfortably wide for the AC-29.7 5-shuffle (5 non-pinned slots × 1 pin = 6 visible). No curated 1:1 replacement needed — the rotation just stops surfacing the junk. The AC-29.7 deterministic top-up branch (pool smaller than `visibleCount - 1`) is unreachable in production today because 27 ≫ 5; the branch still pins the empty-pool / synthesized-Latest-Recipes contract for the REST-failure path. **What this does NOT change.** The `pickTrySlate(...)` helper is byte-identical (the filter is upstream at the pool boundary, not inside the shuffle). The `cachedTrySlate` storage + the T-640 / CL-118 only-cache-full-slate rule are unchanged. The Latest-Recipes pin contract is unchanged (the pin is filtered against id 1590 / name match, not slug, and `latest-recipes` is not in `excludedTryPoolSlugs`). The `topCategorySuggestions` public surface (still consumed by no internal call site — backward-compat only) is unchanged because it pre-dates `topTrySlatePool` and is intentionally not filtered (a future caller, if any, would want the raw top-5; if a new consumer needs the exclusion, lift the filter into a sibling helper rather than mutating `topCategorySuggestions`). The L4 `SearchViewSnapshotTests` baselines for the idle state do not re-record because the fixture pool used in the snapshot test path does not include Uncategorized. **Spec/trace:** US-29 amendment / AC-29.7 amendment (one sentence noting the exclusion-set filter at the pool boundary; canonical lowercase slug is the match key; extensible). Implementing PR: T-641 (`fix/T-641-try-pool-exclude-junk`). Test additions: three new L1 tests — `topTrySlatePool_excludes_uncategorized` (fixture with Uncategorized + 5 others → returns 5, Uncategorized absent), `topTrySlatePool_exclusion_is_case_insensitive_on_slug` (fixture with slug `"Uncategorized"` uppercase-U → still filtered via the `.lowercased()` call), `pickTrySlate_does_not_surface_excluded_categories` (sanity end-to-end: pool that contained Uncategorized produces a slate that doesn't contain it). No `e2e` label (three-line filter + tests; no nav / persistence / composition-root touch).

- **CL-118 (US-29 amendment / T-640, 2026-05-30)** — **The rotating "Try" pill slate's cache rule is tightened to only-cache-full-slate to close the cold-start race that locked the user to a single pinned pill for the session.** Hot-fix follow-up to T-639 / CL-117 — landed within hours of T-639's PR merge after @spencer0706 + @adamsned both observed on TestFlight that the Search-tab idle "Try" row showed only the "Latest Recipes" pill with no shuffled others. **The confirmed root cause.** `SearchViewModel.displayedTrySlate` (in `SearchViewModel+T639.swift`) cached the slate unconditionally on first access via `cachedTrySlate = slate`. The view appears before `loadCategoriesIfNeeded()` resolves the async category fetch, so the first read of `displayedTrySlate` fires while `availableCategories` is still empty → `topTrySlatePool` is empty → `pickTrySlate(from: [], ...)` returns the AC-29.7 empty-pool fallback `[syntheticLatestRecipes]` (length 1) → that 1-pill slate cached forever → categories load milliseconds later but the cache hit short-circuits the recompute → the user sees one pill for the rest of the session. **The fix: only cache when the slate hits the full visible count.** `displayedTrySlate` guards `cachedTrySlate = slate` behind `if slate.count >= Self.trySlateVisibleCount` and the cache-hit branch also guards on `cached.count >= Self.trySlateVisibleCount`. Partial / single-pill slates are returned to the caller but NOT cached. SwiftUI's `@Observable` triggers a re-render on the `availableCategories` mutation when `loadCategoriesIfNeeded()` lands → `IdleSuggestionsView` re-reads `displayedTrySlate` → cache miss → the full 6-pill slate computes and caches. From there the stable-within-session contract holds exactly as before. **Why only-cache-full-slate and not a different strategy.** Considered: (a) **prefetch categories before instantiating `SearchViewModel`** — would solve the symptom but couples the search VM lifecycle to the categories fetch's completion; failure modes (REST timeout, offline) would push the entire Search tab open until the load completes, which is worse UX than the current "show Latest Recipes immediately, expand to the full row when categories land" path. (b) **observe `availableCategories` and explicitly invalidate the cache on first non-empty assignment** — adds an `@Observable` observer or a manual property-observer hook, which is more wiring than the one-line guard. The guard exploits the existing @Observable re-render path the view already uses for every other VM property, so it's strictly less code. (c) **make `displayedTrySlate` `async` and await categories** — turns a synchronous SwiftUI `var` read into an async computation, which doesn't fit the AC-29.7 `[DODDomain.Category]` non-async return contract. **What this does NOT change.** The `pickTrySlate(...)` helper's behavior (still pinned-Latest-Recipes-first + shuffle remainder + deterministic top-up + empty-pool fallback to the single pinned pill) is byte-identical — only the consuming `displayedTrySlate` accessor's caching rule changes. The `cachedTrySlate` backing storage (declared on the main `SearchViewModel` type) is unchanged. The L4 `SearchViewSnapshotTests` baselines for the idle state do not re-record because the rendered count in the snapshot test fixture's path (categories pre-loaded via the test fake before the snapshot's `body` read) was already at the full count. **Spec/trace:** US-29 amendment / AC-29.7 amendment (the one sentence "cached only when it reaches the full visible count" inline). Implementing PR: T-640 (`fix/T-640-try-pool-cache-race`). Test additions: three new L1 tests in `SearchViewModelTests` — `emptyPoolAtFirstAccessDoesNotCachePartialSlate`, `fullSlateCachesAndIsStableAcrossReads`, `partialPoolSmallerThanVisibleCountDoesNotCache`. No `e2e` label (single-line cache-rule change + tests; no nav / persistence / composition-root touch). **Numbering note:** CL-118 / T-640 (this entry) is the next free CL slot above CL-117. The "T-640" task ID collides with the Phase 10 "Recipes & Articles tab + article rendering" task that shipped earlier; the collision is deliberate per the round-10 spec author's instruction to use T-640 because this is the immediate hot-fix to T-639 and the two tasks live in completely different code areas (Phase 10's T-640 owned `App/AppTab.swift` + `FeedView.swift` + the article-renderer package — zero overlap with the search-feature-package change here). Both entries coexist in `tasks.md` under the same `### T-640 — ...` heading slot via the second-T-640-as-amendment convention (the round-7 task entry stays; the round-10 hot-fix is appended below CL-117's T-639 entry to keep cross-references readable).

- **CL-117 (US-29 amendment / T-639, 2026-05-30)** — **The Search-tab idle "Try" pill section rotates per cold launch over a wider source pool, stable within session, with "Latest Recipes" pinned first on every render.** Graduates the round-10 backlog entry "Rotating Try pill pool on Search page" (captured 2026-05-30, @spencer0706). The pre-T-639 path showed a fixed slate of 5 category pills (the `topCategorySuggestions` top-5-by-count) — the **same set every time**, so a returning user memorized them and the section stopped reading as a discovery affordance. **The six constraints:** **(1) Wider source pool.** `SearchViewModel.topCategorySuggestions` (top-5) is too narrow to rotate over. T-639 adds a sibling property `topTrySlatePool` that returns the **top-30** WP categories by recipe count (the production `dutchovendaddy.com` site has well over 30 active categories so this never runs short in practice). `topCategorySuggestions` stays for backward compat — no other call site touches it. **(2) Shuffle on every cold launch.** "Cold launch" = fresh `SearchViewModel` instance. Implementation: a lazy `displayedTrySlate: [DODDomain.Category]` stored property is computed **once** on first access via `Array.shuffled()` (system RNG, which is per-process-seeded by the kernel — different on every launch with no extra wiring). **(3) Stable within session.** The slate persists across `IdleSuggestionsView` re-creates (tab switches, navigation) because `displayedTrySlate` lives on the viewmodel, which survives the view lifetime — `IdleSuggestionsView` consumes `viewModel.displayedTrySlate` instead of `viewModel.topCategorySuggestions`. The view holds no `@State` slate of its own. **(4) Visible count is a fixed constant of 6.** Today's `prefix(5)` returns 5 pills; T-639 bumps the visible count to **6** (one pinned Latest Recipes + 5 shuffled) — the small bump gives the rotation a more visible "pool" feel without crowding the row beyond what the FlowLayout can handle on the narrowest device. Layout never shifts between launches; only the selection inside the 6-slot row rotates. **(5) Pool-too-small guardrail.** If `topTrySlatePool` returns fewer than `visibleCount - 1` items (the non-pinned slot count), `pickTrySlate(...)` tops up by **deterministic repetition** of whatever the pool produced — same category fires the same tap handler, so the user sees duplicate pills rather than a short row (the spec's "don't render a short row" rule). Empty pool (REST failed before `loadCategoriesIfNeeded` populated `availableCategories`) returns **just the pinned Latest Recipes** synthesized in-line — safer than rendering an empty Try section because the canonical "what's new" affordance is the single most valuable pill in the row. **(6) "Latest Recipes" pinned first.** The Latest-Recipes category (id **1590**, slug `latest-recipes` — the same canonical pin from T-637 / CL-106) is filtered OUT of the rotation source pool before shuffling, then **prepended** as the first element of the returned slate. This means: the Latest-Recipes pill **always renders first**, never gets shuffled away, and the rotation only swaps the trailing 5 pills. If the source pool does not contain a Latest-Recipes entry (REST returned without it for some reason), the helper synthesizes one in-line (`Category(id: 1590, name: "Latest Recipes", slug: "latest-recipes", count: 0)`) so the pin contract holds unconditionally. **Implementation seam:** `SearchViewModel.pickTrySlate(from: [Category], visibleCount: Int, using: inout RandomNumberGenerator) -> [Category]` is a `static` pure function on the viewmodel — fully unit-testable against a deterministic `SeededRandomNumberGenerator` test seam (the L1 tests assert "same seed + same pool = same slate"). The production call site (`displayedTrySlate`'s lazy init) passes `var rng = SystemRandomNumberGenerator()` — non-deterministic between launches but deterministic across re-accesses because the slate is cached on first access. **Considered and rejected:** **(a) Curated seasonal-terms extras list** (per the round-10 backlog "pool ≈ 20-30 terms" sketch) — rejected for v1 because dutchovendaddy.com's category taxonomy already has 30+ entries, so the top-30 from REST is already the target pool size without inventing a hand-maintained seasonal list. Adding the curated-extras path is a follow-up if the WP categories ever shrink below 30. **(b) Persistent "you've seen these" exclusion across launches** — rejected per the backlog's "over-engineered for v1; shuffle alone solves the staleness for most users." **(c) Time-of-day / weather-aware curation** — rejected per the backlog's "interesting but out of scope." **(d) Visible count = 5 (matching today)** — considered for byte-identical layout but rejected because the "pool" framing reads more clearly at 6 (one pin + five rotating) than at 5 (one pin + four rotating); the FlowLayout already handles 6 pills on every supported device width per the L4 snapshot baselines for IdleSuggestionsView. The rendered count change is documented as a deliberate "Layout shifts from 5 → 6 pills, then stays at 6 across every subsequent cold launch" — the constraint is "no shift between launches," which 6→6→6 satisfies. **(e) Re-shuffle on tab switch** — rejected because the stable-within-session rule explicitly forbids it; the slate is held on the viewmodel for exactly this reason. **What this does NOT change:** the pill text / accessibility label / surface-elevated capsule visual (AC-29.1 byte-identical), the tap handler routing (every pill — including the pinned Latest Recipes — fires the existing `surfaceLatestRecipes()` vs `selectCuratedSuggestion(_:)` branch in `SearchView`'s `onCategoryTap`), the `dod.search.tryPill.latestRecipes` accessibility identifier (T-638 / CL-107 — still resolves on the pinned pill because the per-pill `isLatestRecipesCategory(_:)` check is unchanged), the `topCategorySuggestions` public surface (kept for backward compat; no internal consumer remaining), the recent-searches store, the cook-time hydration path, the chip-row gating, every other view on the page. **Spec/trace:** US-29 amendment / AC-29.1 amendment (the "Try" pill source is now `displayedTrySlate`, not `topCategorySuggestions`; the visible count is 6 with Latest Recipes pinned first) + new **AC-29.7** (the rotation contract: shuffle on cold launch, stable within session, Latest Recipes always first, deterministic top-up if the pool shrinks below 5 non-pinned slots). Implementing PR: T-639 (`feat/T-639-rotating-try-pool`). Helper + viewmodel rotation seam + L1 tests for the pure helper + L1 test for the viewmodel's stable-within-session contract; no `e2e` label (single feature package, no nav / persistence / composition-root touch). The L4 `SearchViewSnapshotTests` baselines for the idle state will re-record because the visible count goes from 5 to 6 — the snapshot diff is the documented design change, not a regression.

- **CL-116 (US-41 / T-702b, 2026-05-30)** — **Drop the five `@Attribute(.unique)` constraints from the SchemaV4 models so the CloudKit private-DB mirror (`cloudKitDatabase: .private`) opens cleanly instead of crashing at container open.** CloudKit's mirroring invariants forbid unique constraints. The five that shipped through SchemaV4 — `CachedRecipe.id`, `CachedComment.id`, `CachedRating.recipeID`, `CachedListPage.key`, `CachedImage.urlString` — were **redundant**: `RecipeStore` already dedups every insert via explicit fetch-or-create (`let existing = try fetchRecipe(id:)` then update-or-insert), so the DB-level uniqueness was belt-and-suspenders, never load-bearing. Dropping them is a **non-destructive, in-place schema relaxation** (no existing row violates a *removed* constraint), mirroring how the additive `articleBodyHTML` column migrated without a dedicated stage. **Verified:** all 59 DODPersistence tests pass — the RecipeStore CRUD/LRU suites (which exercise the fetch-or-create dedup, so duplicates would surface as failures) and the V1→V2→V3 migration suites. The models are also relationship-free and all-attributes-optional/defaulted, so no other CloudKit invariant is violated. **What this does NOT change:** `RecipeStore` (the live `@Model` class names + public API are byte-identical — only the `.unique` attribute is gone, so every call site compiles unchanged), the opt-in gating (`makeProductionConfiguration()` still returns `.none` on the opt-out path per AC-41.1, so the app is identical for users not signed into iCloud / who declined sync), the analytics surface, the migration-plan registration. **Residual verification (inherent to CloudKit):** the actual cross-device sync, and the with-unique→no-unique transition on an existing 1.0(2) install, can only be confirmed on real devices signed into iCloud — there is no simulator path for two-device sync. **Spec/trace:** US-41 / AC-41.4 (the synced field scope is unchanged); supersedes the `SchemaV4.swift` "schema-identity marker only" framing from T-702 (the schema is now genuinely CloudKit-compatible, not merely configuration-gated). Implementing PR: T-702b (`feat/T-702b-cloudkit-schema-compat`). Five one-line constraint removals + a doc-comment update + this CL; no app-code change, no analytics event, no `e2e` label.

- **CL-122 (US-40 / T-721, 2026-05-30)** — **Ship the Settings → "Cook Mode voice" gender picker (the round-9 "male/female toggle in Settings" ask) and relocate the AVFoundation-free voice value-types + preference store from `DODFeatureRecipeDetail` to `DODSupport` so the picker can bind to them without a feature→feature dependency edge.** **What shipped.** A `Picker` (Female / Male / No preference) in `SettingsView`, bound to a new `SettingsViewModel.voiceGender` computed property whose get/set route through `VoicePreferenceStore` (canonical key `dod.voice.preferredGenderV1`, default `.female`, unknown→`.female` per AC-40.11) — the exact store the T-720 `SystemSpeechSynthesizer` reads at every utterance, so a change applies to the next step read with no Cook Mode restart. Accessibility id `settings-picker-voice-gender`; the picker follows the existing `AppearancePreference` / `ShareFormatPreference` row pattern. **The module move (the load-bearing decision).** `SettingsView` lives in `DODFeatureFeed`; the voice types lived in `DODFeatureRecipeDetail`, which `DODFeatureFeed` does **not** (and should not) depend on — a feature→feature edge is a layering smell. The whole `VoiceSelection.swift` unit (`VoiceGender`, `VoiceQuality`, `VoiceDescriptor`, `VoicePreference`, `VoiceSelector`, `VoicePreferenceStore`) is `import Foundation`-only by design (CL-109), so it moved wholesale to **`DODSupport`** — the shared utilities module **both** features already import, and which already hosts an analogous UserDefaults-backed store (`GuestIdentityStore`). `DODDomain` was rejected as the home because a side-effecting `UserDefaults` store doesn't belong in the pure value-type layer. `VoiceReader` / `SystemSpeechSynthesizer` now consume the types via `import DODSupport` (one added import, zero behavior change); the two voice test files moved to `DODSupportTests`. **Picker plumbing.** `VoiceGender` gained `CaseIterable` + `Hashable` (for `ForEach` / `.tag`); `displayName` (the user-facing "Female"/"Male"/"No preference" copy) is a `DODFeatureFeed`-local extension, NOT on the `DODSupport` type — UI strings stay in the feature layer, mirroring how `AppearancePreference.displayName` lives beside its view. **Deferred (NOT the round-9 ask, AC-40.12+ reserved):** the per-locale specific-voice picker (`AVSpeechSynthesisVoice.speechVoices()` filtered + quality tier shown), the "Download more voices" deep-link (`UIApplication.openSettingsURLString`), and the `voicePreferenceChanged` adoption-analytics event + constitution §9 allowlist amendment. **Verified:** `DODSupport` 235 tests pass (incl. the moved `VoiceSelectionTests` + `VoicePreferenceStoreTests`); `DODFeatureFeed` 25 `SettingsViewModel` tests pass (incl. 5 new `SettingsViewModelVoiceTests` cases — default-female, canonical-key round-trip, read-back across instances, unknown-value fallback, all-cases round-trip); `DODSupport` + `DODFeatureRecipeDetail` + `DODFeatureFeed` + the app target all build clean. Implementing PR: T-721 (`feat/T-721-voice-settings-picker`). The `SettingsView` snapshot baselines gain a row; they are re-recorded on-device as needed and are not CI-gated (L4 runs only `-scheme DODDesignSystem`).

- **CL-126 (US-3 amendment / US-29 amendment / T-648, 2026-05-30)** — **Extract a shared `DODSearchField` component (`DODDesignSystem`) so the Search-tab and Categories-tab search bars share both the brand brown surface (`DODColor.surfaceElevated`) and a `Capsule` (pill) shape; Categories migrates off `.searchable` to consume the same field.** **The user-visible gap this closes.** The Search tab rendered its search field as an inline `HStack(magnifyingglass, TextField, optional Clear)` with `.padding(DODSpacing.sm)` + `.background(RoundedRectangle(cornerRadius: DODSpacing.sm).fill(DODColor.surfaceElevated))` — brand brown surface, moderate corner radius (12pt). The Categories tab used SwiftUI's `.searchable(text:placement:.navigationBarDrawer(displayMode:.automatic))` — dark mode rendered the system `secondarySystemBackground` (near-black) inside a tall capsule. **Two surfaces, same job, two different visual languages** — one wrong color (Categories) and one not-quite-pill enough (Search). The user's stated end state: Search's COLOR (brand brown) + Categories' SHAPE (full capsule) on both bars. **The fix — one shared component.** A new `public struct DODSearchField: View` in `Packages/DODDesignSystem/Sources/DODDesignSystem/Components/DODSearchField.swift` — body is `HStack(spacing: DODSpacing.xs) { magnifyingglass, TextField, optional Clear button }`, padding `.padding(.vertical, DODSpacing.sm).padding(.horizontal, DODSpacing.md)` (gives the wider-than-tall pill proportion), background `Capsule(style: .continuous).fill(DODColor.surfaceElevated)`. **Public API.** `init(text: Binding<String>, placeholder: String, onClear: (() -> Void)? = nil)` — `onClear` defaults to `text.wrappedValue = ""` when no closure is provided, so the simplest call site (`DODSearchField(text: $searchText, placeholder: "Search")`) works without ceremony. **Call sites — Search tab** (`Packages/DODFeatureSearch/Sources/DODFeatureSearch/SearchView.swift`): the old inline `searchField` private var is deleted; the body's first child becomes `DODSearchField(text: $viewModel.query, placeholder: "Search recipes", onClear: { viewModel.clear() })`. **The `onClear` closure preserves the VM-side cleanup** the prior inline button called — `SearchViewModel.clear()` wipes `query` + `items` + `lastQuery` + `state` + `lastMergedRESTOrdering` + `lastMergedLocalOrdering` + `lastSurface` (and the debounce-cancel via the state machine), not just the query string. Skipping that closure would have collapsed the cleanup down to a string-clear and silently re-introduced stale `items` / `state` between searches. **Call sites — Categories tab** (`Packages/DODFeatureCategories/Sources/DODFeatureCategories/CategoryListView.swift`): the `.searchable(text:placement:.navigationBarDrawer(displayMode:.automatic))` modifier is **removed**; the loaded-list `body` becomes `VStack(spacing: 0) { DODSearchField(text: $searchText, placeholder: "Search").padding(.horizontal, DODSpacing.md).padding(.bottom, DODSpacing.sm); List { ... } }` so the field sits as a sticky element above the List. The List keeps its existing `.scrollContentBackground(.hidden)` + `.background(DODColor.surface)` + `.listStyle(.insetGrouped)` (iOS) modifiers byte-identical; the `.navigationTitle("Categories")` stays on the outer view. **The deliberate trade.** Removing `.searchable` means Categories' search bar no longer slides into a nav-bar drawer the way the iOS-stock `.searchable` modifier does — it now sits as a sticky element above the List. That is the explicit trade for visual unity with Search and a single shared component; the alternative (keep `.searchable` and try to restyle its system search bar) is impossible per Apple's API surface (the bar's color + shape are private and not configurable via standard SwiftUI modifiers). **Accessibility identifiers for L5 deterministic queries.** Each call site supplies a stable identifier via `.accessibilityIdentifier("dod.search.field.search")` (Search tab) and `.accessibilityIdentifier("dod.search.field.categories")` (Categories tab) at the *call site* rather than on the component itself, so the same component can serve multiple surfaces without identifier collisions. The pre-T-648 Search tab's `TextField("Search recipes", ...)` was queried via `app.textFields["Search recipes"]` (placeholder-as-label fallback per Apple's accessibility synthesis); that path **continues to work** because the placeholder is unchanged, so the existing `SmokeTests` + `CoreUserJourneysE2ETests` queries pass without modification. The new identifiers are additive belt-and-suspenders. **Considered and rejected.** **(a) Restyle the system `.searchable` bar via `UISearchBar.appearance()` proxy** — would tint every navigation-bar search field in the app (including any future surface) without a per-call-site opt-out, and the radius isn't configurable through the proxy. **(b) Leave Categories on `.searchable` and only re-skin Search** — fails the "both bars look identical" contract the user stated. **(c) Put `DODSearchField` in `DODSupport` instead of `DODDesignSystem`** — `DODSupport` is Foundation-only by design (CL-109 / CL-122); a `SwiftUI.View` belongs in the design system where other components like `EmptyState` + `Snackbar` + `RecipeCard` live. **(d) Pass the radius / background tint as parameters** — premature parameterization; the only two consumers want the exact same look, so defaults > knobs until a third consumer needs a different look. **What this does NOT change.** Search tab's `FilterChipRow` + `content` switch (US-12 / AC-12.2..AC-12.3) is byte-identical. Categories tab's `categoryRow(_:)` + `filtered(categories:matching:)` (US-19 / AC-19.3) is byte-identical — the search-text binding still drives the same client-side `localizedCaseInsensitiveContains` filter, only the UI surface for entering text changed. The empty-search row (`"No categories match '\(searchText)'"` per AC-19.6) still renders inside the List when the filter narrows to zero. The Search-tab `navigationTitle("Search")` + the toolbar layout-toggle (US-38 / T-650) are unchanged. The Categories-tab `navigationTitle("Categories")` is unchanged.
